# Design: ai-model-routing

Status: v1.135, 2026-11-08 (v1.135 specifies that `evaluate` (12.3) computes each task and seed's calibration sums, each model's held-out correct count and cost and the oracle's once, shared by every bar and flavor, with the same result bit for bit and a call-count test ahead of the code; v1.134, from review, a test that a second run recomputes its held-out sums and a checked count of keys per cell group; v1.133 implements the v1.132 held-out sums, once per path key, with the same result bit for bit and about half the CPU time of `research/no_path_clears.py` on the pinned files; v1.132 specifies that `evaluate_delegation` (13.9) computes each held-out path's correct count and cost once per task, seed, point, parent, path and model, shared by every bar and flavor, with the same result bit for bit and a call-count test ahead of the code; v1.131, from review, one committee-size check shared by the search and the drop rules; v1.130 implements the v1.129 judge candidate search, about 24 times faster on the pinned files with the same rows and report)

## 1. Problem
- A product or pipeline makes the same kind of model call many times: triage a support ticket, extract invoice fields, write a unit test, grade an answer against a rubric. Each such kind of call is a **task**: a repeated call with an **acceptance check** that says whether one output is right (agreement with a gold label, exact match on extracted fields, generated tests passing, a rubric pass)
- Sending every call of a task to the strongest model wastes money; sending it to the cheapest model silently lowers quality, and nothing in the logs says so
- The fix is to route per task: measure each candidate model's **success rate** against the task's acceptance check on a calibration set, and pick the cheapest model whose measured rate clears the task's bar with statistical confidence, with fallback when a call fails, a log of every attempt, and cost rolled up by any tag
- Judge and eval calls are the first-class use case, because a wrong judge corrupts every decision downstream of it and because a judge must not grade its own family's output (generator isolation, 5.2). Nothing in the routing rules is specific to judging: the library has no notion of a domain beyond a task name, its bar and its acceptance counts

## 2. Two flavors, one algorithm
- `cost_effective`: among models that clear the bar, pick the one with the lowest expected cost per call; ties go to higher quality
- `cost_blind`: identical pipeline, but the router treats token cost as 0, so among models that clear the bar it picks the highest quality. Fallback, monitoring and attribution are unchanged; the log still records list-price cost so spend stays visible
- The flavor changes exactly one thing: the `routing_cost` used to rank candidates

## 3. Package layout (Python 3.10+, standard library only)
```
model_routing/
  __init__.py     re-exports every public name below
  __main__.py     `python -m model_routing` runs `cli.main`
  registry.py     Model, Registry
  tasks.py        Task, load_tasks
  stats.py        wilson_lower_bound, Stats, calibrate_from_results
  priors.py       Priors
  router.py       Candidate, RouteDecision, Router, NoModelClearsBar, reason codes
  providers.py    Completion, ProviderError, Provider, FakeProvider, OpenAICompatibleProvider
  executor.py     Attempt, CallResult, Executor, AllModelsFailed
  monitor.py      Monitor, load_records, report
  evaluate.py     Outcome, outcomes_from_rows, load_outcomes, split_items, evaluate (v1.3, section 12)
  delegation.py   DelegationDecision, mode and reason constants, the logic of Router.decide_delegation (v1.4, section 13)
  delegation_eval.py  evaluate_delegation, offline evidence for the inline-or-sub-agent decision (v1.19, section 13.9)
  demo.py         run_demo
  cli.py          main
  py.typed        empty PEP 561 marker: type checkers read the package's own annotations (v1.114)
  data/
    models.json         public list prices and context windows (section 9)
    priors.json         leaderboard-derived priors (section 9)
    example_tasks.json  judge and non-judge example tasks used by the CLI defaults and docs
tests/            owned by the QA author; the implementer never edits it
```
- `json_validator` (section 7) lives in `executor.py`
- `model_routing.DATA_DIR` is the `pathlib.Path` of the packaged `data/` folder
- v1.114, packaging: `model_routing/py.typed` is an empty file, the PEP 561 marker that tells a type checker in a user's project to read the annotations of the package instead of treating it as untyped, and `pyproject.toml` ships it as package data beside the data files, `[tool.setuptools.package-data]` `model_routing = ["data/*.json", "py.typed"]`, so a built wheel carries it. The version has one source, `__version__` in `model_routing/__init__.py`: `pyproject.toml` writes no `version` under `[project]`, lists `"version"` in its `dynamic` list and reads it with `[tool.setuptools.dynamic]` `version = { attr = "model_routing.__version__" }`. `__version__` stays one top-level assignment of a string literal of the form `X.Y.Z`, and the only binding of that name in the file, so the build backend reads it from the source without importing the package. Chosen over a test that two written copies agree, because a value written once cannot drift. The version stays 0.1.0 here. Checked by `tests/test_packaging.py`: the marker exists and is empty, the package data is exactly those two entries, the version is dynamic and read from that attribute, the literal is the one binding, and where the distribution `ai-model-routing` is installed in the running interpreter (an editable install, as CI makes), its metadata version equals `model_routing.__version__`; with no such install that last test skips. The two tests that read `pyproject.toml` (the package data and the dynamic version) load a TOML parser inside the test with `pytest.importorskip`, `tomllib` from Python 3.11 and `tomli` before it (pytest itself depends on `tomli` there), so where that parser does not load only those two skip and the other four still run, the marker among them (v1.121). `tests/test_packaging_skips.py` runs `tests/test_packaging.py` in a child pytest of the same interpreter twice, as is and with that parser's name set to `None` in `sys.modules` before collection, and checks that both runs hold the same six tests, that the block turns exactly those two from passed to skipped with the parser's name in the skip reason, and that every other test keeps its outcome and reason
- v1.116, the installed wheel: the other `ci.yml` steps that run the package use an editable install, which reads `model_routing/data/*.json` and `py.typed` from the source tree, so a wheel that lost them would pass those steps. The last step of the `ci.yml` job `test`, named `Built wheel runs outside the checkout (one Python version)`, holds only the keys `name`, `if` and `run`. Its `if` is `matrix.python-version == '3.12'`, one version of the matrix, as the pandas step: the wheel is pure Python, so one version shows what it carries, and 3.12 stays in the matrix whichever way its oldest and newest versions move. Its `run` block is, in order: `python -m pip wheel . --no-deps -w "$RUNNER_TEMP/dist"`, so pip's build isolation installs the `[build-system]` requirement and builds the wheel a user's pip would build; `python -m venv "$RUNNER_TEMP/wheel-venv"`, with no system site packages, so the editable install of the earlier steps is not visible in it; that venv's pip installing `"$RUNNER_TEMP"/dist/*.whl`; `cd "$RUNNER_TEMP"`, out of the checkout; that venv's `model-routing demo --out "$RUNNER_TEMP/wheel-demo"`; and that venv's Python reading a standard-library check from a quoted heredoc. The check exits with one line naming the first failure unless `model_routing` imports from inside the venv's `sys.prefix` and not from `$GITHUB_WORKSPACE`, the installed metadata version of `ai-model-routing` equals `model_routing.__version__`, the checkout holds at least one `model_routing/data/*.json`, and `importlib.resources.files("model_routing")` holds each of those files and `py.typed` with the checkout's bytes; then it prints one `wheel ok:` line. The demo alone would not show the data, since it builds its fictional `demo/` registry in code: in a prototype of this step on 2026-11-08, a wheel built with `data/*.json` dropped from the package data still ran the demo and failed the check on `data/example_tasks.json`, while with only `py.typed` dropped from the package-data entry the wheel still held it, so the entry states the intent and the check covers the result. README: `## Quickstart` gains one paragraph right after its first `bash` block, saying how another project installs the package, `pip install "<name> @ git+<clone URL>@<commit>"` with `<name>` the `[project]` name in `pyproject.toml`, `<clone URL>` the URL of that block's `git clone` line and a full commit SHA for `<commit>`, and what the CI step checks; and the `## Layout` block names `py.typed` (the v1.13 sub-bullet below). Tests, in `tests/test_ci_wheel.py` (standard library only, reading `ci.yml` with the line parser of `tests/test_evidence_workflow.py`): the step's name (once), position (last), keys, `if` (with 3.12 in the matrix) and `run` lines exactly, and each of 12 broken workflows fails the check: the step removed, its `cd` dropped, a `cd` back to the checkout before the check, the demo run from the `PATH`'s `model-routing` (the editable install), an editable install in place of the wheel, a venv with the system site packages, the check without `py.typed`, an `if` naming a Python outside the matrix, 3.12 dropped from the matrix, a `continue-on-error`, a `working-directory`, and a step after it; and the README paragraph verbatim, once, right after the first `bash` block of `## Quickstart`, with its name and URL derived as above. The run itself is checked on GitHub, not by the suite
- v1.13: what the README claims about the package is checked against the repo by `tests/test_readme.py`, so a new module, data file or subcommand fails the suite until the README names it:
  - README.md says `about N lines` exactly once, and N is within 10% of the total line count of `model_routing/*.py`, counted as `wc -l` counts (newline characters)
  - README.md has a `## Layout` section with a fenced block right after the heading (blank lines before it and a language tag on the fence allowed) that holds a `model_routing/` line followed by one entry per line indented two spaces (name, then description). The `.py` entries are every `model_routing/*.py` file, `__init__.py` and `__main__.py` included, each named once, and no other file
  - v1.116: the entries that are neither a `.py` file nor `data/` are every other file at the top of `model_routing/` (directories and hidden files aside; today `py.typed`, from v1.114), each named once, and no other file, so a file the package ships cannot go unnamed
  - the block has exactly one `data/` entry; its description is a comma-separated list of file names, and those are every file in `model_routing/data/` (hidden files such as `.DS_Store` aside), each named once, and no other file
  - the `cli.py` entry reads `model-routing` followed by the subcommands separated by ` | `, and that list is exactly the subcommands `cli.py` defines, each once

## 4. Data model

### 4.1 Model and Registry (`registry.py`)
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Model`: `id: str`, `family: str`, `price_in: float` (USD per 1M input tokens), `price_out: float` (USD per 1M output tokens), `context_window: int`, `aliases: dict[str, str]` (default empty; provider name to that provider's model ID), and from v1.15 `price_valid_until: str | None` (default `None`)
- v1.15 `price_valid_until` is the last day, inclusive, on which the vendor states the list price, as an ISO date string `YYYY-MM-DD` (four, two and two ASCII digits joined by hyphens, naming a day that exists). Any other value, including a `datetime.date` object, another date spelling or an impossible day such as `2026-02-30`, raises `ValueError` at construction. It is kept as a string so `dataclasses.asdict(model)` stays JSON-able and round-trips through `Registry.load`
- v1.15 `Model.price_expired(today) -> bool` is `True` exactly when `price_valid_until` is set and `today` is a later day; on the stated day itself the price is still valid. `today` is a `datetime.date`, and a `datetime.datetime` counts as its own date; any other type raises `TypeError`
- `Model.cost(input_tokens: int, output_tokens: int) -> float` returns `(input_tokens * price_in + output_tokens * price_out) / 1_000_000`
- `class Registry`: `Registry(models: Iterable[Model])`; `Registry.load(path) -> Registry` reads the models.json format (section 9); `.models -> tuple[Model, ...]` in file order; `.get(model_id) -> Model` raises `KeyError` for an unknown ID; duplicate IDs raise `ValueError` at construction
- v1.15 `Registry.load` reads the optional `price_valid_until` key of a row (absent or `null` means `None`; an invalid value raises `ValueError` naming the file, as any invalid row does), and `Registry.expired_prices(today) -> tuple[Model, ...]` returns the models whose `price_expired(today)` is true, in file order

### 4.2 Task (`tasks.py`)
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Task` fields and defaults:
  - `name: str`
  - `bar: float` (required, in [0, 1]; the minimum acceptable success rate against the task's acceptance check, for a judge its agreement with gold labels)
  - `min_samples: int = 30` (fewer measured trials than this can never clear the bar)
  - `confidence: float = 0.95`
  - `independent_of_generator: bool = True` (exclude the generator's model AND its family when the caller names a `generator_model`; no effect otherwise)
  - `exclude_families: tuple[str, ...] = ()`
  - `expected_input_tokens: int = 1000`, `expected_output_tokens: int = 200`
  - `on_no_model: str = "refuse"` (or `"best_available"`)
  - `max_fallbacks: int = 2`
  - `prior_category: str | None = None`
  - `timeout_s: float = 60.0`
- Invalid values raise `ValueError` at construction: bar outside [0, 1], `min_samples < 1`, confidence outside (0, 1), unknown `on_no_model`, negative token counts or `max_fallbacks`
- `load_tasks(path) -> dict[str, Task]` reads `{"tasks": [ {field: value, ...}, ... ]}`; JSON lists become tuples; duplicate names raise `ValueError`

### 4.3 Quality statistics (`stats.py`)
- `wilson_lower_bound(successes: int, trials: int, confidence: float = 0.95) -> float`
  - z = `NormalDist().inv_cdf(1 - (1 - confidence) / 2)` (two-sided interval, lower end); p = successes / trials
  - LB = `(p + z*z/(2n) - z*sqrt(p*(1-p)/n + z*z/(4n*n))) / (1 + z*z/n)`, clamped to `>= 0.0`
  - returns `0.0` when `trials == 0`; raises `ValueError` when `successes < 0`, `trials < 0` or `successes > trials`
  - reference values at confidence 0.95 (to 6 decimals): (27, 30) 0.743789; (45, 50) 0.786398; (90, 100) 0.825634; (170, 200) 0.793944; (10, 10) 0.722467; (0, 10) 0.0
- `class Stats`: in-memory map from `(task, model_id)` to `(successes, trials)`
  - `Stats()`; `.get(task, model_id) -> tuple[int, int]` returns `(0, 0)` when absent; `.add(task, model_id, successes, trials)` accumulates (adds to existing counts) and validates like `wilson_lower_bound`
  - `Stats.load(path)` and `.save(path)` use `{"stats": [{"task": ..., "model": ..., "successes": int, "trials": int}, ...]}`; save writes rows sorted by (task, model)
  - `.merge(other: Stats) -> Stats` returns a new Stats with summed counts
- `calibrate_from_results(rows: Iterable[dict]) -> Stats`: each row has `task`, `model`, and either `correct` (bool) or both `prediction` and `gold` (correct when equal with `==`); a row with neither raises `ValueError`. Each row is one trial
- v1.1 validation: `correct` must be a real boolean (`type(correct) is bool`); a string such as `"false"`, a number or null raises `ValueError`. A row that is not a dict raises `ValueError`. `Stats.load` requires `successes` and `trials` to be integers that are not booleans (`isinstance(v, int) and not isinstance(v, bool)`); floats such as `30.9`, strings and booleans raise `ValueError`, never truncation
- v1.33 measured token counts: `Stats` also holds, per `(task, model_id)`, optional token totals: `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`, the summed input and output tokens of `token_trials` of its trials, the calls whose token counts were recorded. A key with none has all three at 0. Token totals only come with trials, so `0 <= token_trials <= trials` always holds, and a model with token totals is measured (`trials > 0`, 5.3)
- v1.33 `.add(task, model_id, successes, trials, *, input_tokens=None, output_tokens=None, token_trials=None)`: `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` are given together or not at all. Given, each is an integer `>= 0` that is not a bool, and they are totals over `token_trials` of this call's `trials`; `token_trials` defaults to `trials` and must be an integer that is not a bool with `1 <= token_trials <= trials`, so tokens cannot come with zero trials. One total without the other, `token_trials` without the totals, or any invalid value raises `ValueError`, and nothing is added when `add` raises
- v1.33 `.tokens(task, model_id) -> tuple[int, int, int]` returns `(input_tokens, output_tokens, token_trials)`, `(0, 0, 0)` when none are recorded. `.mean_tokens(task, model_id) -> tuple[int, int] | None` returns the mean input and output tokens per recorded call, each rounded up to an integer in integer arithmetic (`-(-total // token_trials)`, never through a float), or `None` when `token_trials` is 0. Rounding up means a model is never priced below the mean list cost of its recorded calls (`Model.cost` is linear in the token counts), though one call may cost more than its mean; integers keep `Model.cost` on whole token counts, as everywhere else
- v1.33 `.merge` sums the token totals and `token_trials` as it sums the counts. `.get` and `.items()` are unchanged and carry no tokens
- v1.33 files: a `Stats.load` row may carry `input_tokens`, `output_tokens` and `token_trials`, all three or none. A partial set, a value that is a bool, null or anything but an integer, a negative total, or a `token_trials` outside `1..trials` raises `ValueError` naming the file. Rows for the same key accumulate as before. `.save` writes the three keys, after `trials` and in that order, only for a key with `token_trials > 0`, so a Stats with no token totals saves the bytes v1.32 saves and a v1.32 file loads unchanged
- v1.33 `calibrate_from_results`: a row may carry `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`, both or neither, each an integer `>= 0` that is not a bool (one without the other, null, a float or a string raises `ValueError`, and every row is still validated before the returned Stats exists). A row with them adds one token trial with those counts; a row without them adds a trial with none. The `calibrate` command (10) needs no change: its printed table is unchanged, and the file it writes carries the token keys of the rows that had them

### 4.4 Priors (`priors.py`)
- Leaderboard numbers are not agreement rates, so priors NEVER clear a bar. They only order candidates when nothing has been measured (cold start) and tell you which models to calibrate first
- `Priors.load(path)` reads `{"categories": {name: {"metric": str, "source": str, "as_of": str, "scores": {model_id: number}}}}`; `Priors(categories: dict)` takes the same inner dict
- `.score(category, model_id) -> float | None`; unknown category or model returns `None`
- `.ranked(category) -> list[str]` model IDs by descending score, ties by ascending ID

## 5. Routing (`router.py`)

### 5.1 Types
- Reason codes (module constants with these exact string values): `EXCLUDED_MODEL = "excluded_model"`, `EXCLUDED_FAMILY = "excluded_family"`, `GENERATOR = "generator"`, `GENERATOR_FAMILY = "generator_family"`, `CONTEXT_TOO_SMALL = "context_too_small"`, `INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLES = "insufficient_samples"`, `BELOW_BAR = "below_bar"`, `PASSES = "passes"`
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Candidate`: `model_id`, `family`, `successes`, `trials`, `quality_lb: float`, `routing_cost: float`, `list_cost: float`, `eligible: bool`, `passes: bool`, `reason: str`
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class RouteDecision`: `task: str`, `flavor: str`, `primary: str`, `fallbacks: tuple[str, ...]`, `below_bar: bool`, `basis: str` (`"measured"` or `"prior"`), `candidates: tuple[Candidate, ...]` (one per registry model, registry order); `.to_dict()` returns plain JSON-able types (tuples become lists, candidates become dicts)
- `class NoModelClearsBar(Exception)` with attribute `.candidates` (the tuple above)
- Flavor names: `"cost_effective"`, `"cost_blind"`; anything else raises `ValueError`

### 5.2 `Router(registry, tasks: dict[str, Task], stats: Stats, priors: Priors | None = None)`
The constructor stores its arguments as attributes `.registry`, `.tasks`, `.stats`, `.priors`.
`.select(task_name, flavor="cost_effective", generator_model=None, exclude_models=()) -> RouteDecision`; unknown task raises `KeyError`. Steps, in order, per registry model (the first rule that applies sets `reason`):
1. ID in `exclude_models`: `excluded_model`
2. family in `task.exclude_families`: `excluded_family`
3. `independent_of_generator` and ID equals `generator_model`: `generator`
4. `independent_of_generator` and family equals the generator's family (generator looked up in the registry; an unknown generator ID raises `KeyError`): `generator_family`
5. `context_window < expected_input_tokens + expected_output_tokens`: `context_too_small`
6. measured `trials < task.min_samples`: `insufficient_samples` (still eligible for the cold-start path below)
7. `wilson_lower_bound(successes, trials, task.confidence) < task.bar`: `below_bar`
8. otherwise `passes`
- `eligible` is True for reasons 6 to 8; `passes` only for 8
- `list_cost = model.cost(expected_input_tokens, expected_output_tokens)` (from v1.33 at the model's sizes, below); `routing_cost = list_cost` for `cost_effective`, `0.0` for `cost_blind`
- `quality_lb = wilson_lower_bound(successes, trials, confidence)` for every candidate (0.0 when unmeasured)
- v1.33 sizes: a model's **sizes** for the task are `stats.mean_tokens(task, model_id)` when that is not `None`, else `(expected_input_tokens, expected_output_tokens)`, and `list_cost` is `model.cost` of its sizes. So a model measured with token counts is priced at what it read and wrote per call on this task, and any other model at the task's expected tokens. Rule 5 keeps the task's expected tokens for every model: a mean is not a bound on one call, so it cannot say whether a call fits (set the task's sizes to the longest calls you expect). Measured token counts therefore change a candidate's `list_cost` and `routing_cost` and the 5.3 orders that use them, never its `reason`, `eligible` or `passes`. `Candidate` gains no field. With no token totals in the stats, every candidate and decision is v1.32's, bit for bit

### 5.3 Choosing
- If any candidate passes: sort passing candidates by `(routing_cost, -quality_lb, model_id)`; `primary` is the first; `fallbacks` are the next `max_fallbacks`; `below_bar=False`; `basis="measured"`
- Else if `on_no_model == "refuse"`: raise `NoModelClearsBar`
- Else (`best_available`): among eligible candidates with `trials > 0`, sort by `(-quality_lb, routing_cost, model_id)`; if that list is non-empty use it (`basis="measured"`); otherwise, if the router has priors and the task has `prior_category`, use eligible candidates that have a prior score, sorted by `(-score, routing_cost, model_id)` (`basis="prior"`); `primary` is the first, `fallbacks` the next `max_fallbacks`, `below_bar=True`; if both lists are empty raise `NoModelClearsBar`
- Note: in `cost_blind` the passing sort reduces to `(-quality_lb, model_id)`, so the best measured model wins

## 6. Providers (`providers.py`)
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Completion`: `text: str`, `input_tokens: int`, `output_tokens: int`
- `class ProviderError(Exception)`: any failure a fallback should handle (timeout, rate limit, 5xx, bad response)
- `class Provider(Protocol)`: `complete(self, model_id: str, messages: list[dict], timeout_s: float) -> Completion`
- `FakeProvider(script: dict[str, Callable[[list[dict]], Completion]])`: calls `script[model_id](messages)`; an exception raised by the callable propagates unchanged; a model ID not in the script raises `ProviderError`. `.calls` is a list of `(model_id, messages)` in call order
- `OpenAICompatibleProvider(base_url: str, api_key_env: str = "OPENAI_API_KEY", model_map: dict[str, str] | None = None)`: POSTs `{"model": mapped_id, "messages": messages}` to the endpoint `base_url.rstrip("/") + "/chat/completions"`; v1.1: when `base_url` has a query string (for example `?api-version=...`), `/chat/completions` is appended to the path and the query is kept after it (`https://h/v1?q=1` posts to `https://h/v1/chat/completions?q=1`) with `Authorization: Bearer <key>` via `urllib.request`, reads `choices[0].message.content` and `usage.prompt_tokens` / `usage.completion_tokens`; any network, HTTP or parse failure raises `ProviderError`. v1.1 redaction: no `ProviderError` the adapter raises may contain the API key value in `str()`, `repr()` or its exception chain; the adapter replaces every occurrence of the key with `[redacted]` in the message and raises with `from None`; every URL anywhere in a message, including URLs inside text taken from the opener's exception, the HTTP reason and the HTTP body, is shown without userinfo, query string or fragment (`scheme://host[:port]/path`); in addition the password and every query parameter value of `base_url` that is at least 8 characters long, in both its raw form as written in `base_url` (percent escapes and `+` intact) and its decoded form, are replaced with `[redacted]` wherever they appear on their own (the password likewise in both forms) (shorter values such as `v=1` or `beta=true` cannot carry a credential and would only mangle the message; the API key is redacted whatever its length), so credentials embedded in `base_url` never reach a message or a log. If formatting the underlying exception or its HTTP reason itself raises, the adapter still raises `ProviderError` (naming only the exception class) and nothing from the failed formatting leaks. The constructor raises `ValueError` for a `base_url` that is not an `http` or `https` URL with a host, and that message never contains the URL (no `__cause__`, `__suppress_context__` True). For an `urllib.error.HTTPError` the message includes the status code and at most the first 200 characters of the response body, after redaction, and the error has attribute `.status` (int, else `None` on non-HTTP failures). `timeout_s` is passed to the opener and bounds each blocking socket operation (urllib semantics), not the total call. Works with any OpenAI-compatible endpoint (vendor APIs, gateways, local servers). Tests must not touch the network: it accepts an optional `opener` callable (default `urllib.request.urlopen`) for injection

## 7. Execution with fallback (`executor.py`)
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Attempt`: `model: str`, `outcome: str` (`"ok"`, `"error"` or `"invalid"`), `error: str | None` (exception class name and message for errors, `"validation failed"` for invalid), `latency_ms: float`, `input_tokens: int`, `output_tokens: int`, `cost_usd: float`
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class CallResult`: `call_id: str`, `text: str`, `model: str`, `attempts: tuple[Attempt, ...]`, `decision: RouteDecision`
- `class AllModelsFailed(Exception)` with `.attempts` and `.decision`
- `Executor(router, provider, monitor=None, clock=time.monotonic, now=None, id_factory=None)`; `now` returns an ISO 8601 UTC string (default: current UTC time); `id_factory` returns a call ID (default: `uuid.uuid4().hex`)
- `.call(task_name, messages, flavor="cost_effective", generator_model=None, exclude_models=(), tags=None, validate=None) -> CallResult`
  1. `decision = router.select(...)` (its exceptions propagate)
  2. for each model in `(primary, *fallbacks)`: time `provider.complete(model, messages, task.timeout_s)` with `clock`
     - `ProviderError` raised: attempt outcome `error`, tokens 0, cost 0.0; continue. Any other exception propagates (it is a bug, not an outage)
     - `validate` given and `validate(text)` is falsy: outcome `invalid`, tokens and cost from the completion; continue
     - otherwise: outcome `ok`; stop
  3. every attempt is passed to `monitor.record(...)` (when a monitor is given) as it happens, using the record schema in 8.1
  4. no `ok` attempt: raise `AllModelsFailed`
- `cost_usd` of an attempt is the list-price cost of its actual tokens: `registry.get(model).cost(input_tokens, output_tokens)`
- Helper `json_validator(*required_keys) -> Callable[[str], bool]`: True when the text parses as a JSON object containing every key
- v1.11: `.call_delegated(decision, messages, tags=None, validate=None) -> CallResult` runs the hand-off of a `DelegationDecision` (13.6) whose `mode` is `"delegate"`. `messages` is the brief the parent wrote. Steps 2 to 4 of `.call` run over `(decision.route.primary, *decision.route.fallbacks)` with the `timeout_s` of the task named `decision.task` in this executor's router (an unknown task raises `KeyError`), and `CallResult.decision` and `AllModelsFailed.decision` are `decision.route`. The route is used as decided: `select` is not called again, so a stats change after the decision does not move the call
  - Every record's `flavor`, `below_bar` and `basis` come from `decision.route`, and its `tags` are the caller's tags plus `"delegated": "true"` and `"parent": decision.parent`, so `report --by tag:delegated` and `--by tag:parent` split the spend. The caller's dict is not modified
  - `ValueError`, raised before any provider call and any record: a decision whose `mode` is not `"delegate"` (inline work happens in the parent's own call, so there is nothing to run), a `delegate` decision whose `route` is `None` (one built by hand; `decide_delegation` never returns one), and a caller tag named `delegated` or `parent`
  - `.call` is unchanged: it still looks its task up under the caller's `task_name` and names that key in `AllModelsFailed`, after `select` and before `id_factory` and the first provider call
  - The records cover the delegate's calls only. The parent's share of `delegate_cost_usd` (writing the brief, re-reading it and the result, 13.2) is spent in the parent's own calls, which this executor does not see

## 8. Monitoring and cost attribution (`monitor.py`)

### 8.1 Record schema (one JSON object per attempt)
`ts` (str), `call_id` (str), `task` (str), `flavor` (str), `model` (str), `family` (str), `attempt` (int, 0-based index within the call), `final` (bool, True on the call's last attempt), `outcome` (str), `error` (str or null), `latency_ms` (float), `input_tokens` (int), `output_tokens` (int), `cost_usd` (float, list price, recorded in both flavors), `routing_cost_usd` (float: equals `cost_usd` in `cost_effective`, `0.0` in `cost_blind`), `below_bar` (bool), `basis` (str), `tags` (object of string to string; `{}` when none; v1.11: `call_delegated` adds `delegated` and `parent`, 7)

### 8.2 `Monitor(path=None)`
- `.record(rec: dict)` appends to `.records` (a list) and, when `path` is set, appends one JSON line to that file
- `load_records(path) -> list[dict]` reads a JSONL file, skipping blank lines. v1.1: a non-blank line that is not valid JSON, or is valid JSON but not an object, raises `ValueError` whose message contains the path and the 1-based line number (for example `calls.jsonl:3`)

### 8.3 `report(records, by="model") -> list[dict]`
- `by` is `"model"`, `"task"`, `"flavor"`, `"family"`, or `"tag:<key>"` (records without that tag group under `"(none)"`); anything else raises `ValueError`
- Attempt-level sums per group use each attempt's own value: `attempts`, `ok`, `errors`, `invalid`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cost_usd`, `routing_cost_usd` (costs rounded to 6 decimals)
- Call-level metrics use the call's FINAL attempt to pick the group: `calls` (calls whose final attempt is in the group), `failed_calls` (final outcome not ok), `fallback_calls` (final attempt index > 0), `fallback_rate` (`fallback_calls / calls`, 0.0 when no calls)
- `p50_latency_ms`, `p95_latency_ms`: nearest-rank percentile over the group's `ok` attempts (sorted ascending, index `ceil(q * n) - 1`), `None` when there are none
- Each row is a dict with key `group` plus the keys above; rows sorted by descending `cost_usd`, then `group`

## 9. Data files
- `model_routing/data/models.json`: `{"version": str, "as_of": "YYYY-MM-DD", "currency": "USD", "models": [{"id", "family", "price_in", "price_out", "context_window", "source"}]}`; IDs are provider-neutral (`vendor/model-name`); map them to a provider's IDs with `aliases`; extra keys such as `notes` are allowed and ignored by `Registry.load`; `aliases` is optional; v1.15: `price_valid_until` is optional (format in 4.1) and is set on a row whose vendor page states an end date for the price, such as a promotional rate (the packaged `openai/gpt-5.6-sol` row: `2026-11-21`)
- `model_routing/data/priors.json`: format in 4.4; each category cites one public leaderboard with its date
- `model_routing/data/example_tasks.json`: three judge tasks (`faithfulness_judge`, `code_correctness_judge`, `pairwise_preference_judge`) whose generator-independence and bars are realistic, and, from v1.2, three non-judge tasks from different domains that check no other model's output and set `independent_of_generator` to `false` to document that intent: `support_ticket_triage` (classification), `invoice_field_extraction` (structured extraction) and `unit_test_codegen` (code generation, accepted when the generated tests pass). For `invoice_field_extraction` an item is accepted only when every field matches exactly, which is why its bar is 0.95. `support_ticket_triage` uses `professional_writing` as the closest packaged prior; priors only order cold-start candidates (4.4). Every packaged task routes in both flavors against the packaged registry once its candidates have passing stats
- v1.90 `tool_calling_support_agent`, a fourth non-judge task, from a fourth domain (a tool-calling agent), with `independent_of_generator` `false` since it checks no other model's output: one item is one whole conversation in which the agent serves a customer by calling tools, accepted when the program checks on the conversation's end state pass, as tau2-bench scores its runs (12.14). Its values, and the reason for each:
  - `bar` 0.7, the bar of `unit_test_codegen`, the other packaged task that program checks accept. The 2025 agents of the tau2-bench evidence reach 0.368 to 0.798 per domain at trial 0 (12.14), so 0.7 is above every airline and telecom rate and below two of the four retail ones (0.737 and 0.798)
  - `min_samples` 50: each sample is a whole conversation of 2 to 100 model calls (median 14 over the 834 per-call rows of 12.14 v1.76), so it costs more to label than a one-call sample, and 50 is the size of the smallest of the three pinned tau2-bench domains (airline, 50 tasks, 12.14). At 50 a model clears 0.7 only with 42 or more successes (Wilson lower bound 0.7149 at 42 and 0.6920 at 41, 4.3)
  - `expected_input_tokens` 89,000 and `expected_output_tokens` 1,500, to two significant figures the medians of the 1,112 trial-0 conversations of the four tau2-bench agents read with `--tokens` (12.14), each the sum over the conversation's agent calls: 88,833.5 input and 1,532 output tokens, against means of 118,822.7 and 2,671.0, which conversations of up to 2,096,093 input tokens pull up. The router prices this task per conversation, so a calibration row records a conversation's sums (README Calibrate step 2), and from then on each model is priced at its own mean (5.2 v1.33). Rule 5 of 5.2 reads these sizes as one call, so here it compares the conversation's summed reads, which count every re-read of the history, with each context window: a stricter test than any one call of the conversation needs. Every packaged window is at least 500,000 tokens, so no packaged model fails it
  - `timeout_s` 120.0, the longest of the packaged tasks, as for `unit_test_codegen`: `Executor.call` (7) passes it to each provider call and runs no tool loop, so for this task it limits one call of the agent's loop, which can read the whole conversation so far, not the conversation
  - `on_no_model` `refuse`: the agent's tool calls change records, such as a booking or an order, so when no model clears the bar the caller gets `NoModelClearsBar` and can hand the conversation to a person rather than run it on a model below the bar
  - `prior_category` none, as for `invoice_field_extraction`: no packaged category (4.4) scores conversational tool use, and none of the four tau2-bench agents is in `models.json` (12.14), so the evidence gives no prior either. 5.3 reads a prior only on the `best_available` path, so on this `refuse` task a prior would order nothing
  - `max_fallbacks` 2, as for every packaged task
  - README: Calibrate step 1 says the file ships three judge tasks and four from other domains and lists `tool_calling_support_agent` after the other three; step 2 gains, after the `unit_test_codegen` sentence, "For `tool_calling_support_agent` a line is one whole conversation: `correct` means the checks on its end state passed, and its token counts are the sums over the conversation's model calls."
  - Tests: `tests/test_registry_tasks_data.py` checks the task's values, the Wilson numbers above from `wilson_lower_bound`, the smallest packaged window, every candidate passing in both flavors once it has passing stats, and the README Calibrate lines that name it; when the 12 pinned tau2-bench files are in the default cache (12.14), one more test rebuilds from them the token medians, means and maximum above, requiring the rounded medians in the task, and the retail rates above the bar (CI never downloads the files, so there it skips)

## 10. CLI (`model-routing`, entry point `model_routing.cli:main(argv=None, *, today=None) -> int`)
- `select TASK [--flavor cost_effective|cost_blind] [--generator MODEL] [--exclude MODEL ...] [--registry PATH] [--tasks PATH] [--stats PATH] [--priors PATH] [--json]`
  - prints primary, fallbacks, basis, below_bar and a candidate table; `--json` prints `RouteDecision.to_dict()`
- `calibrate --results PATH --stats PATH`: reads results JSONL (section 4.3 rows), merges into the stats file (created if missing), prints per (task, model) counts and lower bounds
- `report --log PATH [--by KEY] [--json]`
- `demo [--out DIR]`: runs `run_demo` and prints its summary (v1.12: the inline-or-sub-agent lines are pinned in 11)
- `evaluate --outcomes PATH ...` (v1.3): offline evaluation; options, output and exit codes in 12.4
- `delegate TASK --parent MODEL ...` (v1.4): inline or sub-agent for one piece of work; options, output and exit codes in 13.7
- Defaults: `--registry` and `--priors` and `--tasks` point at the packaged files under `model_routing.DATA_DIR`; `--stats stats.json` in the current directory (a missing stats file means empty stats; a missing priors file means no priors)
- Exit codes: 0 success; 2 bad input (missing file, bad JSON, unknown task or model, invalid value); 3 `NoModelClearsBar` on `select` and `delegate`
- v1.1: every `--exclude` ID and the `--generator` ID must exist in the loaded registry, else exit 2; `calibrate` validates every result row before writing, so bad input leaves an existing stats file byte-identical and creates no new one
- v1.15 price expiry warnings: once `select` or `delegate` has printed its decision (exit 0) or the candidate table of `NoModelClearsBar` (exit 3), it prints on stderr, after all its other output, one line for each model in `Registry.expired_prices(today)` of the loaded registry, in file order: `warning: list price of MODEL was stated valid through YYYY-MM-DD; check the vendor page and update the registry`. Every registry model is a candidate of the decision (5.3), and the delegate is a `select` over the same registry (13.3), so each of these prices is one the decision used. stdout is unchanged, `--json` included, so every README transcript is too. A run that exits 2 prints no warning, so its `error:` line stays the last line (13.7)
- v1.15 `today` is a `datetime.date`; `None` means `datetime.date.today()` when `main` runs, and any other type raises `TypeError` before the command runs, so nothing is printed. `calibrate`, `report` and `demo` read no registry prices, so they never warn. `evaluate` does not warn either: matrix rows normally carry their own `cost_usd`, and flagging the token-form rows priced from an expired registry row (12.1) is left open

## 11. Demo (`demo.py`)
- `run_demo(out_dir: str | Path, seed: int = 7) -> dict` uses `FakeProvider` and clearly fictional models (`demo/` prefix) so no one mistakes simulated accuracy for a real measurement: four judges in two families with different prices and true accuracies, one generator in a third family
- Steps: build a 300-item synthetic labeled calibration set; calibrate every judge; select in both flavors; run 200 judge calls per flavor with a simulated outage on a fraction of the primary's calls; write `calls.jsonl` and `stats.json` to `out_dir`
- v1.5: `run_demo` also writes `outcomes.jsonl`, the same calibration set as an outcome matrix in the 12.1 format so `model-routing evaluate` has a ready input: one row per (judge, item), judges in the demo's order and items in order within each judge, item IDs `item-000` to `item-299`, `correct` when the judge's prediction equals the gold label, and `cost_usd` equal to `model.cost(1000, 200)` (the demo task's expected tokens). Each judge's correct count equals its successes in `stats.json`. Writing it draws no random numbers, so `calls.jsonl`, `stats.json` and the summary are unchanged
- v1.9: `run_demo` also writes its registry as `models.json` (the four judges in the demo's order, then the generator, each row with `id`, `family`, `price_in`, `price_out` and `context_window` in the 9 file format) and its task as `tasks.json` (one task with every 4.2 field), so `select` and `delegate` run on the demo's output with `--registry`, `--tasks` and `--stats` pointing into `out_dir`. `Registry.load` and `load_tasks` read them back equal to the demo's registry and task. Writing them draws no random numbers, so the other files and the summary are unchanged
- v1.12: after both flavors' 200 calls, `run_demo` asks `Router.decide_delegation` one question in each flavor, in summary order: a `demo/atlas-max` parent (module constant `DELEGATION_PARENT`) 60,000 tokens into a session (`DELEGATION_CONTEXT_TOKENS`) reaches one call of the demo task, with `brief_tokens=400`, `result_tokens=60`, `generator_model` the demo generator and the other options at their defaults. It is the question of the first README `delegate` transcript (13.8). A `delegate` decision is run once through `Executor.call_delegated` (7) with the demo's validator, tags `{"suite": "demo", "dataset": "synthetic"}` and call ID `<flavor>-delegated-0001`, and the simulated outage applies to its route's primary as it does to any primary. An inline decision runs nothing, because inline work happens in the parent's own call. With seed 7, `cost_effective` delegates to `demo/atlas-mid` (`delegate_cheaper`) and `cost_blind` stays inline (`inline_as_good`)
  - The hand-off's records follow the 400 judge-call records in `calls.jsonl` and its random draws come after theirs, so those records, `stats.json`, the other files and every pre-v1.12 summary value are unchanged: each flavor's `calls`, `fallback_calls` and `cost_usd` still count its 200 judge calls only. `report --by flavor` over `calls.jsonl` counts the hand-off under its flavor; `--by tag:delegated` splits it out
  - Each flavor's summary gains `delegation`, a dict with keys in this order: `parent`, `context_tokens`, `mode`, `model`, `reason`, `inline_cost_usd`, `delegate_cost_usd` (the decision's values, not rounded), `hand_off_calls` (1 for a delegate decision, 0 for inline) and `hand_off_cost_usd` (the list-price cost of the hand-off's attempts rounded to 6 decimals, `0.0` for inline)
  - The `demo` CLI prints every pre-v1.12 line unchanged up to and including the spend line, then a blank line, `inline or sub-agent, a <parent> parent at <context_tokens with comma thousands separators> context tokens:`, one line per flavor in summary order (two spaces, `<flavor>:` padded with spaces to 15 characters, one space, `delegate to <model>` or `inline on <model>`, then ` (<reason>), inline <inline_cost_usd> vs delegate <delegate_cost_usd> USD` with 6 decimals and `-` for `None`), then `  hand-off calls: <sum of hand_off_calls>, list-price USD <sum of hand_off_cost_usd, 4 decimals>`. The `log:` line stays last
- Returns a summary dict with keys `cost_effective` and `cost_blind`, each holding `primary`, `fallbacks`, `calls`, `fallback_calls`, `cost_usd` (from v1.12 also `delegation`, below); deterministic for a given seed
- v1.1 safety: `run_demo` writes an empty marker file `.model-routing-demo` into `out_dir`. If `out_dir` already contains `calls.jsonl` or `stats.json` (from v1.5 also `outcomes.jsonl`, from v1.9 also `models.json` or `tasks.json`) and has no marker, it raises `FileExistsError` and modifies nothing (CLI exit 2). With the marker present it replaces its own previous output

## 12. Offline evaluation (`evaluate.py`, v1.3)
Purpose: show from a public outcome matrix, with no API key, whether routing at a bar holds that bar on items it never saw, and what it costs next to simple baselines. The evaluator routes through `Router.select`, so the evidence exercises the shipped rules (min_samples, the Wilson admission rule, the 5.3 sort keys) and not a re-implementation of them.

### 12.1 Outcome matrix
- JSONL, one object per (task, item, model): `task` (str), `item` (str), `model` (str), `correct` (bool, strict as in 4.3), and the cost of that one call as either `cost_usd` (an int or float that is not a bool, finite and `>= 0`) or both `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` (integers `>= 0` that are not bools), priced with `registry.get(model).cost(input_tokens, output_tokens)`. When `cost_usd` is present it is used and token counts are ignored. Extra keys are ignored
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Outcome`: `task: str`, `item: str`, `model: str`, `correct: bool`, `cost_usd: float`
- `outcomes_from_rows(rows: Iterable[dict], registry: Registry | None = None) -> tuple[Outcome, ...]`, in input order. `ValueError` for: a row that is not a dict; `task`, `item` or `model` missing or not a non-empty string; `correct` missing or not a bool; neither `cost_usd` nor both token counts; an invalid cost or token value; a token-form row when `registry` is `None` or does not contain the model; a repeated (task, item, model)
- `load_outcomes(path, registry: Registry | None = None) -> tuple[Outcome, ...]` reads JSONL and skips blank lines; a non-blank line that is not valid JSON, or is not an object, raises `ValueError` whose message contains `<path>:<line>` (1-based) as in 8.2; rows are then validated as above

### 12.2 Split
- `split_items(items: Iterable[str], seed: int, calibration_fraction: float = 0.5) -> tuple[tuple[str, ...], tuple[str, ...]]` returns `(calibration, held_out)`, each sorted ascending with duplicates collapsed. An item is in calibration exactly when `int.from_bytes(hashlib.sha256(f"{seed}:{item}".encode("utf-8")).digest()[:8], "big") < calibration_fraction * 2**64`; otherwise it is held out. The split depends only on the seed and the item ID, so it is the same on every machine and for every row order
- `calibration_fraction` outside the open interval (0, 1) raises `ValueError`

### 12.3 `evaluate(outcomes, bars, seeds=range(10), flavors=("cost_effective", "cost_blind"), calibration_fraction=0.5, min_samples=30, confidence=0.95) -> dict`
- Checked before any work, each a `ValueError`: no outcomes; no bars, a bar that is a bool, not an int or float, not finite (so NaN is rejected) or outside [0, 1], or a repeated bar; no seeds, a seed that is a bool or not an int, or a repeated seed; no flavors, a name not in 5.1, or a repeated flavor; `min_samples < 1`; confidence outside (0, 1); `calibration_fraction` as in 12.2; an incomplete matrix (every model that appears anywhere must have a row for every (task, item) pair that appears anywhere; the message names one missing (task, item, model)); a (task, seed) whose calibration or held-out split is empty (the message names the task and the seed)
- The empty-split rule is deliberately strict: a task with very few items would otherwise be scored on a handful of items in some seeds and not at all in others. When it trips, drop or enlarge that task, use fewer seeds, or move `calibration_fraction` toward 0.5
- One **cell** per (bar, flavor, seed, task), nested in that order: bars, flavors and seeds in the order given, tasks ascending. In a cell, C and H are the task's calibration and held-out items from `split_items(task_items, seed, calibration_fraction)`
  - calibration counts per model: successes = correct rows in C, trials = |C|; mean calibration cost = total cost over C divided by |C|
  - `routed`: the primary of `Router.select(task, flavor)` for a task with this `bar`, `min_samples` and `confidence` and `on_no_model="best_available"`, over a registry in which each model's list cost per call equals its mean calibration cost (so `cost_effective` ranks by measured cost) and no model is excluded by rules 1 to 5 of 5.2. `cleared` is `not decision.below_bar`
  - `cheapest`: the model with the lowest mean calibration cost, ties by model ID
  - `best_on_calibration`: the model with the highest calibration success rate, ties by lower mean calibration cost, then model ID (no bar and no confidence bound)
  - `oracle`: on each held-out item it is correct when any model is correct, and its cost is the lowest cost among the correct models on that item, or the lowest cost among all models when none is correct
  - `model:<id>` for every model in the matrix: that model on every held-out item
  - for every strategy, `success` = correct held-out items / |H| and `cost_usd` = total cost over H
  - v1.135, computed once per split: the calibration counts and mean calibration costs, each model's held-out correct count and cost, and the `oracle`'s held-out `success` and `cost_usd` depend only on the task and the seed, through C and H, never on the bar or the flavor; `routed`, `best_on_calibration` and `cheapest` take the held-out pair of the model they name, and only `Router.select` reads the bar and the flavor. So a run computes them once per (task, seed), the first time a cell needs them, and every later cell of that (task, seed), at any bar or flavor, reads the same integers and floats: per (task, seed), with M models, one `sum` per model over C and one over H (2M) and one `math.fsum` per model over C and one over H plus one for the `oracle` (2M + 1), and none per cell, where the v1.134 code made 2M + 3 and 2M + 4 per cell. No value is kept from one call to the next, and each cell has its own `models`, `success` and `cost_usd` dicts. Every cell, summary row and per-task row is the v1.134 one bit for bit
- Strategy order everywhere: `routed`, `oracle`, `best_on_calibration`, `cheapest`, then `model:<id>` by ascending ID
- Cell keys: `bar`, `flavor`, `seed`, `task`, `calibration_items` (int), `held_out_items` (int), `cleared` (bool), `models` (`{"routed": id, "best_on_calibration": id, "cheapest": id}`), and `success` and `cost_usd`, each a dict keyed by strategy name in strategy order
- Return value keys:
  - `config`: `bars`, `seeds` and `flavors` (lists, in the order given), `calibration_fraction`, `min_samples`, `confidence`
  - `tasks`: `{task: {"items": int, "models": [ids ascending]}}`
  - `cells`: the cells above, in cell order
  - `summary`: one row per (bar, flavor, strategy), in that order, with keys `bar`, `flavor`, `strategy`, `success` (mean over seeds of that seed's item-weighted held-out success across all tasks: total correct held-out items / total held-out items), `cost_usd` (mean over seeds of that seed's total held-out cost across all tasks), `below_bar_rate` (fraction of that strategy's (task, seed) cells whose held-out success is below the bar), `cleared_rate` (for `routed`, the fraction of cells with `cleared`) and `bar_violation_rate` (for `routed`, among cleared cells the fraction whose held-out success is below the bar, `None` when no cell cleared); the last two are `None` for every other strategy
  - `per_task`: one row per (bar, flavor, task, strategy), in that order, with the summary keys plus `task` and `routed_models`; `success` and `cost_usd` are means over seeds of that task's cell values, the three rates count that task's cells only, and `routed_models` is `{id: number of seeds routed to it}` for `routed` and `None` otherwise
- Arithmetic: every success rate and every rate is an integer count divided by an integer count (for the summary `success`, total correct held-out items over total held-out items, both integers). Every cost total, and the sum behind every mean, is computed with `math.fsum`, which is exactly rounded and so independent of summation order; a mean is that sum divided by its count
- Granularity: in `summary`, `success` and `cost_usd` weight every held-out item equally within a seed, while `below_bar_rate`, `cleared_rate` and `bar_violation_rate` count (task, seed) cells, so a small task weighs as much as a large one in the rates but not in `success`. `success` above the bar does not imply a zero `below_bar_rate`
- Deterministic: the same outcomes in any row order with the same arguments give an equal dict, and `json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True)` is byte-identical
- Reading the numbers: `wilson_lower_bound` is the lower end of a two-sided interval (4.3), so for one model judged once its nominal chance of admitting a true rate below the bar is about `(1 - confidence) / 2` (2.5% at 0.95), and that figure is approximate because Wilson coverage is not exact. Three things push the observed `bar_violation_rate` above it: the routed model is the cheapest (or best) of several passing models, and that selection favours models whose calibration rate came out high by chance; the held-out rate is itself a noisy estimate, so a model whose true rate sits just above the bar misses it on a small held-out set often; and the seeds are re-splits of one item pool, so cells are correlated rather than independent trials. Read the rate as a resampling estimate for this matrix, next to `(1 - confidence) / 2` and the held-out size, never as a guarantee and never against zero

### 12.4 CLI
- `model-routing evaluate --outcomes PATH [--bar B [B ...]] [--seeds K] [--flavor NAME [NAME ...]] [--calibration-fraction F] [--min-samples N] [--confidence C] [--registry PATH] [--json]`
  - `--bar` takes one or more values and may be repeated (default `0.8`); `--seeds K` means seeds `0` to `K - 1` (default 10, `K >= 1`); `--registry` defaults to the packaged models.json and is only used to price token-form rows
  - `--flavor` (v1.63) takes one or more flavor names of 5.1 and may be repeated, as `--bar`; the flavors run in the order given and are the result's `config.flavors`, and without the option both run, `cost_effective` then `cost_blind`, as every run did before v1.63. An unknown or repeated name is an invalid option value (exit 2): `evaluate` checks it (12.3), so it is reported after the outcome file is read. The option is for a matrix that records no cost, such as 12.16's, where a `cost_effective` run would rank every model as equally cheap; with one flavor the text output has one table per bar
  - prints one table per (bar, flavor) with a row per strategy and the columns success, cost_usd, below_bar_rate, cleared_rate and bar_violation_rate; `--json` prints the result without `cells` (keys `config`, `tasks`, `summary`, `per_task`) so a committed results file stays small
  - v1.5 text layout: for each (bar, flavor) in config order, a heading line `bar <bar>, <flavor>` (the bar as Python `str` prints it), then a table in the layout of the other commands (header, a dash row, one row per strategy in strategy order) with columns `strategy`, `success`, `cost_usd`, `below_bar_rate`, `cleared_rate`, `bar_violation_rate`, built from the `summary` rows; rates and success to 3 decimals, `cost_usd` to 4, `-` for `None`. One blank line between tables and none after the last
  - exit codes: 0 success; 2 bad input (missing or unreadable file, bad JSON, invalid row, incomplete matrix, empty split, invalid option value), with a one-line `error: ...` message on stderr as for the other commands

### 12.5 Exports and test status
- `Outcome`, `outcomes_from_rows`, `load_outcomes`, `split_items` and `evaluate` are re-exported from `model_routing` and listed in `__all__`
- The v1.3 contract tests (`tests/test_evaluate.py`) were committed before `evaluate.py` exists, under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=ModuleNotFoundError)`, so the suite stays green while they fail for the one expected reason. The implementing commit removes that marker; from then on any failure in them fails the suite
- v1.5: the README shows an `evaluate` transcript on the demo's `outcomes.jsonl` (11). `tests/test_readme.py` runs every fenced `text` block in the README whose first line starts with `$ model-routing`, in README order in one empty folder (so later blocks read what earlier ones wrote), and requires exit code 0 and stdout equal to the rest of the block line for line, so the demo, report and evaluate transcripts cannot drift

### 12.6 Public evidence: the RouterBench converter (`evidence/routerbench/convert.py`, v1.6)
Purpose: turn a public dataset that already holds every model's output, correctness and cost per prompt into the 12.1 matrix, so the evaluator runs on third-party data with no API key. It lives outside the package: `model_routing` never imports it, it is standard library except that reading the frame needs pandas (the `evidence` extra), and the tests that need pandas use `pytest.importorskip`, so CI passes without it
- Source, pinned: dataset `withmartian/routerbench` on Hugging Face at revision `784021482c3f320c6619ed4b3bb3b41a21424fcb`, file `routerbench_0shot.pkl`, `SIZE = 99567659` bytes, `SHA256 = "ba4f77f19517610a707c374e99322d7750c30fc4ae7ff5527888595a1e65d36d"` (its LFS object ID). The RouterBench code is MIT licensed (https://github.com/withmartian/routerbench); the dataset card states no data licence, and the evidence README says so
- `default_cache_dir()` is `~/.cache/ai-model-routing-evidence/routerbench/` (from `Path.home()`), never inside the repo. `fetch(cache_dir=None) -> Path`: the file is `cache_dir / "routerbench_0shot.pkl"`. A present file is verified and used, and one that fails `verify` is a `ValueError` naming it and is left in place (delete it to download again); a missing file is downloaded with `urllib.request.urlopen` from `https://huggingface.co/datasets/withmartian/routerbench/resolve/<revision>/routerbench_0shot.pkl` into a `.part` file next to it, verified, then renamed, and the `.part` file is removed when the check fails
- `verify(path, size=SIZE, sha256=SHA256)`: `ValueError` naming the expected and the actual value when the byte size or the sha256 differs. Nothing is unpickled before it passes
- `RestrictedUnpickler(pickle.Unpickler)`, `restricted_load(fileobj)` and `load_frame(path)`; `load_frame` opens the file, runs `restricted_load` and turns any other exception raised while unpickling (an empty or truncated file, an allowed callable given bad arguments) into a `ValueError` naming the file, so only `pickle.UnpicklingError` and `ValueError` leave it. In `restricted_load`, `find_class` resolves only the (module, name) pairs in `ALLOWED_GLOBALS`, exactly the 11 globals the pinned file references (found with a static `pickletools` opcode scan, which runs no code): `builtins.slice`, `numpy.dtype`, `numpy.ndarray`, `numpy.core.multiarray._reconstruct`, `numpy.core.numeric._frombuffer`, `pandas._libs.internals._unpickle_block`, `pandas.core.frame.DataFrame`, `pandas.core.indexes.base.Index`, `pandas.core.indexes.base._new_Index`, `pandas.core.indexes.range.RangeIndex`, `pandas.core.internals.managers.BlockManager`. Any other global raises `pickle.UnpicklingError` naming it, with no module-prefix wildcard; names are checked as written, with no Python 2 name mapping (`fix_imports=False`), and persistent IDs are refused. Because the file is hash-pinned, the list only has to cover that one file; a pickle written by another pandas or numpy version is refused, not trusted
- `model_id(column) -> str`: a column name of the form `vendor/model` (one `/`, both sides non-empty) is kept as is, and any other name containing `/` is a `ValueError`; a name starting `claude-` becomes `anthropic/<name>` and one starting `gpt-` becomes `openai/<name>`; anything else is a `ValueError` rather than a guess
- `frame_columns(frame) -> dict[str, list]`: `ValueError` naming the type when `frame` has no `columns` attribute (a pickle that loads to something other than a frame); otherwise plain Python lists, from `.tolist()`, for `sample_id`, `eval_name` and, for every model column `<m>`, both `<m>` (the score) and `<m>|total_cost`. A model column is any column without `|` other than `sample_id`, `prompt`, `eval_name` and `oracle_model_to_route_to`. Prompts and responses are never read. `ValueError` when `sample_id` or `eval_name` is missing, there is no model column, or a model has no `|total_cost` column
- `convert(columns, pass_score=None, min_items=100) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]`, where `columns` is what `frame_columns` returns (any `dict[str, list]` of that shape):
  - `ValueError`, checked before any output: columns of different lengths; a `sample_id` that is not a non-empty string or is repeated; an `eval_name` that is not a non-empty string; a score that is a bool, not an int or float, NaN or outside [0, 1]; a cost that is a bool, not an int or float, not finite or below 0 (score and cost messages name the `sample_id` and the column); `pass_score` that is not `None` and is a bool, not an int or float, or outside (0, 1] (so NaN is rejected); `min_items` that is a bool, not an int, or below 1; a model column that `model_id` rejects, or two columns mapping to one ID
  - each source row is one item: task = `eval_name`, item = `sample_id`, and one output row per model with model = `model_id(column)`, `cost_usd` = the recorded `total_cost` as a float, with a negative zero written as `0.0`
  - scores and costs must be Python `int` or `float` values (a `float` subclass such as numpy's float64 counts); other numeric types such as numpy's int64 or float32 are rejected, which `.tolist()` never produces but a hand-built `columns` dict could
  - graded tasks: a task is graded when any of its scores, for any model, is not exactly 0 or 1. With `pass_score=None` a graded task is dropped; otherwise it is kept. In a kept task `correct` is `score >= pass_score` when `pass_score` is set and `score == 1` when it is not, which agree on 0 or 1 scores for every allowed `pass_score`. The pinned file's graded tasks (`grade-school-math` with scores in {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1}, `consensus_summary`, the `mtbench` tasks, `chinese_idioms`) do not document what a partial score means, so excluding them is the default and including them is the user's stated choice
  - small tasks: a task left with fewer than `min_items` items is dropped. The default 100 gives calibration and held-out halves of about 50 items, so in practice 12.3's empty-split rule does not trip and `min_samples = 30` can be met
  - output rows have the keys `task`, `item`, `model`, `correct`, `cost_usd` in that order, sorted by (task, item, model) as strings; they form the complete matrix 12.3 requires, and the result does not depend on the source row order
  - the report has keys `models` (IDs ascending), `kept`, `dropped_graded` and `dropped_small` (each `{task: number of items}` with tasks ascending) and `rows` (the number of output rows)
- `write_jsonl(rows, path)`: one `json.dumps(row)` per line, newline-terminated, in the given order
- CLI: `python evidence/routerbench/convert.py --out PATH [--cache DIR] [--pass-score T] [--min-items N]`, entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`. Options are checked first (no download for a bad one); then it fetches, verifies, loads with `load_frame`, converts, writes `<out>.part`, prints the report as `json.dumps(report, indent=2)`, flushing stdout, and only then renames `<out>.part` onto `--out` (v1.94, as 13.11), so a write to stdout that fails (a closed pipe) is an I/O error that leaves `--out` as it was. It returns 0 on success and 2 with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr and nothing on stdout for a missing or invalid option, a size or hash mismatch, a refused global, a file that does not unpickle to a frame, invalid data or an I/O error, but for a rename that fails after the print, the one failure with the report already on stdout. The matrix is written to `<out>.part` and renamed onto `--out`; on any failure `--out` is not written and `<out>.part` is removed
- Reading it: the 11 models date from 2023 and the costs are the ones the dataset recorded at that time's prices; the evaluator routes per task (an `eval_name`), not per prompt

### 12.7 RouterBench results (`evidence/routerbench/results.py`, v1.7)
Purpose: commit the evaluator's numbers on RouterBench in a file small enough to review, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the tables in `RESULTS.md` checked against it by the test suite. Standard library only: it reads JSON and never imports pandas
- `reduce(result: dict) -> dict`: from an `evaluate` result (12.3, with or without `cells`, so the `evaluate --json` output too), a new dict with the keys `config`, `tasks`, `summary` and `per_task` in that order, where `per_task` keeps only the rows whose `strategy` is `routed`, in their order; `cells` and the other per-task strategies are left out so the file stays under about 300 KB. Values are copied unchanged and the input is not modified, so `reduce(reduce(r)) == reduce(r)`. `ValueError` naming the key when one of the four is missing
- `table(results: dict) -> str`: two tables in the layout of 12.4 (header, a dash row, one row per line, trailing spaces stripped), separated by one blank line, with no newline after the last:
  - routed: one row per (bar, flavor) in config order, from that block's `routed` summary row, with columns `bar` (as Python `str` prints it), `flavor`, `success`, `cost_usd`, `vs_best_model`, `cleared_rate` and `bar_violation_rate`; `vs_best_model` is the routed `cost_usd` divided by the best model's `cost_usd`
  - baselines: from the summary rows of the first (bar, flavor) in config order (held-out success and cost do not depend on the bar or the flavor), one row per strategy other than `routed`, in summary order, with columns `strategy`, `success` and `cost_usd`
  - the best model is the `model:<id>` row of that first block with the highest `success`, ties by lower `cost_usd`, then by strategy name
  - success, rates and `vs_best_model` to 3 decimals, `cost_usd` to 4; `-` for `None`, and for `vs_best_model` when the best model's cost is 0
  - `ValueError` when `config` or `summary` is missing, a (bar, flavor) in the config has no `routed` summary row, or the first block has no `model:` row
- CLI: `python evidence/routerbench/results.py --evaluate PATH --out PATH` writes `json.dumps(reduce(result), indent=2)` and a newline to `--out`, where `result` is the JSON object read from `--evaluate`; `python evidence/routerbench/results.py --table PATH` prints `table(results)` for the JSON object read from `PATH`. Exactly one of `--evaluate` and `--table` is given, and `--out` comes with `--evaluate` and never with `--table`. The file is written to `<out>.part` and renamed onto `--out` only after the write completes; on any failure an existing `--out` is left as it was, a new one is not created, and `<out>.part` is removed. Entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`: 0 on success, 2 with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr and nothing on stdout for a usage error, a missing or unreadable file, invalid JSON, a JSON value that is not an object, a write or rename error, or a `ValueError` above; a carriage return or line feed inside the message (a path can contain one) is written as `\r` or `\n`, so the message stays on one line
- Committed: `evidence/routerbench/results.json`, from the default converter matrix (12.6) and `model-routing evaluate --bar 0.6 0.7 0.8 0.9 --seeds 50 --json` reduced as above, and `evidence/routerbench/RESULTS.md` with the regenerate commands, the caveats of 12.6 "Reading it", and the `table` output in a fenced `text` block whose first line is `$ python evidence/routerbench/results.py --table evidence/routerbench/results.json`. `tests/test_evidence_results.py` checks that the committed file is under 300,000 bytes, is exactly `json.dumps(reduce(itself), indent=2)` plus a newline, has that config, the converter's 11 model IDs on every task and its 65 tasks with their item counts (pinned by the sha256 of `json.dumps({task: items}, sort_keys=True)`, which matched the converter's `kept` report when the file was made), 28,281 items in all, unique summary and per-task keys and 50 seeds behind every `routed_models`, and that `RESULTS.md` contains that block with the current `table` output, so the published tables cannot drift from the published file
- CI: one Python version also installs the `evidence` extra and runs the converter tests, so the tests that need pandas run in CI and not only locally
- RESULTS.md prose (v1.18): `tests/test_evidence_results.py` also builds from the committed file, and requires verbatim in `RESULTS.md`, the intro counts (models, evals, prompts, seeds) and every number of the "Reading it" bullets: for `cost_effective` the first bar's cleared rate as a percentage to 1 decimal, its cost ratio to the best single model, its success and that model's, the last bar's cleared rate as a percentage and its cost ratio (the ratio must rise with the bar), and the lowest and highest bar-violation rate over the bars next to the nominal one-sided `(1 - confidence) / 2` and the confidence; for `cost_blind` its success and cost ratio, which must be the same at every bar, next to those of `best_on_calibration` and how much more it costs as a percentage to 1 decimal, with the words "the same success to three decimals" allowed only while both successes print the same to 3 decimals and `cost_blind` costs more; how many evals hold the fewest prompts and that count; the oracle's success and cost. No bullet may call `cost_blind` level with `best_on_calibration`
- Checks on the pinned file (v1.18): when the pinned pickle is found (the `ROUTERBENCH_PKL` path when that variable is set, else the default cache) and pandas is installed, the tests also rebuild, from the converter's output, the held-out half sizes of the evals with the fewest prompts over the seeds (the 12.2 split on their prompt IDs, as a lowest-to-highest range) and the graded-evals sensitivity counts (the graded evals dropped by default, the two that `--pass-score 0.75` brings back with their prompt counts, the rest still under 100 prompts, and that matrix's evals, prompts and rows), and require them verbatim in `RESULTS.md`; without `ROUTERBENCH_PKL` they skip when the file or pandas is missing, so CI never downloads the file, and with it set a missing file or a missing pandas is a failure, not a skip. The sensitivity run's routed numbers need an evaluate of about 20 seconds, so they are rebuilt only when `ROUTERBENCH_PKL` is set. The evaluate time and output size in the Regenerate paragraph are not checked

### 12.8 README evidence section (v1.10; AutoBench v1.17; at a glance v1.23; judges v1.28; workflow v1.30; variant points v1.48; tau2-bench v1.55; LiveCodeBench v1.65; delegation on tau2-bench v1.80; its pooled run v1.84; its cached point v1.92)
Purpose: the README itself shows what the router did on public data, with links a reader can follow to the source, and no number that can drift from the committed results
- README.md has a section `## Evidence: held-out results on public data`, placed before the price and leaderboard section, with one `###` subsection per dataset that has committed results; v1.17 has two, `### RouterBench` and then `### AutoBench`, and no other `###` heading; v1.22 adds a third after them, `### Delegation on AutoBench` (13.10); v1.28 adds a fourth, `### AutoBench judges` (12.12), between `### AutoBench` and `### Delegation on AutoBench`, so the headings are `RouterBench`, `AutoBench`, `AutoBench judges` and `Delegation on AutoBench` in that order; v1.55 adds a fifth, `### tau2-bench` (12.15), between `### AutoBench judges` and `### Delegation on AutoBench`, so from v1.55 the headings are `RouterBench`, `AutoBench`, `AutoBench judges`, `tau2-bench` and `Delegation on AutoBench` in that order. The section's opening paragraph holds for every subsection: it describes each dataset's label (right or wrong on RouterBench, a Delta-Hit from judge scores on AutoBench, and, v1.28, agreement with the other judges' grades for a judge model's grade in AutoBench judges, and, v1.55, after them, whether an agent's conversation passed the run's program checks on tau2-bench) and says that each cost is either recorded in the dataset or estimated from its token counts at list prices (v1.28), the recorded cost being that of a whole conversation on tau2-bench (v1.55). v1.30: the opening paragraph ends with the 12.13 workflow sentence, which links `.github/workflows/evidence.yml`, names the results files of its `Compare` line (the three `results.json` files; v1.48 adds `evidence/delegation/variants.json` after them, and v1.55 adds `evidence/tau2-bench/results.json` after the judges file and the tau2-bench subsection to the subsections it names, 12.13) and says why RouterBench is left out. v1.65 adds a sixth subsection, `### LiveCodeBench` (12.17), between `### tau2-bench` and `### Delegation on AutoBench`, so from v1.65 the headings are `RouterBench`, `AutoBench`, `AutoBench judges`, `tau2-bench`, `LiveCodeBench` and `Delegation on AutoBench` in that order; the opening paragraph's label list ends, after the tau2-bench label, with whether a model's first sample passed the problem's test cases on LiveCodeBench, its cost clause says in brackets that LiveCodeBench records neither a cost nor token counts, so it is `cost_blind` evidence only, and its workflow sentence names `evidence/livecodebench/results.json` after the tau2-bench file and the LiveCodeBench subsection after tau2-bench (12.13). v1.80 adds a seventh subsection, `### Delegation on tau2-bench` (13.12), after `### Delegation on AutoBench`, so from v1.80 the headings are `RouterBench`, `AutoBench`, `AutoBench judges`, `tau2-bench`, `LiveCodeBench`, `Delegation on AutoBench` and `Delegation on tau2-bench` in that order; the opening paragraph keeps its words, since its labels and costs already name tau2-bench and its workflow sentence's "delegation subsections" covers both, and the sentence's list of files gains `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.json` last (12.13); v1.84 adds `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/pooled.json` after it, the 13.12 pooled run, and keeps the other words
- Each subsection links the dataset page and its pinned revision, states the licence as the dataset states it, links that dataset's `RESULTS.md`, `results.json` and evidence README, gives the regenerate commands, and states the limits (model age, recorded prices, routing per task rather than per prompt, the task mix, what the converter drops); the delegation subsection (13.10) links the same AutoBench dataset page, pinned revision and licence, and links its own `RESULTS.md` and `results.json` (and, v1.48, `variants.json`) in place of an evidence README
- Results at a glance (v1.23): the opening paragraph is followed, before the first `###`, by the line `Results at a glance, each number from the committed results of the subsection it links:`, a blank line and one bullet per subsection in heading order, then (v1.124) a blank line and the one paragraph of the where-routing-pays bullet below, and by nothing else. Each bullet starts with a link to its subsection, the heading as display text and GitHub's anchor for it as target (lower case, characters other than letters, digits, spaces, `-` and `_` dropped, spaces to `-`), then a colon, and holds at most two result values (a held-out success, a cost or a cost ratio, at the rounding of that subsection's tables: success and ratio to 3 decimals, cost to 4) taken from that subsection's committed results.json, plus the bar, the parent count and the context sizes that qualify them, and (v1.40) whether inline work takes a parent call of its own. Each keeps its unfavourable result: RouterBench, the `cost_effective` routed row at the lowest bar (held-out success and the 12.7 best-single-model cost ratio) and the best single model, named, whose held-out success no routed row at any bar reaches; AutoBench judges (v1.28), after a lead that names the label as agreement with the other judges and not a human label, the same two values and claim with the best single judge of 12.12 in place of the best single model; AutoBench, that no routed row beats always calling `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` on held-out success and cost together, in the 12.10 sense, with its held-out success and cost; tau2-bench (v1.55), on trial 0, that no routed row beats any of the single agents on held-out success and cost together, in the 12.15 sense, and that at the lowest bar `cost_effective` routing costs more than always calling the cheapest single agent (named, chosen as in 12.15) for no more held-out success, with those two costs as its two values; LiveCodeBench (v1.65), that the dataset records no cost, so only `cost_blind` routing is run, and that it reaches one held-out success at every bar, below that of always calling the best single model (named, chosen as in 12.7), with those two successes as its two values (a file with a second flavor, a routed success that differs between bars, or one that reaches the best model's fails the test); delegation, at bar 0.8, with the brief assumed to carry the whole question and (v1.40) inline work in a parent call of its own (the 13.10 points with `inline_separate_call` True; the folded point is left to the subsection), that the `cost_effective` decision delegates on every seed for every parent at every context size of those points, so its summary rows have the success and cost of always delegating exactly (v1.32; before v1.32 it was level with always delegating to two decimals and cost more), its mean held-out success over the parents against always inline (each the same at every context size, means as in 13.10), and the context sizes where it costs more and where it costs less than always inline, both sets non-empty and the larger-cost sizes first in context order (a file where the decision costs more, or less, at every size fails the test, so that sentence is reworded instead of printed one-sided);
