delegation on tau2-bench (v1.80), on trial 0, that the `cost_effective` decision delegates on every seed for each of the parents at every bar and point (every `delegate_rate` of its decision rows is 1.0), and that at the lowest bar and point 0 (8,000 context tokens, inline work in a parent call of its own) its mean cost over the parents is the first of two costs against always inline's, for less held-out success, with those two costs as its two values (a file where a decision row delegates on fewer seeds, where that mean cost is not below always inline's, or where the decision's mean held-out success is not below always inline's at 3 decimals fails the test). `tests/test_evidence_at_a_glance.py` builds the whole list from the committed files (four since v1.28, five since v1.55, six since v1.65, seven since v1.80) and requires it verbatim there; each data-dependent word (no routed row reaches or beats, every seed, more, less, no more) is asserted against the files, so a results file that breaks one fails the test instead of leaving a stale sentence
- RouterBench tables, from the committed `results.json` summary, in this order with one blank line between them:
  - routed: one row per (bar, flavor) in config order, columns `Bar` (as Python `str` prints it), `Flavor` (in backticks), `Held-out success`, `Cost (USD)`, `Cost / best single model`, `Cost / cheapest model`, `Cleared rate`, `Bar-violation rate`; the best single model is chosen as in 12.7, and the cheapest model is the `cheapest` summary row of the first (bar, flavor); success, rates and the best-model ratio to 3 decimals, cost to 4, the cheapest-model ratio to 1
  - baselines: the oracle, the best single model (named), `best_on_calibration` and `cheapest` rows of the first (bar, flavor), columns `Baseline`, `Held-out success`, `Cost (USD)`, success to 3 decimals and cost to 4
- `tests/test_evidence_results.py` rebuilds both tables, and the counts sentence (models, evals, how many of them start with `mmlu`, prompts, seeds), from the committed file and requires them verbatim in that subsection (from its `###` heading to the next `###` or `##` heading); it also requires the dataset URL, the revision link (display text the first 7 characters of the converter's `REVISION`, target the tree URL at the full `REVISION`), the code licence link, the three evidence links, the bar range, the kept-of-86 eval count, and the five-line regenerate block taken from `RESULTS.md`
- RouterBench "Reading it" (v1.18): no bullet calls `cost_blind` level with `best_on_calibration`. The test builds from the committed file and requires verbatim in the subsection the `cost_blind` sentence (its held-out success and cost, which must be the same at every bar, those of `best_on_calibration`, and the percentage more it costs, under the three-decimal rule of 12.7), the nominal one-sided rate with the confidence of the Wilson bound, and the oracle's success and cost next to the highest routed success of either flavor at any bar, which must be at least 0.1 below the oracle's for "nowhere near"; and it checks the `cost_effective` bullet's claims against the file: its cleared rate falls and its cost rises as the bar rises, at the two lowest bars it spends under half of the best single model's cost at a lower held-out success than `cost_blind`, and at the highest bar its cost is at least 0.9 of the best single model's
- AutoBench tables (v1.17), from the committed `evidence/autobench-2026/results.json` summary, in the same order with one blank line between them:
  - routed: the RouterBench routed columns, order and formats, except that a rate the file holds as `null` (the bar-violation rate when no seed cleared) is printed as `-`
  - baselines: the oracle, the best single model (named, chosen as in 12.7), the model the dataset card's headline names (`openai/gpt-5.6-luna`, named), `best_on_calibration` and `cheapest` rows of the first (bar, flavor), columns as in RouterBench. The oracle label says some model has a Delta-Hit on every question, so the test also requires the oracle's success to be 1.0
- `tests/test_evidence_autobench_results.py` rebuilds both AutoBench tables, the counts sentence (models, the items of the one pooled task out of the 252 questions, seeds), the bar range and the Luna statement of 12.10 from the committed file and requires them verbatim in the `### AutoBench` subsection (from its heading to the next `###` or `##` heading), next to the card sentence; it also requires the number of files the converter reads (the `FILES` entries other than `SHA256SUMS`, as a word), the dataset URL, the revision link (display text the first 7 characters of the AutoBench converter's `REVISION`, target the tree URL at the full `REVISION`), the CC-BY-4.0 licence link and the attribution, the three evidence links, the default width named as the dataset's `delta_signal` of 0.2007 and not its full-precision value, the 2026-08-29 freeze, the relative-label caveat, the 12 to 33 questions per domain, the dropped counts of 12.9 (49 questions with an unscored pool cell, 107 cells, 388 rows for 13 models outside the pool), and the five-line regenerate block taken from `RESULTS.md`. A test also requires the opening paragraph, before the first `###`, to name the three labels as v1.28 words them (the two of v1.17, then the judge label) and the list-price estimate

- Where routing pays (v1.124): `research/where-routing-pays.md` and its script `research/where_routing_pays.py` (standard library and `model_routing.stats.wilson_lower_bound`, the bound the router uses) read only the five committed routing results files (12.7, 12.10, 12.12, 12.15, 12.17) and print, per dataset in the order of this section's subsections: the tasks, models, seeds, `calibration_fraction`, `min_samples` and `confidence`; the items per task and the expected calibration half `int(items * calibration_fraction)` (the 12.2 hash split varies the real count by seed, which the files do not record), named per task for at most five tasks and as lowest, highest and median otherwise, with the number of tasks whose half is below `min_samples`; the best single model of 12.7 (highest held-out success, then lower cost, then ID; a file where it differs between bars or flavors is an error) with its held-out success and cost, and the Wilson shortfall of that success, `success - wilson_lower_bound(round(success * n), n, confidence)`, at the smallest half and, when it differs, the median half; the `best_on_calibration` row of 12.3 at the first bar and flavor; one line naming what the files lack (each task's single-model rows and each seed's calibration size); and one row per flavor and bar, in config order, with the routed held-out success, cost, cost over the best single model's (`-` when that cost is 0), cleared rate, the number of distinct models in the `routed_models` of the per-task rows, the number of tasks whose `routed_models` hold two or more, and the most-picked model's share of all task and seed picks. Exit 0, or 2 with one `error: ` line on a missing or unreadable file, a document that is not a JSON object or holds a value of the wrong type (v1.126), a missing key or a summary row that is absent or repeated. The note opens with `## Facts` and holds, in order, `## Method`, `## Results` (one `text` block, the command line `$ python research/where_routing_pays.py` then the output, compared byte for byte with a fresh run on the committed files), `## Reading it`, `## What your own data needs`, `## Limits` and `## Regenerate`; every number of its prose outside the block, a URL and a code span (v1.126: a code span holds a model ID, a path or a name, whose digits are no claim) is in that output or is a DESIGN section number, and (v1.126) a test checks on the files that no routed row and no `best_on_calibration` row reaches the best single model's held-out success, the note's main claim. The README paragraph after the Results at a glance bullets is exactly `Why routing costs less than the best single model on some of these datasets and never reaches its held-out success: [research/where-routing-pays.md](research/where-routing-pays.md) sets each dataset's tasks, calibration halves and picks beside the best single model, read from the same files`; `research/README.md` gains its row and a bullet. Tests: `tests/test_research_where_routing_pays.py` and `tests/test_evidence_at_a_glance.py`. No change under `model_routing/`, to any results file or to any README transcript

### 12.9 Public evidence: the AutoBench 2026 converter (`evidence/autobench-2026/convert.py`, v1.14; tokens and registry v1.21)
Purpose: a second public outcome matrix in the 12.1 format, with 2026 models and billed cost per call. Standard library only (argparse, csv, hashlib, json, math, re, sys, urllib, pathlib), so it needs no extra; the package never imports it
- Source, pinned: dataset `AutoBench/router-benchmark-2026-10` on Hugging Face at revision `43755180be7d8097068982d7a3f37fc04cd15379`, licence CC-BY-4.0 (the card asks users to cite its paper). `FILES` maps each file the converter reads or checks to its byte size and sha256: `matrix.csv` (1,899,644 bytes), `questions.csv` (298,329), `pool.json` (6,582), `frozen_params.json` (2,042) and `SHA256SUMS` (1,198). The pins in the script are the trust root; the dataset's own `SHA256SUMS` must agree with them for the four data files, as a second check that the pins came from this revision
- `default_cache_dir()` is `~/.cache/ai-model-routing-evidence/autobench-2026/` (from `Path.home()`), never inside the repo. `fetch(cache_dir=None) -> Path` returns that folder after, for each file in `FILES` order: a present file is verified and kept, and one that fails `verify` is a `ValueError` naming it and is left in place; a missing one is downloaded with `urllib.request.urlopen` from `https://huggingface.co/datasets/AutoBench/router-benchmark-2026-10/resolve/<revision>/<name>` into `<name>.part`, verified, then renamed, and the `.part` file is removed when that fails. Last, `check_sums` runs on the text of `SHA256SUMS`
- `verify(path, size, sha256)`: `ValueError` naming the expected and the actual value when the byte size or the sha256 differs, as in 12.6
- `check_sums(text)`: reads lines of the form `<64 lowercase hex>  <name>` (two spaces, as `sha256sum` writes them); blank lines are skipped and any other line is a `ValueError`. `ValueError` when one of the four data files has no entry, or an entry that differs from `FILES` (the message names the file)
- `model_id(openrouter_id) -> str`: a string with exactly one `/` and both sides non-empty, else `ValueError`. Hyphens are removed from the vendor part with `re.sub("-", "", vendor)`, so `x-ai/grok-4.6` becomes `xai/grok-4.6` (models.json writes `xai/grok-4.7`) and `z-ai/glm-5.3` becomes `zai/glm-5.3`; the model part is kept as is. A rule, not a list, so a new hyphenated vendor needs no code change
- `pool_models(pool) -> dict[str, str]`: display ID to `model_id(openrouter_id)` for each entry of `pool["members"]`, in pool order. `ValueError` when `pool` is not a dict, `members` is missing or not a non-empty list, an entry is not a dict, a `display_id` or `openrouter_id` is not a non-empty string, a display ID repeats, or two entries map to one ID
- `pool_registry(pool) -> dict` (v1.21): a registry in the models.json format that `Registry.load` (4.1) reads, built from the pool's list prices: `{"source": {"dataset": "AutoBench/router-benchmark-2026-10", "revision": <the pinned revision>, "file": "pool.json"}, "models": [...]}`, one model per `members` entry, sorted by ID ascending so the pool order does not matter, each with the keys `id` (`model_id(openrouter_id)`), `family` (as written), `price_in` and `price_out` (`input_cost_per_mtok` and `output_cost_per_mtok` as floats, USD per 1M tokens, the unit of 4.1) and `context_window`, in that order. `ValueError` for an invalid pool as in `pool_models` (checked first, with its messages), and for a member whose `family` is not a non-empty string, whose `input_cost_per_mtok` or `output_cost_per_mtok` is not a finite number `>= 0` (a bool is not a number), or whose `context_window` is not an int `>= 1` (a bool is not an int); these messages name the display ID and the key. The prices are the pool's recorded list prices, frozen with it, so a cost from this registry is a list-price estimate (13.9), never the billed `cost`
- `delta_hit(score, pool_scores, width, top_k) -> bool`: the reference is the `math.fsum` of the `top_k` highest values of `pool_scores` divided by `top_k`, and the result is `score >= reference - width`. This is the dataset's Delta-Hit: with `width = delta_signal` and `top_k` from `frozen_params.json`, it gives the `delta_hit_signal` value of the dataset's `decisions.csv` on all 6,291 router decisions that carry one (checked when this section was written, against every scored pool cell of the question; `>` instead of `>=` also gives 6,291, so the boundary is not tested by the data)
- `convert(matrix, questions, pool, width, top_k=3, task_by="domain", tokens=False) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]`; `matrix` and `questions` are lists of dicts as `csv.DictReader` yields them (every value a string), `pool` is the parsed `pool.json`:
  - `ValueError`, checked before any output and the pool first, because `top_k` is bounded by its size: an invalid pool as in `pool_models`; a `width` that is a bool, not an int or float, not finite or `<= 0`; a `top_k` that is a bool, not an int, below 1 or above the number of pool models; a `task_by` other than `domain` and `all`; a `tokens` that is not a bool; a question whose `prompt_id` is not a non-empty string or repeats, or whose `ddc_domain` is not a non-empty string when `task_by` is `domain`; a matrix row whose `prompt_id` or `model` is not a non-empty string, whose `prompt_id` is not a question, or whose (`prompt_id`, `model`) pair repeats; for a pool model's row, a non-empty `score` that does not parse as a finite float, or a scored row whose `cost` does not parse as a finite float `>= 0` or, only when `tokens` is True, whose `input_tokens` or `output_tokens` is missing or does not parse as a finite float that is a whole number `>= 0` (these messages name the `prompt_id`, the model and, for a token count, the column)
  - repeated pairs: a (`prompt_id`, `model`) pair in two rows, for example once with `source` `original` and once with `fill_7_4`, is refused, never resolved by picking one. The pinned file has none: its 388 `fill_7_4` rows are all for 13 models outside the 20-model pool
  - out of pool: rows for a model that is not a pool display ID are dropped and counted (in the pinned file, exactly those 388 `fill_7_4` rows, which the dataset uses to grade router picks outside the pool)
  - unscored: an empty `score` is an unscored cell, and a pool model with no row for a question counts as one too. A question is kept only when every pool model has a score on it, so the output is the complete matrix 12.3 requires; a question with an unscored pool cell is dropped and counted, never scored as a miss
  - each kept question is one item: item = `prompt_id`; task = its `ddc_domain` as written (for example `9 History`), or `all` for every item when `task_by` is `all`; one row per pool model with model = its mapped ID, `correct` = `delta_hit(score, the scores of all pool models on that question, width, top_k)` and `cost_usd` = the billed `cost` as a float, with a negative zero written as `0.0`
  - output rows have the keys `task`, `item`, `model`, `correct`, `cost_usd` in that order, sorted by (task, item, model) as strings; the result does not depend on the row order of either file
  - tokens (v1.21): with `tokens` True each row also has `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`, in that order after `cost_usd`: that cell's `matrix.csv` columns written as ints (the pinned file writes them as, for example, `303.0`). `output_tokens` already counts the reasoning tokens (13.9), so `reasoning_tokens` is never read. With `tokens` False, the default, no token column is read, so the rows, the report and every error are those of v1.14. The report is the same in both
  - the report has keys `models` (IDs ascending), `width`, `top_k`, `task_by`, `kept` and `dropped_unscored` (each `{task: number of questions}` with tasks ascending), `unscored_cells` (pool cells with no score, missing rows included), `dropped_out_of_pool` (`{model as written: rows}`, ascending) and `rows` (the number of output rows)
- `read_csv(path) -> list[dict]` reads UTF-8 with `csv.DictReader`; `write_jsonl(rows, path)` as in 12.6
- CLI: `python evidence/autobench-2026/convert.py --out PATH [--cache DIR] [--width W] [--task-by {domain,all}] [--tokens] [--registry PATH]`, entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`. Options are checked first (no download for a bad one); then it fetches and checks the files, takes `top_k`, and `delta_signal` as the width unless `--width` is given, from `frozen_params.json`, converts, writes `<out>.part` (and, with `--registry`, the registry's `.part` file), prints the report as `json.dumps(report, indent=2)`, flushing stdout, and only then renames them (v1.94, as 13.11), so a write to stdout that fails (a closed pipe) is an I/O error that leaves `--out` and `--registry` as they were. `--width 0.3011090610836323` is the dataset's official width (`delta_official`). `--tokens` (v1.21) passes `tokens=True`. `--registry PATH` (v1.21) also writes `json.dumps(pool_registry(pool), indent=2)` plus a newline to PATH; a `--registry` for which `--out`, `--registry` and their two `.part` paths do not resolve to four different files (for example `--registry` equal to `--out`, or `--out x.part --registry x`) is a usage error whose message says they name the same file, and an invalid pool for the registry fails before any file is written. Both files are written in full to their `.part` paths before either is renamed, `--out` first. A `--width` that is not a finite number `> 0` is a usage error whose message names the value; a `frozen_params.json` that is not an object, has no `top_k`, or has no `delta_signal` when `--width` is not given is invalid data whose message names the file and the key. It returns 0 on success and 2 with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr and nothing on stdout for a bad option, a size, hash or `SHA256SUMS` mismatch, invalid data or an I/O error, but for a rename that fails after the print (v1.94), the one failure with the report already on stdout; on any failure before the renames neither `--out` nor `--registry` is written, and each `.part` file is removed
- Default options on the pinned files (checked when this section was written): 20 models; 203 of 252 questions kept, 49 dropped for 107 unscored pool cells; 4,060 rows; 10 domain tasks of 12 to 33 questions. Mean hit rate over all cells 0.667 at the default width and 0.762 at the official width. With `--tokens` (v1.21) the rows are the same with the two counts added: `input_tokens` 66 to 2,341 and `output_tokens` 3 to 42,188; 2 rows are billed 0, and on the other 4,058 the `--registry` list prices times those counts give a median ratio of 1.000 to the billed `cost`
- Reading it: a Delta-Hit is relative, not an absolute acceptance check. It means "rated about as well as the best three answers in this pool on this question" by a committee of up to 10 judge models taken from the same pool, so a hit depends on which other models were asked, and the ratings are model judgments, not gold labels. The costs are the billed USD per call in the matrix frozen on 2026-08-29 (the card dates the `fill_7_4` rows 2026-09-05, and every one of them is dropped). A domain has 12 to 33 kept questions, so its calibration half holds fewer than 30 items and the default `min_samples = 30` (5.3) cannot be met per domain; `--task-by all` pools them into one task of 203 items, which is the matrix the results slice commits (the per-domain matrix only as a stated sensitivity at a lower `--min-samples`)

### 12.10 AutoBench results (`evidence/autobench-2026/results.json`, v1.16)
Purpose: the evaluator's numbers on the AutoBench matrix in the shape of 12.7, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the tables and the numbers RESULTS.md states (listed under Tests below) checked against the committed file or, for the sensitivity runs and the split sizes, against the pinned files when they are cached
- Script: `evidence/routerbench/results.py` (12.7) is reused unchanged. Nothing in `reduce` or `table` depends on the dataset, so the AutoBench file is written and printed by the same `--evaluate` and `--table` commands with AutoBench paths
- Committed: `evidence/autobench-2026/results.json`, from the converter's `--task-by all` matrix at the default width (12.9: one task, `all`, of 203 items over 20 models) and `model-routing evaluate --bar 0.6 0.7 0.8 0.9 --seeds 50 --json` at the default `min_samples` 30 and confidence 0.95, reduced by `results.py --evaluate`; and `evidence/autobench-2026/RESULTS.md` with the regenerate commands, the caveats of 12.9 "Reading it", and the `table` output in a fenced `text` block whose first line is `$ python evidence/routerbench/results.py --table evidence/autobench-2026/results.json`
- The dataset card's headline: RESULTS.md quotes the card at the pinned revision, "no commercial pass beats always calling GPT-5.6 Luna on quality and cost together", and states from the committed summary whether any routed (bar, flavor) row beats the `model:openai/gpt-5.6-luna` row of the first (bar, flavor) on both counts: held-out success at least as high and cost at most as high, compared at full precision, one of the two strictly. The test builds that statement from the file, so it changes when the numbers do. RESULTS.md also gives the number of the 50 seeds that `cost_effective` at bar 0.7 routes to that model, taken from its routed per-task row
- Sensitivity, stated in RESULTS.md with the commands that give it and not committed as files: the official width (`--width 0.3011090610836323`) at bar 0.8, and the per-domain matrix (the default `--task-by domain`, 10 tasks of 12 to 33 items) at `--min-samples 5` at bars 0.6 and 0.8, each with 50 seeds
- Tests, in `tests/test_evidence_autobench_results.py`: the committed file is under 300,000 bytes, is exactly `json.dumps(reduce(itself), indent=2)` plus a newline, has that config, the one task `all` with 203 items and the converter's 20 model IDs, unique summary and per-task keys, and 50 seeds behind every `routed_models`; RESULTS.md contains the table block with the current `table` output, the five-line regenerate block, the card sentence, the Luna statement, the bar 0.7 seed count with the direction of its gap to that model and the weaker single-model rates of the other models it routes to, the "Reading it" numbers built from the file (the bar 0.6 cost ratio and success against the best single model, the cleared-seed count and cost ratio at bar 0.8, the best single model's success at 0.9, the `cost_blind` row next to `best_on_calibration`, the bar-violation counts at bars 0.7 and 0.8 over the seeds that cleared, the nominal `(1 - confidence) / 2`) and the committed file's byte size. When the pinned files are in the default cache (12.9), three more tests rebuild `results.json` in process (convert, `evaluate` with `cells` removed, `reduce`) and require the committed bytes; rebuild every sensitivity number and require each phrase; and rebuild the calibration-half sizes (pooled over the 50 seeds, and per domain over every (domain, seed) split, whose largest must stay below `min_samples`) with the count of per-domain halves smaller than the fewest questions on which a perfect score has a 95% Wilson lower bound of 0.8, and the pooled half sizes in the "Reading it" sentence for bar 0.9 (v1.17). CI never downloads the files, so there they skip. RESULTS.md says what the card graded only in the card's own words, in double quotes: "Seven routers were then graded by lookup: a router only names a model; we read that cell", and, for `scoreboard.json`, "The published board" and "28 passes" (v1.17). The test requires each quote in RESULTS.md and, when the card at the pinned revision is in the default cache as `README.md` with sha256 `30f119c55031ca3c70f740ff3dfc395e580fe633bd513be94891a9c3acfdd168`, each quote and the headline sentence in the card with its `**` bold markers removed, and the two `scoreboard.json` quotes on the card's `scoreboard.json` table row, which is the attribution RESULTS.md makes, else it skips. The card is not one of the converter's files (it is fetched by hand), and its bytes differ from the `SHA256SUMS` entry for `README.md`, so the pin is its own
- The evidence README's counts (107 unscored pool cells, 49 dropped questions, 388 rows for 13 models outside the pool, mean success 0.667 and 0.762, 10 domain tasks of 12 to 33) are required in that README as written, and the cached-file test of 12.9 builds the same phrases from the converter report

### 12.11 Judge evidence: the AutoBench committee converter (`evidence/autobench-2026/judges.py`, v1.24; visible answer v1.26)
Purpose: public evidence for the first-class case, choosing a judge model, on 2026 models. AutoBench graded every answer with a committee of judge models and publishes each judge's grade, so a judge outcome matrix in the 12.1 format can be built from it with no API key. Standard library only (argparse, csv, importlib, itertools, json, math, sys, pathlib); the package never imports it
- Reuse: the script loads `convert.py` from its own folder by path with `importlib` and exposes that module as its attribute `convert`. The pinned revision, `FILES`, `fetch`, `verify`, `check_sums`, `pool_models`, `pool_registry`, `TASK_BY`, `read_csv`, `write_jsonl` and its whole-number token check `_count` (v1.25) are that module's (12.9), never copies, so the script holds no sha256 literal and no download code
- Data facts, checked on the pinned files when this section was written: `judges_json` is a JSON object {judge display ID: grade}, with exactly 10 judges on each of the 4,933 scored pool cells and `{}` on the 107 unscored ones; a grade is an int from 1 to 5, a float from 1.1 to 5.0, or `null` (738 nulls); the 14 judges are the pool's `judge_models`, all of them pool members, and `Kimi-k2.6` is listed on only 274 cells and grades 269 of them (5 `null`); the 4,933 cells carry 508 different judge sets, so no judge grades every cell; the dataset's `score` differs by more than 1e-6 from the plain mean of the non-null grades on 4,695 of the 4,933, so `score` only tells scored from unscored cells and the grades are read directly
- Terms: an **answer** is a scored pool cell (one `prompt_id` and the pool model that answered it); a **candidate** is a judge the router chooses among, a model of the output matrix; an answer's **graders** are its judges whose grade is not `null`; for a candidate c on an answer by model a, the **committee** is every grader other than c and a, so an answer model's grade of its own answer never enters a committee
- `judge_matrix(matrix, pool, tolerance=0.5, candidates=None, size=5, min_committee=3, rubric_tokens=400, grade_tokens=200, task_by="all", questions=None, answer_tokens="visible") -> tuple[list[dict], dict]` (`answer_tokens` v1.26, last so that positional calls keep their meaning); `matrix` and `questions` are lists of dicts as `csv.DictReader` yields them, `pool` is the parsed `pool.json`:
  - `ValueError`, before any output, in this order: an invalid pool as in `pool_models`; a `tolerance` that is a bool, not an int or float, not finite or `< 0`; `candidates` that is not `None` and not a non-empty list or tuple, holds an entry that is not a pool display ID, or repeats one; a `size` or `min_committee` (each `>= 1`), or a `rubric_tokens` or `grade_tokens` (each `>= 0`), that is a bool, not an int, or below its bound (`size` is checked even when candidates are given, which then ignore it); an `answer_tokens` that is not one of the strings `visible` and `output` (v1.26); a `task_by` not in `TASK_BY`; with `task_by` `domain`, `questions` that is not a list, or a question with no or a repeated `prompt_id` or no `ddc_domain` as in 12.9 (with `all`, `questions` is never read). Then per matrix row: not a dict, or a `prompt_id` or `model` that is not a non-empty string; with `domain`, a `prompt_id` that names no question; a repeated (`prompt_id`, `model`)
  - a row whose model is not a pool display ID is dropped and counted per name, and a pool row with an empty `score` is counted as unscored, before anything else in it is read. On every other row `judges_json` must be a JSON object with non-empty keys whose values are ints or floats (not bools, finite) or `null`, and `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`, and with `answer_tokens` `visible` also `reasoning_tokens` (v1.26), must be whole numbers `>= 0` as in 12.9 (the `_count` check, so an empty value is an error), checked in that order, so a row with several bad counts names the first; otherwise a `ValueError` naming the cell. With `output`, `reasoning_tokens` is never read, so the rows and the errors are those of v1.25. On the pinned files every scored pool cell has a whole `reasoning_tokens` (0 on 471 of them); the 118 empty values are on 94 of the 107 unscored pool rows and on 24 rows outside the pool, which are never read
  - candidates: the given ones, or by default the `size` judges, among the pool display IDs that grade at least one answer, whose set keeps the most answers under the rules below; ties go to the set whose ascending display IDs come first, which is the first such set in `itertools.combinations` order over the sorted IDs. Judges outside the pool may sit on committees but are never candidates, since they have no price. `ValueError` when `size` is more than those judges. (v1.129) The default search checks the `min_committee` rule below once per answer, groups the answers that pass it by the set of pool judges among their graders other than their answer model, and counts for each candidate set, in `itertools.combinations` order, the answers of the groups whose set holds it. Those are the answers the rules below keep for that set, since a set of pool judges that holds neither the answer model nor a judge outside the graders is exactly a subset of that group set, so the chosen set is the one that applying the rules to every answer for every set gives; the grouping reads each answer once, and the count for each set then grows with the number of distinct groups instead of answers. The rules below then run once per answer, for the chosen set only
  - each answer meets the first of these that applies and is counted under it: its answer model is a candidate (`dropped_self_graded`: generator isolation, 4.2, would never let a model grade its own answer, and dropping the whole answer keeps every candidate on every item); some candidate is not among its graders (`dropped_missing_candidate`, so the matrix is complete as 12.3 requires); it has fewer than `min_committee + 1` graders other than its answer model, so some committee would be smaller than `min_committee` (`dropped_small_committee`); otherwise it is kept. An answer with no graders at all is a missing-candidate drop, and a given candidate that grades no answer is accepted and keeps none, so no committee is ever empty
  - each kept answer is one item: item = `<prompt_id>|<the answer model's mapped ID>`; task = `all`, or the question's `ddc_domain` as written with `task_by` `domain`; one row per candidate with model = its mapped ID, `correct` = `abs(grade - mean) <= tolerance` in floating point as written, with no epsilon, where mean = `math.fsum` of the committee's grades divided by the committee size; `input_tokens` = the cell's `input_tokens` + the answer + `rubric_tokens` (the question, the answer and an assumed rubric), where the answer (v1.26) is `max(output_tokens - reasoning_tokens, 0)` with `answer_tokens` `visible`, the default, and the cell's `output_tokens` with `output`; and `output_tokens` = `grade_tokens` (an assumed grade length). Rows carry no `cost_usd`: they are token-form rows (12.1) that `model-routing evaluate --registry` prices with the registry `pool_registry` builds, at the pool's list prices, so every judge cost is an ESTIMATE, never a billed cost
  - row keys `task`, `item`, `model`, `correct`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens` in that order, rows sorted by (task, item, model); the result does not depend on the order of the rows of either input
  - report keys, in order: `candidates` (mapped IDs ascending), `candidates_by` (`given` or `most_cells`), `tolerance`, `min_committee`, `rubric_tokens`, `grade_tokens`, `answer_tokens` (v1.26), `task_by`, `scored_cells` (the answers read), `kept` ({task: answers}, tasks ascending), `dropped_self_graded`, `dropped_missing_candidate`, `dropped_small_committee`, `self_graded_cells` (answers whose answer model is among their graders, kept or not: the cells a self-grading check would measure), `unscored_cells`, `dropped_out_of_pool` ({name: rows}, names ascending), `rows`. `scored_cells` equals the kept answers plus the three dropped counts
- The answer a judge reads (v1.26): a judge is shown the answer, never the answer model's reasoning, and a cell's `output_tokens` is the billed output count with the reasoning in it. On the 4,058 billed rows of 12.9, the `--registry` list prices times `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` give a median ratio of 1.000 to the billed `cost`, and 1.710 when `reasoning_tokens` is added on top (checked when this was written; 13.9 finds 1.001 and 1.718 on the 4,931 billed scored cells). So `visible`, the output less the reasoning, is the default estimate of the answer's length. The two counts are not exact: on 104 of the 4,933 scored cells `reasoning_tokens` is above `output_tokens`, by at most 21%, and there the floor counts the answer as 0 tokens, an underestimate, since its true length is unknown. `output` keeps the v1.25 estimate, an upper bound that counts the reasoning as read. The option changes only `input_tokens`: the candidates, the drops, the items and every `correct` are the same under both
- What the label is: a leave-one-out committee label, agreement with the other judges, and not a human gold label. A judge that always grades as the committee does scores 1.0 whether or not the committee is right, and a judge that is right where the committee is wrong scores lower. Answers to the same question are separate items, so the 12.2 split can put answers to one question in both halves
- CLI: `python evidence/autobench-2026/judges.py --out PATH [--cache DIR] [--registry PATH] [--tolerance T] [--candidates A,B,...] [--size K] [--min-committee N] [--rubric-tokens N] [--grade-tokens N] [--task-by {domain,all}] [--answer-tokens {visible,output}]`, each option defaulting to the function's (`--task-by` to `all`, `--answer-tokens`, v1.26, to `visible`; any other value is a usage error). It calls `convert.fetch(--cache)`, runs `judge_matrix` on the cached `matrix.csv` and `pool.json` (and `questions.csv` with `domain`) with `--candidates` split on commas, writes the rows as JSONL and, with `--registry`, `json.dumps(pool_registry(pool), indent=2)` and a newline (byte-identical to `convert.py --registry`), each through `<path>.part`, both written before either is renamed, with the `.part` files removed on failure; `--out`, `--registry` and their two `.part` paths must be four different files, as in 12.9. It prints `json.dumps(report, indent=2)` once the `.part` files are written and before either rename, flushing stdout (v1.94, as 13.11), so a write to stdout that fails (a closed pipe) is an `OSError` that leaves `--out` and `--registry` as they were, and exits 0, or exits 2 with one `error: ...` line on stderr (line breaks escaped) for a usage error (including an empty name in `--candidates`) or any `ValueError`, `OSError` or `csv.Error`; a rename that fails after the print is the one failure with the report already on stdout. A failure before the renames writes no output file and leaves an existing one as it was; the two renames are not atomic together, so a failure in the second leaves `--out` renamed and `--registry` not, as in 12.9
- On the pinned files (a private prototype of this contract, run when this section was written, and the script since v1.25; the cached-file tests pin these): the default candidates are `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731`, `google/gemma-4-31b-it`, `meta/muse-glimmer-30b`, `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` and `tencent/hy3`; 756 of the 4,933 answers are kept (1,252 self-graded, 2,925 missing a candidate, 0 with a small committee), 3,780 rows; 2,478 answers are self-graded in all (2,514 list their answer model among their judges when `null` grades count); 107 unscored cells and 388 out-of-pool rows over 13 names. `size` 5 is the default as a trade between candidates and items: each candidate more cuts the kept answers by about a third (1,191 at 4, 756 at 5, 460 at 6), and at 5 the router still has 756 answer items, against the 203 question items of the 12.9 answer matrix. On the 756 kept answers, 707 report reasoning tokens, their `reasoning_tokens` summing to 0.738 of their `output_tokens`, and on 14 `reasoning_tokens` is above `output_tokens`, by at most 10% (counts of v1.25). v1.26: the floor applies to those 14, and with the default `visible` the judge input is 1,218,584 tokens per candidate over the 756 kept answers, 0.406 of the 3,001,074 that `output` counts (v1.26)
- Test status: `tests/test_evidence_autobench_judges.py` was committed before the script, under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=ModuleNotFoundError)`, which the implementing commit removed (v1.25). All but five tests use tiny in-memory tables. Two read the evidence README (`evidence/autobench-2026/README.md`, section `Judge evidence: one item per graded answer`), which states the regenerate line, every assumption above with its option and default, and the counts on the pinned files. Three read the pinned files only when they are already cached and skip otherwise: the default report, the rows feeding `evaluate`, and every count of that README section built from the report and `matrix.csv` and required as written, as the 12.10 counts test does. v1.26: the `answer_tokens` tests (the subtraction and its floor, the option check, `reasoning_tokens` read only with `visible`, the report key, the CLI option) were committed failing before the code, and the counts test also builds the two judge input totals from the rows of both options

### 12.12 Judge results on AutoBench (`evidence/autobench-judges/results.json`, v1.27; routing and whole-prose checks v1.29)
Purpose: the evaluator's numbers for choosing a judge model on public data, in the shape of 12.7 and 12.10, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the table and every number RESULTS.md states checked against the committed file or, for the sensitivity runs, against the pinned files when they are cached
- Script: `evidence/routerbench/results.py` (12.7) is reused unchanged, as in 12.10. The judge rows carry token counts and no cost, so `evaluate` prices them from the registry `judges.py --registry` writes (12.1 token form, 12.11), and every `cost_usd` in the file is a list-price estimate, never a billed cost
- Committed: `evidence/autobench-judges/results.json`, from the 12.11 judge matrix at its default options (one task, `all`, of 756 answer items over the 5 default candidates) and `model-routing evaluate --registry <that registry> --bar 0.6 0.7 0.8 0.9 --seeds 50 --json` at the default `min_samples` 30 and confidence 0.95, reduced by `results.py --evaluate`; and `evidence/autobench-judges/RESULTS.md` with the five-line regenerate block, the `table` output in a fenced `text` block whose first line is `$ python evidence/routerbench/results.py --table evidence/autobench-judges/results.json`, the size of the committed file in bytes, and the sections below
- Names: the best single judge is the best model of 12.7 (highest `success` of the first (bar, flavor), ties by lower cost, then by name); the cheapest candidate is the `model:` row of the first (bar, flavor) with the lowest `cost_usd`, ties by name. A cost ratio is a routed `cost_usd` over the best single judge's, to 3 decimals; success to 3 decimals and cost to 4, as in 12.7
- Intro: the number of answer items, of candidates and of seeds, and the 12.11 default tolerance, each from the committed file or the script's default; a header paragraph names the best single judge as the `vs_best_model` divisor. v1.29: the test builds the whole intro paragraph, the whole header paragraph (the one before the table block) and the whole Caveats list from the committed file, the 12.11 defaults and the converter's dataset and revision, and requires each to equal its RESULTS.md text, so a number added beside a checked phrase fails, as it does for the table and the Reading it bullets
- Reading it, each bullet built from the committed file by the test and required verbatim:
  - every seed clears: the bars at which `cost_effective` clears on every seed and routes all 50 seeds to one judge, which must be the cheapest candidate, with its held-out success, its cost ratio, and the best single judge's success and cost
  - partly and never: the first bar at which `cost_effective` clears on some seeds but not all, with the cleared seed count, the seeds per routed judge (most seeds first, then by name), its success and cost ratio; then the first bar at which no seed clears, with the best single judge's held-out success; the fallback judge is the one with the highest calibration-half Wilson lower bound by the 5.3 `best_available` sort, which the per-seed check below confirms on the pinned files (v1.29)
  - `cost_blind`: its success, cost ratio and seeds per routed judge, which must be the same at every bar, and whether it is level with `best_on_calibration` at full precision at every bar (said as "level with" only then); it routes to the judge with the highest calibration-half Wilson lower bound because under `cost_blind` both 5.3 sorts reduce to (minus the lower bound, ID) when every candidate has at least `min_samples` calibration answers, which the per-seed check below confirms on the pinned files (v1.29)
  - bar violations: each routed rate above 0 as a count of the seeds that cleared (one task, so a rate counts seeds), next to the nominal one-sided rate `(1 - confidence) / 2` of the Wilson bound
  - the oracle: its success and cost; while its success is below 1, that where no candidate agrees with the committee it takes the cheapest candidate on that answer, counted wrong (12.3), with that share of the held-out answers, 1 minus its success, and when it is 1, that some candidate agrees on every held-out answer; next to the best single judge's success
  - price: the two candidates with the highest cost, named with their success and cost, stated to be the two that agree least only when they are; `openai/gpt-5.6-luna`, when it is one of them, is named as the answer model of the dataset card's headline (12.10)
- Sensitivity, stated in RESULTS.md with the commands that give it and not committed as files: `--tolerance 1.0`, and `--answer-tokens output` (12.11, the judge reads the reasoning too, an upper bound on cost with the same labels), each at bar 0.8 with 50 seeds; for each, the best single judge's line and both routed lines in the line format of 12.10. The `--tolerance 1.0` matrix has the default matrix's (task, item, model) rows in the same order, every default success stays a success and more rows are successes, which RESULTS.md states as more of the judges' grades counting as successes; the `--answer-tokens output` matrix has the same rows with the same labels and no row with fewer input tokens, and RESULTS.md says only of its three lines, which the test checks against the committed file, that held-out success, cleared and bar-violation rates are those of the default run and only the costs rise
- Caveats: the committee label is agreement with the other judges, not a human gold label; the cost is a list-price estimate at the pool's prices with the assumed rubric and grade tokens of 12.11; the candidates are the 5 that keep the most answers, not chosen by quality or price; answers to one question are separate items and the split can put them in both halves; the licence, dataset URL and pinned revision; a link to the 12.11 evidence README section
- Tests, in `tests/test_evidence_autobench_judges_results.py`: the committed file is under 300,000 bytes, is exactly `json.dumps(reduce(itself), indent=2)` plus a newline, has that config, the one task `all` with 756 items and the 5 default candidates of 12.11, unique summary and per-task keys and 50 seeds behind every `routed_models`; RESULTS.md holds the table block, the two command blocks, the intro, the header, the file size and every Reading it bullet above, built from the file, and every other number of its prose (seed counts, the sensitivity bar, the candidate count, section references) as written; two tests read the pinned files only when cached and skip otherwise (CI never downloads them): one rebuilds the committed bytes in-process from `judge_matrix` and `evaluate`, the other rebuilds every sensitivity line and checks the matrix comparisons and the higher costs of the sensitivity bullet. The tests were committed before the files under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=FileNotFoundError)`, which the commit that adds the files removes. v1.29: when the pinned files are cached (12.9), a test rebuilds the matrix, runs `evaluate` with its cells at the committed config, and for every (bar, flavor, seed) cell recomputes each candidate's calibration-half successes over the 12.2 split (its hash rule re-derived in the test), its `wilson_lower_bound` at the run's confidence and its mean calibration cost, and requires the cell's `cleared` and routed judge to be what 5.3 gives: when some candidate's lower bound reaches the bar, the first such candidate by (routing cost, minus the lower bound, ID), else the first candidate by (minus the lower bound, routing cost, ID), with routing cost 0 under `cost_blind`; every calibration half must hold at least `min_samples` answers, and the routed seed counts per (bar, flavor) must equal the committed `routed_models`. The routed judge must differ from the cheapest by mean calibration cost in some fallback cell and in some `cost_blind` cell. Every candidate is graded on the same calibration half, so its Wilson lower bound orders the candidates as their calibration success does, and the check confirms the routed judge is the highest on both. CI never downloads the files, so there it skips

### 12.13 Regeneration on GitHub (`.github/workflows/evidence.yml`, v1.30; pinned runner v1.49; LiveCodeBench v1.65; delegation on tau2-bench v1.80; its pooled run v1.84)
Purpose: a reader can see the AutoBench-based results files, from v1.55 the tau2-bench one, from v1.65 the LiveCodeBench one, from v1.80 the tau2-bench delegation one and from v1.84 the pooled tau2-bench delegation one regenerate byte for byte from the public data. The cached-file tests of 12.10, 12.12, 12.15, 12.17, 13.10 and 13.12 show it only where the pinned files are cached, and `ci.yml` never downloads them, so on GitHub those tests skip
- The workflow `.github/workflows/evidence.yml`, `name: evidence`, runs on `workflow_dispatch` and on `push` to `main` limited by `paths` to `evidence/**`, `model_routing/**`, `pyproject.toml` and the workflow file itself; `permissions: contents: read`; `bash` as the default `run` shell (so a failing command stops the step); no secret, no `pull_request_target`, no write permission. `README.md` is not in `paths`: a push that changes a command in one of the README regenerate blocks the workflow reruns (three to v1.54, four from v1.55, five from v1.65, six from v1.80, and from v1.84 seven: one per rerun subsection and the pooled one of `### Delegation on tau2-bench`) fails `tests/test_evidence_workflow.py` in `ci.yml` unless the workflow changes with it, and a change to the workflow runs it
- One job, `regenerate`, holding only `runs-on` (that of `ci.yml`) and `steps`, with the `actions/checkout` and `actions/setup-python` versions of `ci.yml`; checkout keeps no credentials (`persist-credentials: false`), and setup-python installs one Python version, one of those in the `ci.yml` matrix
- Runner (v1.49): `ci.yml` and this workflow each have one `runs-on`, the versioned image label `ubuntu-24.04`, the same in both, never `ubuntu-latest`. GitHub moves `ubuntu-latest` to Ubuntu 26.04 between 2026-10-19 and 2026-11-19 (runner-images issue 14748, the notice on each of the 5 check runs of v1.48, all of its second commit, since its two commits were pushed together), and a pinned label keeps each run on the image the suite last passed on, so a move of the label does not change the image under a push (a new release of an action or of a Python build still can change a run with no code change). In each file, the column-0 comment lines directly above `jobs:` say the runner is pinned to that image, with the date of the pin and the reason. Moving the label is a change to both files together, kept only after a green run on the new label; on 2026-11-06 `actions/python-versions` (revision 0bd7e714) lists x64 builds for Ubuntu 26.04 of every `ci.yml` matrix version (3.10.22, 3.11.18, 3.12.16 and 3.13.18), so setup-python could serve the matrix there
- Python versions (v1.118): the `ci.yml` matrix is `["3.10", "3.11", "3.12", "3.13", "3.14"]`, the one `python-version` list of the job `test`, every minor version from the lowest to the highest in ascending order, and (v1.120, review) the only key of its `strategy.matrix`, with no `include` or `exclude`, while every other `ci.yml` line that names `python-version` only reads `matrix.python-version`, so the list is the Python of every job; `pyproject.toml` keeps `requires-python = ">=3.10"`, the lowest matrix version, so every Python minor version from the floor the package states through the newest one tested has a CI job; and the line in the README `## Development` section says `CI runs the suite and the demo on Python 3.10 to 3.14`, the first and last matrix versions. The basis is the Python devguide status page (https://devguide.python.org/versions/, read 2026-11-08): 3.15 (first release 2026-10-09) and 3.14 (first release 2025-10-07) in bugfix, 3.13, 3.12 and 3.11 in security, and 3.10 end-of-life since 2026-10-01; v1.118 specifies adding 3.14, which the v1.117 matrix lacked, and v1.119 adds it. 3.10 stays: the package is standard library only and passes on it, `actions/setup-python` still installs it on the pinned runner (the `actions/python-versions` manifest at revision 0bd7e714 lists 3.10.22 for Ubuntu 24.04 x64, and the `test (3.10)` job of `ci` run 42529224187 on 2026-11-08 passed), and raising the floor would refuse an install on 3.10 with no change in the code. It leaves the matrix, with `requires-python` raised to `>=3.11` in the same commit, when setup-python can no longer install it on the pinned runner or the code needs a 3.11 feature. 3.15 is not in the matrix yet, although the same manifest lists 3.15.2 for Ubuntu 24.04 x64: the pre-push check runs the suite locally on every matrix version, and on 2026-11-08 the interpreter installer it uses offered only the pre-release 3.15.0a8, so a 3.15 job would be one that check could not repeat; 3.15 joins the matrix once a final 3.15 runs there. Tests in `tests/test_ci_pythons.py` (standard library only) require the matrix to be the list this bullet writes, with quoted `X.Y` items, consecutive and ascending, the matrix block to hold that list alone and no other `python-version` line but reads of `matrix.python-version` (v1.120), the `requires-python` floor to be its first version and the README line, in `## Development`, to name its first and last, and each of 14 broken variants (10 to v1.119) of those four files fails the check
- Steps, in this order: the checkout, the Python, `Install` (`python -m pip install -e .`, the install line every one of those README regenerate blocks starts with), `Regenerate` (one `run` block whose lines are, in order, the README regenerate commands of `### AutoBench`, `### AutoBench judges`, `### tau2-bench` (v1.55), `### LiveCodeBench` (v1.65), `### Delegation on AutoBench` and `### Delegation on tau2-bench` (v1.80), in README heading order, each block without its install line; the `--table` lines are kept so the log shows the regenerated tables; the converters download the pinned files and check their sizes and sha256 as in 12.9 and 12.14, the tau2-bench one 12 run files of 295,933,742 bytes in all from `raw.githubusercontent.com`, and, v1.65, the LiveCodeBench one its 67 files of 1,233,548,121 bytes in all from there, each path URL-quoted as in 12.16; v1.80: the tau2-bench delegation block's converter line, `--calls`, finds the 12 run files already in the cache the `### tau2-bench` line filled, and `fetch` checks each one's size and sha256 again before it reads them; v1.84: the `### Delegation on tau2-bench` lines are both of its blocks in order, the per-domain one and then the pooled one, which has no install line, and the pooled converter line, `--calls --task-by all`, reads the same cached files), and `Compare` (`git diff --exit-code --` followed by every `--out` path under `evidence/` of those commands, in command order: from v1.65 `evidence/autobench-2026/results.json`, `evidence/autobench-judges/results.json`, `evidence/tau2-bench/results.json`, `evidence/livecodebench/results.json`, `evidence/delegation/results.json` and `evidence/delegation/variants.json`, from v1.80 `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.json` after them, and from v1.84 `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/pooled.json` last (v1.55 to v1.64 the same without the LiveCodeBench file; to v1.54 the three AutoBench-based `results.json` files and, from v1.48, `variants.json` after them, since the delegation README block then ends with the two Variant points lines of 13.10)), so the run fails when a regenerated file differs by one byte from the committed one. Each step holds only its keys: `uses` and `with` for the two actions, `name` and `run` for the three named steps, so no step has an `if`, `continue-on-error`, `shell`, `working-directory` or `env`
- RouterBench stays out: its source is a 99,567,659-byte pickle (the 12.6 `SIZE`) that needs pandas (the `evidence` extra), and its evaluate step takes about 70 seconds. The comment at the top of the workflow gives the same reason in the words of the README sentence of 12.8, and from v1.55 names the four subsections whose commands it reruns, from v1.65 the five, from v1.80 the six
- LiveCodeBench is rerun (v1.65) although its files are about 1.23 GB, about 12 times RouterBench's pickle: its converter is standard library only, reads no pickle, and its evaluate step took about 5.5 seconds (12.17), so the run's added time is mostly the download. When v1.65 was written, a `HEAD` request for the URL-quoted path of a kept file whose folder name holds spaces and brackets (`Grok-3-Mini%20%28High%29/...`) got HTTP 200 with that file's pinned byte count, 4,361,019; the whole download on GitHub is checked by the run, not by the suite
- README: the evidence section's opening paragraph (12.8) ends with the sentence that names the workflow, links the file, names the subsections it reruns and the results files of the `Compare` line (three to v1.47, four from v1.48, five from v1.55, six from v1.65, seven from v1.80, eight from v1.84) and gives the RouterBench reason; the test requires it verbatim
- Tests, in `tests/test_evidence_workflow.py` (standard library only, so they run in CI): the workflow is read with a line parser for this file's shape, and the test requires the triggers, permissions, default shell, the job header, the actions, the Python version, the step names, order and keys, the install line, the `Regenerate` lines rebuilt from the README blocks (one `bash` block per rerun subsection, and from v1.84 two in `### Delegation on tau2-bench`, read in order; a subsection with another count fails) and the `Compare` line rebuilt from their `--out` paths, and the top comment's RouterBench reason; a comment or blank line inside a block is read as part of it; it fails on a secret, a `pull_request_target` trigger or any `write` permission, by substring, so a comment that names them fails too; and each of 16 broken workflows (17 from v1.84, which adds a `Compare` line without `pooled.json`) fails the check (from v1.85 the two `Compare` mutants, without `variants.json` and without `pooled.json`, on the `Compare` check alone), among them a README command dropped, swapped or added, a changed `paths` list, a write permission, a job shell without `pipefail`, an `if: false` on `Regenerate`, a `continue-on-error` on `Compare`, a `working-directory`, a comment inside `Regenerate` and a second job; from v1.49 the runner check requires in each of `ci.yml` and the workflow one `runs-on` naming a versioned `ubuntu-XX.YY` image, the same in both, and the comment above `jobs:` naming it with a date that `datetime.date.fromisoformat` reads, and each of 6 broken pairs fails it: either file back on `ubuntu-latest`, the two files on different images, the `ci.yml` comment dropped, the workflow's comment without its date, and the `ci.yml` comment with a date that does not exist. The run itself is checked on GitHub, not by the suite

### 12.14 Public evidence: the tau2-bench converter (`evidence/tau2-bench/convert.py`, v1.50)
Purpose: a third public outcome matrix in the 12.1 format, on an agentic task whose label comes from program checks and whose cost was recorded per conversation, the dataset `research/datasets.md` recommends. Standard library only (argparse, hashlib, json, math, re, sys, urllib, pathlib), so it needs no extra; the package never imports it
- Source, pinned: the GitHub repo `sierra-research/tau2-bench` at commit `REVISION = "033bf082f48e4a3c6a1256208e93cf2ce9c3bb6f"`, folder `data/tau2/results/final`, `LICENCE = "MIT"` (the repo's LICENSE file), `BASE_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sierra-research/tau2-bench/<REVISION>/data/tau2/results/final/"`. `FILES` maps each of the 12 default run files to its byte size and sha256, in ascending name order: the agents `claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219`, `gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`, `gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14` and `o4-mini-2025-04-16`, each in the domains `airline`, `retail` and `telecom`, named `<agent>_<domain>_<mode>_gpt-4.1-2025-04-14_4trials.json` with `<mode>` `base` for `gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14` and `default` for the others; 295,933,742 bytes in all, 7,599,444 to 51,038,687 each. The folder's other 14 run files (the telecom `op`, `no-user` and `no-user-op` ablations and the `telecom-workflow` domain, for two of the agents) are not read. The folder has no checksum file, so the pins in the script are the only trust root
- `default_cache_dir()` is `~/.cache/ai-model-routing-evidence/tau2-bench/` (from `Path.home()`), never inside the repo. `fetch(cache_dir=None) -> Path` creates the folder if needed and returns it after, for each file in `FILES` order: a present file is verified and kept, and one that fails `verify` is a `ValueError` naming it and is left in place; a missing one is downloaded with `urllib.request.urlopen` from `BASE_URL` plus its name into `<name>.part`, verified, then renamed, and the `.part` file is removed when the download or the check fails. Each download has a timeout of 60 seconds, and any error but a `ValueError` while downloading is raised as an `OSError` that names the file (an HTTP protocol error, such as an incomplete chunked read, is not an `OSError` and would otherwise escape the CLI's one-line error) (v1.51). `verify(path, size, sha256)` as in 12.6
- `model_id(llm) -> str`, a rule over the run's `llm` name, not a list: a name containing `/` is a `ValueError` (a routing or platform prefix is never guessed away); `claude-<rest>` becomes `anthropic/claude-<rest>`; `gpt-<rest>`, and a name that starts with `o` and a digit (`o4-mini-2025-04-16`), become `openai/<name>`; `<rest>` must be non-empty, and any other name, an empty string or a non-string is a `ValueError` rather than a guess
- `extract(run, trial=0, tokens=False) -> dict` reduces one parsed run file to what the matrix needs, so the converter holds one run file in memory at a time:
  - `ValueError`: first the arguments, a `trial` that is a bool, not an int or `< 0`, and a `tokens` that is not a bool; then a `run` that is not a dict or has no `info`, `tasks` or `simulations`; an `info` that is not a dict; an `info.environment_info.domain_name`, `info.agent_info.llm` or `info.user_info.llm` that is not a non-empty string (the message names the key path); a domain containing `|`; an agent or user name that `model_id` rejects, the agent first (these three in that order, so a missing user name is reported before a domain containing `|`, v1.51); `tasks` not a list, a task whose `id` is not a non-empty string, or a repeated task id; `simulations` not a list. Then each simulation in file order, so the first bad simulation decides the error: one that is not a dict, whose `task_id` is not a task id of the file, whose `trial` is a bool, not an int or `< 0`, or whose (`task_id`, `trial`) repeats an earlier one; and, only when its trial is the chosen one, a `reward_info.reward` that is a bool, not an int or float, or not exactly 0 or 1 (a partial reward is refused, never rounded), an `agent_cost` that is a bool, not an int or float, not finite (an int too large for a float counts as not finite, v1.51) or `< 0`, and with `tokens`, `messages` that is not a list, a message that is not a dict, or an agent message (role `assistant`) with no `usage` key (each of the 73,540 agent messages in the pinned files has one, 4,448 of them `null`, v1.51) or whose `usage` is neither `null` nor a dict whose `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens` are ints `>= 0` that are not bools. Last, a file in which no simulation has the chosen trial. A message about a simulation names the domain, the agent ID and the task id, and a reward or cost message also names its key (`reward`, `agent_cost`)
  - returns the keys `domain` (the `domain_name` as written), `agent` and `user` (each as `model_id` maps it), `trial`, `tokens`, `cells` and `missing_trial`, in that order. `cells` maps each task id that has a simulation at the chosen trial, ascending, to `{"correct": reward == 1, "cost_usd": float(agent_cost)}`, a negative zero written as `0.0`, and with `tokens` also `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`, the sums of `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens` over the agent messages whose `usage` is not `null`. `missing_trial` lists, ascending, the file's task ids with no simulation at that trial. User and tool messages are never counted, and with `tokens` False no message is read
- `convert(extracts, task_by="domain") -> tuple[list[dict], dict]` over a list of `extract` results:
  - `ValueError`, checked before any output: a `task_by` other than `domain` and `all`; `extracts` that is not a non-empty list or tuple; extracts that differ in `user`, `trial` or `tokens` (a matrix holds the user simulator and the trial fixed); two extracts for one (domain, agent); a domain with no extract for an agent that appears in another domain
  - an item is a task id of a domain, written `<domain>|<task id>` under either `task_by`, so `all` pools the same item IDs and 12.2 splits them the same way; it is kept only when every agent has a cell for it, and a task id that some agent lacks (any task id of that domain in a `cells` or `missing_trial`) is dropped and counted, never scored as a miss. The task is the domain, or `all` for every item with `task_by` `all`
  - one row per kept item and agent with the keys `task`, `item`, `model`, `correct`, `cost_usd` in that order, then `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` with `tokens`; rows sorted by (task, item, model) as strings; they form the complete matrix 12.3 requires, and the result does not depend on the order of the extracts or of the tasks and simulations in a run; the input is not modified
  - the report has the keys `models` (agent IDs ascending), `user_models` (a one-entry list), `trial`, `task_by`, `kept` and `dropped_incomplete` (each `{task: items}`, tasks ascending, every task in both, zeros included) and `rows` (the number of rows)
- `read_run(path)` reads one UTF-8 JSON file, and a file that is not valid UTF-8 or not valid JSON, or that nests deeper than the parser's recursion limit, is a `ValueError` naming it (v1.51); `write_jsonl(rows, path)` as in 12.6
- CLI: `python evidence/tau2-bench/convert.py --out PATH [--cache DIR] [--trial N] [--task-by {domain,all}] [--tokens]`, entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`. Options are checked first, so a bad one downloads nothing: `--trial` is decimal digits only (default 0), `--task-by` one of the two (default `domain`), and `--out` must end in a file name (an empty path, `.` or `/` is a bad option, v1.52 review). Then it fetches, reads and extracts the files one at a time in `FILES` order, converts, writes `<out>.part`, prints the report as `json.dumps(report, indent=2)`, flushing stdout, and only then renames it onto `--out` (v1.94, as 13.11), so a write to stdout that fails (a closed pipe) is an I/O error that leaves `--out` as it was. It returns 0 on success and 2 with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr (a carriage return or line feed in the message written as `\r` or `\n`) and nothing on stdout for a missing or bad option, a size or hash mismatch, invalid data or an I/O error, but for a rename that fails after the print, the one failure with the report already on stdout; on any failure `--out` is not written (an existing one is left as it was) and `<out>.part` is removed
- Choices (v1.50):
  - one trial per task: each run holds 4 trials of every task and 12.1 takes one row per (task, item, model). With each trial as its own item, the 12.2 split, which hashes the item ID, would put trials of one task in both halves, so the held-out half would score tasks the calibration half has seen. So one trial is kept, trial 0 by default and the others as a stated sensitivity. In all 12 pinned files trial t ran with the same seed for every agent (626729, 373753, 361454 and 1567 for trials 0 to 3), so a row compares the agents under one simulated-user seed, though the user model still answers each agent's own messages
  - cost: `cost_usd` is the run's recorded `agent_cost`, the agent model's calls in that conversation. On the pinned files it equals the `math.fsum` of the agent messages' `cost` values within 4.5e-16, and the files do not name the price table behind it. None of the 4 agents is in `models.json`, so pricing the token counts would need a dated price list the repo does not hold; `--tokens` adds the summed counts for a later delegation run or a cross-check. `user_cost`, the user simulator's calls, is never counted: it is the benchmark's cost, not the routed call's
  - label: only the number `reward_info.reward` is read, the result of the run's program checks (research/datasets.md lists the reward bases). 36 of the 4,448 simulations in the 12 files ended at `max_steps` rather than `user_stop`, and their reward is used as recorded
  - tasks: one routing task per domain by default. Airline has 50 tasks, and the 12.2 split draws each item's half from its hash, so the airline calibration half holds 16 to 35 items over seeds 0 to 49 and fewer than the default `min_samples` of 30 (5.3) on 40 of those 50 seeds (7 of seeds 0 to 9); on such a seed no model has enough calibration items to clear any bar there at that default, while on the other 10 a model can clear a low enough bar. Retail and telecom hold 45 to 65 and 44 to 68. `--task-by all` pools the 278 items into one task (114 to 158). The results slice states which matrix it commits, with its split sizes and bars
  - regeneration: when this was written the 12 files downloaded in about 6 seconds and converted in about 1 second, with no extra, so the results slice adds the converter to the 12.13 workflow rather than leaving it local like RouterBench
- Default options on the pinned files (checked with a private prototype of this contract when this section was written; the cached-file test pins them): 4 models, the user model `openai/gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`, kept airline 50, retail 114 and telecom 114 items, none dropped, 1,112 rows, and the same counts at every trial from 0 to 3. At trial 0 the agents' success rates per domain span 0.5 to 0.58 (airline), 0.684 to 0.798 (retail) and 0.368 to 0.439 (telecom); `cost_usd` spans 0.00136 to 6.446604; with `--tokens`, `input_tokens` spans 6,629 to 2,096,093 and `output_tokens` 123 to 26,531. Over all 4 trials the `agent_cost` values sum to 487.623954 for `claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219` and 29.755562 for `gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14`, the "Sum cost per LLM" values of the folder's `figs/cost_info.txt` (whose sums for the other two agents also count their ablation runs)
- v1.76, per-call rows for the delegation harness (13.9 v1.74): a mode that writes each conversation's work calls, so a tau2-bench item can be priced on its own calls:
  - `extract(run, trial=0, tokens=False, calls=False)`: `calls` is checked after `tokens`, a `ValueError` when it is not a bool (`calls ... is not a bool`), and `calls` True with `tokens` False is a `ValueError` whose message starts `calls needs tokens`, since a per-call row carries the summed counts too. Every extract gains the key `calls` after `tokens` (the v1.50 key list moves once). With `calls`, each cell gains `call_input_tokens` and `call_output_tokens` after `output_tokens`: over the agent messages whose `usage` is not `null`, in message order, with p_j and c_j the `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens` of the j-th of them and K their number, `call_output_tokens` is c_1 to c_K and `call_input_tokens` is p_1, then p_j - p_{j-1} - c_{j-1} for j from 2 to K, the 13.9 a_j if every prompt re-sends the whole history. An entry of `call_input_tokens` may be negative: the extract records what the usage says, and `convert` applies the 13.9 rule; `call_output_tokens` entries are the completion counts, which the v1.50 usage check already holds to ints `>= 0`, so only a new input can be negative. Because p_j = a_1 + ... + a_j + c_1 + ... + c_{j-1} whatever the signs, `input_tokens` = p_1 + ... + p_K is the 13.9 Rd of the two lists and `output_tokens` is their O. A simulation at the chosen trial with no such message is a `ValueError` whose message, like the other simulation messages, names the domain, the agent ID and the task id (as a quoted repr) and contains `has no model call` (13.9 needs non-empty lists); the pinned files have none at any trial from 0 to 3. Without `calls` the cells, and with `tokens` False the messages, are read as before
  - `convert`: the check that the extracts agree adds `calls` after `tokens`, with the label `calls setting`. With `calls`, after the v1.50 checks, an agent any of whose cells, in any of its extracts, has a negative `call_input_tokens` entry is left out of the matrix with all its rows, the 13.9 rule for completion counts that include reasoning a later call does not re-read; `models` lists the other agents, and an item is kept when each of them has a cell for it, so the kept and dropped counts read those agents only; when every agent is left out, a `ValueError` whose message starts `every agent has a call whose new input is negative`. The rows of the kept agents gain `call_input_tokens` and `call_output_tokens` after `output_tokens`, as copies of the cell's lists (new list objects, so changing a row never changes an extract), and the report gains `left_out` as its last key, `{agent: the number of negative entries over its cells}` with agents ascending, `{}` when none is left out. Without `calls`, the rows and the report are the v1.50 ones bit for bit, with no `left_out` key
  - CLI: `--calls` adds the per-call lists and implies `--tokens`, and otherwise `main` behaves as above: it returns 0 and prints the report, now with `left_out`, on success, and 2 with one `error:` line on a failure; the usage line becomes `python evidence/tau2-bench/convert.py --out PATH [--cache DIR] [--trial N] [--task-by {domain,all}] [--tokens] [--calls]`. Every 13.9 row check then passes on the written rows, and `evaluate_delegation` reads them with `expected_work_calls` on each cell
  - On the pinned files at trial 0 (checked with a private prototype of this contract when this was written; the cached-file test pins them): `--calls` leaves out `openai/o4-mini-2025-04-16`, with 2,962 negative entries over its 3,849 calls after a first one (`research/delegation-datasets.md`); keeps `anthropic/claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219`, `openai/gpt-4.1-2025-04-14` and `openai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14` on the 50 airline, 114 retail and 114 telecom items, none dropped, 834 rows, whose `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` equal those of the `--tokens` rows. K runs from 2 to 100 calls (median 14): 5 to 100 (median 15) for the Anthropic agent, 2 to 99 (12) and 3 to 100 (14) for the two GPT-4.1 agents; the first call's read from 3,252 to 8,245 tokens; the largest last call reads 33,765 tokens. At trials 1 and 2 `openai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14` also has 2 and 1 negative entries, so `--calls` keeps 2 agents and 556 rows there, and at trial 3 it keeps 3 agents and 834 rows (o4-mini 2,880), so a trial sensitivity of the delegation results compares 3 agents at trials 0 and 3 and 2 at trials 1 and 2. The written file is 341,633 bytes, so, like the 12.14 matrix, it is never committed: a results slice commits what the harness computes from it
  - Evidence README (v1.77): one sentence on `--calls` with the left-out agent and the trial-0 counts above, on a line of its own, checked by `tests/test_evidence_tau2.py`, whose cached-file test builds those counts (the left-out agent and its negative entries, the kept agents and rows, and the minimum, median and maximum call count) from the report and rows on the pinned files
  - Test status: `tests/test_evidence_tau2_calls.py` is committed before the code, each test of the new behaviour under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=...)` naming what the v1.75 converter raises: `TypeError` where a test calls `extract` with `calls` (an unexpected keyword), and `AssertionError` where it runs `main` with `--calls` (the v1.75 parser refuses the option, so `main` returns 2) or converts hand-made extracts that carry `calls` (v1.75 drops the lists and writes no `left_out`, and an expected error is asserted through a helper that raises `AssertionError` when none comes). Next to them, plain tests pin that p_1 + ... + p_K equals the Rd of the difference lists on random conversations, that `convert` without `calls` leaves hand-made extracts' rows and report as v1.50 writes them, and, when the 12 pinned files are cached, the SHA-256 of the default and of the `--tokens` matrix as v1.75 writes them. The implementing commit (v1.77) removed the markers and moved `EXTRACT_KEYS` in `tests/test_evidence_tau2.py`, so the 22 tests are plain tests
- Reading it: the 4 agents date from 2025; the airline and retail tasks are the June 2025 task sets, which the folder README says differ from the current base tasks ("airline: 29 tasks differ, retail: 114"); a model (`gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`) plays the user in every conversation, so a reward is the agent's success with that simulated user; the costs are those the runs recorded at the prices of their time
- Evidence README (v1.51): `evidence/tau2-bench/README.md` says what the folder holds and, from v1.53, links its results, `RESULTS.md` and `results.json` (12.15), where v1.51 said they were not committed yet; gives the source link, the revision, the licence with the copyright line of the repo's LICENSE file (`Copyright (c) 2025 Sierra Research`), a table of the 12 pinned files with bytes and sha256, what counts as a success and as the cost, the Reading it caveats above with the folder README's words `airline: 29 tasks differ, retail: 114`, the regenerate line, and the default counts above. `tests/test_evidence_tau2.py` checks it for those strings, and the cached-file test builds its counts from the report on the pinned files
- Test status: `tests/test_evidence_tau2.py` was committed before the script, under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=ModuleNotFoundError)`, which the implementing commit (v1.52) removed, so its 142 tests are plain tests. Every test uses small synthetic run dicts shaped like a run file (top keys `timestamp`, `info`, `tasks`, `simulations`; simulation keys `id`, `task_id`, `trial`, `seed`, `termination_reason`, `agent_cost`, `user_cost`, `reward_info`, `messages`), except one that reads the pinned files only when all 12 are in the default cache and skips otherwise (CI never downloads them)

### 12.15 tau2-bench results (`evidence/tau2-bench/results.json`, v1.53)
Purpose: the evaluator's numbers on the 12.14 matrix in the shape of 12.7 and 12.10, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the tables and the numbers RESULTS.md states checked against the committed file or, for the split sizes, the success span and the sensitivity runs, against the pinned files when they are cached
- Script: `evidence/routerbench/results.py` (12.7) is reused unchanged, as in 12.10
- Matrix: the converter's default, trial 0 with one routing task per domain (`airline` 50 items, `retail` 114, `telecom` 114, 4 agents). A task in this library is one kind of job, and the three domains are three kinds, so routing per domain is what the library would do with these runs; pooling them (`--task-by all`) is a sensitivity run. At the default `min_samples` 30 the airline calibration half is too small on 40 of the 50 seeds (12.14), and on those seeds the router falls back to the best available model there (`on_no_model="best_available"`, 12.3); RESULTS.md says so with the split sizes
- Bars: `0.3 0.4 0.5 0.6 0.7 0.8`, every tenth from the one below the lowest per-domain agent success rate at trial 0 to the one above the highest (0.368 and 0.798, 12.14), so the table runs from bars that most (domain, seed) cells clear to one that none clears. The bars were chosen after a first run at the 0.6 to 0.9 of 12.7 and 12.10, which left two bars, 0.8 and 0.9, at which no cell clears, and said nothing about the bars below 0.6. 50 seeds, the default `min_samples` 30 and confidence 0.95
- Committed: `evidence/tau2-bench/results.json`, from that matrix and `model-routing evaluate --bar 0.3 0.4 0.5 0.6 0.7 0.8 --seeds 50 --json` reduced by `results.py --evaluate`, and `evidence/tau2-bench/RESULTS.md` with the source link, the revision, the licence and its copyright line, links to the evidence README and results.json, the five-line regenerate block, the `table` output in a fenced `text` block whose first line is `$ python evidence/routerbench/results.py --table evidence/tau2-bench/results.json`, the 12.14 Reading it caveats, and the numbers below
- Single models: RESULTS.md states from the committed summary whether any routed (bar, flavor) row beats a single agent's row of the first (bar, flavor) on both counts (held-out success at least as high and cost at most as high, at full precision, one of the two strictly), naming each agent beaten and the rows that beat it, or saying that none is. The test builds that sentence from the file
