## 13. Delegation: inline or sub-agent (`delegation.py`, v1.4; cost accounting v1.8)
Purpose: an agent (the **parent**: a model already running with its own context) reaches a piece of work that is one call of a task, or from v1.38 K successive calls of it (13.1). It can do the work itself (**inline**) or hand it to a fresh sub-agent running the model the router picks (**delegate**). The decision weighs what routing weighs, the measured success rate against the task's bar and list-price cost, plus what each path does to the parent's context. The delegate is chosen by `Router.select`, so the 5.2 rules, the Wilson admission rule and both flavors apply unchanged.

### 13.1 Inputs
`Router.decide_delegation(task_name, parent_model, flavor="cost_effective", *, context_tokens, brief_tokens, result_tokens, carry_turns=0, work_calls=1, inline_separate_call=True, cached_read_ratio=1.0, generator_model=None, exclude_models=()) -> DelegationDecision`
- `parent_model`: the parent's model ID. An unknown task, parent or `generator_model` raises `KeyError`; an unknown flavor raises `ValueError` (5.1)
- `context_tokens` (C): tokens already in the parent's context
- The work is one call of the task unless `work_calls` says more (v1.38): each call reads S_in = `task.expected_input_tokens` and writes S_out = `task.expected_output_tokens`; from v1.33 the cost of each path uses the 5.2 sizes of the model doing the work (13.2)
- `work_calls` (K, v1.38, default 1): the work is K successive calls of the task on one path. Each call takes S_in of new input and writes S_out, and call j (j = 1 to K) reads its path's base context (C inline, B delegated) plus the j inputs and the j - 1 outputs so far, so the path's context grows by S_in + S_out per call. K must be an integer `>= 1` that is not a bool, else `ValueError` naming `work_calls`, checked after `carry_turns`
- The bar and the bounds stay per call whatever K: K changes the cost of each path (13.2), the 13.4 context check and the delegate's `select` (13.3), never a success count, a bound or a bar check, so the 13.5 rows read the same bounds. `DelegationDecision` (13.6) has no field for K
- `brief_tokens` (B): what the parent writes to hand the work off. `result_tokens` (R): what the parent reads back
- `carry_turns` (T): parent calls after the parent's next call (13.2) that still re-read what this work leaves in its context. The next call itself is always charged, so T = 0 means the parent makes one more call after the work and stops. When the work is one of several sibling subtasks that the parent would otherwise do one after another, each later sibling done inline re-reads this one, so those calls count in T
- `inline_separate_call`: True (the default) when inline work takes a parent call of its own, False when the parent folds the work into its next call (13.2). It must be a bool, else `ValueError`
- `cached_read_ratio` (rho, v1.41, default 1.0): the price of the parent's cached re-reads of its existing context C, as a fraction of the parent's list input price, for a parent whose provider caches the prompt prefix it has already read; 13.2 says which reads it prices. It must be an `int` or a `float` that is not a bool, with 0 <= rho <= 1 (so NaN and the infinities fail), else `ValueError` naming `cached_read_ratio`, checked after `inline_separate_call`. It moves `inline_cost_usd` only: never `delegate_cost_usd`, the delegate's `select` (13.3), the 13.4 checks, a bound or a bar check. `DelegationDecision` (13.6) has no field for it
- `generator_model` and `exclude_models` mean what they mean in 5.2 and are passed to the delegate's `select`. `exclude_models` limits the delegate only; it never stops the parent working inline
- `context_tokens`, `brief_tokens`, `result_tokens` and `carry_turns` must be integers `>= 0` that are not bools, else `ValueError`
- The router is not modified: `router.tasks` is the same after the call

### 13.2 Cost of each path (list price, reported in both flavors)
The accounting follows the parent's calls, each of which re-reads the parent's whole context. Both paths are charged for the same calls, and each only for what that path adds to them:
- The **decision turn**, the parent call that makes this decision, happens on both paths. On the delegate path it also writes the brief, so B is charged once as parent output; on the inline path it writes nothing extra. Fetching the work's input is free on both paths; reading it is charged on both
- The parent's **next call** after the work is done (on the delegate path, the call that reads the result) and the T calls after it happen on both paths and re-read C on both, so C is not charged in them. Each of these 1 + T calls is charged for the tokens that the path left in the parent's context. What a call writes besides the work, the parent's own next step, is the same on both paths and is not charged
- The work itself: the delegate reads the brief and the work's input in a fresh context and writes the output. Inline, the parent does it either in a call of its own over its whole context (`inline_separate_call=True`) or inside its next call (`inline_separate_call=False`), which then also reads S_in and writes S_out. A separate call fits work that is a step of its own, for example output that goes into a tool call or a file before the parent goes on; folding fits work the parent would do as part of its very next reply anyway. The default, a separate call, gives the higher inline cost

With P the parent's model and D the delegate, for work of one call (K = 1; the v1.38 bullet below gives K calls):
- `delegate_cost_usd = P.cost(0, B) + D.cost(B + S_in, S_out) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`: the brief written, the delegate's call, then the brief and the result re-read by the next call and the T after it
- `inline_cost_usd = P.cost(C + S_in, S_out) + (1 + T) * P.cost(S_in + S_out, 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is True: the parent's own call for the work, then its input and output re-read by the next call and the T after it
- `inline_cost_usd = P.cost(S_in, S_out) + T * P.cost(S_in + S_out, 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is False: the next call also reads S_in and writes S_out, and both are re-read by the T calls after it
- With K = 1, C appears only in the separate inline call. With a separate call, inline minus delegating to the parent's own model (D = P) is, in exact arithmetic, `P.cost(C, 0) - P.cost(B, B) + (1 + T) * (P.cost(S_in + S_out, 0) - P.cost(B + R, 0))`, so delegating even to its own model can save in two ways: the separate call's re-read of a large context C (the context-isolation case), and later calls that re-read a short brief and result instead of the work's input and output. When the work folds into the next call, C cancels, and delegating to the parent's own model is cheaper only when `T * P.cost(S_in + S_out, 0)` exceeds `P.cost(B, B) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`, so never with T = 0
- Each expression is evaluated left to right as written, in floating point, from `Model.cost` (4.1); `1 + T` is an integer. `delegate_cost_usd` is `None` when there is no delegate
- v1.33: S_in and S_out in these expressions are the 5.2 sizes of the model doing the work on that path. In `inline_cost_usd` they are the parent's, S_in(P) and S_out(P), in its call for the work and in the later re-reads of that input and output; in `delegate_cost_usd` they are the delegate's, in `D.cost(B + S_in(D), S_out(D))`. For a model without measured token counts they are the task's expected tokens, so with no token totals each expression is the v1.32 one. With D = P both paths use the same sizes, so the D = P difference above holds with S_in(P) and S_out(P). The 12.3 evaluator builds its stats from counts alone, so its results do not move; from v1.35 the 13.9 harness adds each model's calibration-half token totals to its stats, so its decisions price each path at these per-model sizes (13.9)
- v1.38, K work calls (13.1). Write w_in = K(K + 1) / 2 and w_out = K(K - 1) / 2, both integers. The K calls of a path with base context X read `K * X + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out` tokens in all and write `K * S_out`, and each path prices its K calls as one `Model.cost` of those integer totals, which is the sum of the K calls' costs in exact arithmetic since `Model.cost` is linear (4.1). S_in and S_out are the sizes of the model doing the work, as in v1.33. With P the parent and D the delegate:
  - `delegate_cost_usd = P.cost(0, B) + D.cost(K * B + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`: the brief written once, the delegate's K calls, then the brief and the result re-read by the next call and the T after it
  - `inline_cost_usd = P.cost(K * C + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out) + (1 + T) * P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is True: K parent calls of the work's own, each over the whole context, then the K inputs and outputs re-read by the next call and the T after it
  - `inline_cost_usd = P.cost((K - 1) * C + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out) + T * P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is False: the last of the K calls is the parent's next call, which re-reads C on both paths, so C is charged only in the K - 1 calls before it. Folding takes the last work call into the next call, never all K
  - With K = 1, w_in = 1 and w_out = 0, so the token totals are the integers of the three expressions above and every cost, reason and check is the v1.37 one bit for bit
  - With a separate call, inline minus delegating to the parent's own model is, in exact arithmetic, `P.cost(K * C, 0) - P.cost(K * B, B) + (1 + T) * (P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0) - P.cost(B + R, 0))`: each inline call re-reads C where each delegated one re-reads B, which is where context isolation pays most. Folded it is `P.cost((K - 1) * C, 0) - P.cost(K * B, B) + T * P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0) - (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`, so from K = 2 a large C can make delegating to its own model cheaper even with T = 0
- v1.41, cached context reads (13.1). The reads rho prices are the parent's reads of C in the inline work calls: the K calls of its own when `inline_separate_call` is True, the K - 1 calls before its next call when it is False. They are the only reads of C that 13.2 charges (the decision turn, the next call and the T calls after it read C on both paths and are not charged), and each of them finds C as the prefix of its prompt, just read by the decision turn. Those K * C or (K - 1) * C tokens are priced at rho times the parent's list input price; everything else stays at list price on both paths: the work's input and output, their re-reads by later work calls and by the 1 + T later parent calls, the brief, the result, every read of the delegate, and all output. With P the parent:
  - `inline_cost_usd = P.cost(K * C * rho + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out) + (1 + T) * P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is True
  - `inline_cost_usd = P.cost((K - 1) * C * rho + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out) + T * P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is False
  - `delegate_cost_usd` is the v1.38 expression whatever rho, and so is the delegate
  - The first argument of `P.cost` is evaluated left to right: the integer `K * C` or `(K - 1) * C`, times rho, then the integer terms added, so with a float rho it is a float. With rho = 1.0 its value is the v1.38 integer total, and `Model.cost` converts an int to that same float before it multiplies by a float price, so every cost, reason and check is the v1.40 one bit for bit (while the token totals and their products with the prices stay below 2^53, about 9e15); with the int rho = 1 the argument is the v1.38 integer itself. Folded work of one call has no C term, so its cost is the same for every rho
  - In exact arithmetic rho scales the C term of each inline minus delegating difference in the v1.38 bullet. With a separate call it is `P.cost(K * C * rho, 0) - P.cost(K * B, B) + (1 + T) * (P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0) - P.cost(B + R, 0))`, so at rho = 0 the parent's context no longer favours delegating to its own model, and only later calls that re-read a short brief and result instead of the work can; folded, the C term is `P.cost((K - 1) * C * rho, 0)`
  - Not modelled: a surcharge for writing the cache, a cache that expires between calls, a discount on any other re-read on either path, and a cached price for the delegate. rho is one number the caller states for the parent; the AutoBench pool (13.9) has no cached prices, so evidence with rho < 1 states it as an assumption
- This is still an approximation. It assumes that each of the K work calls reads its path's base context and every earlier work call in full, with nothing summarised between calls (13.8), no prompt caching beyond the v1.41 ratio on the parent's reads of C, the same parent output after the work on both paths (checking a delegate's result may take more), and that the parent's later calls fit its context on both paths: C + K * (S_in + S_out) is checked for inline (13.4) but C + B + R is not checked for delegate

### 13.3 The delegate
- The delegate is the primary of `Router.select(task_name, flavor, generator_model, exclude_models)` run with the same registry, stats and priors on a copy of the task whose `expected_input_tokens` is `S_in + B`, so the context rule (5.2 rule 5) and the `cost_effective` ranking see the brief the delegate actually reads. It may be the parent's own model
- `route` is that `RouteDecision`, or `None` when that `select` raises `NoModelClearsBar`. `delegate_quality_lb` is the `quality_lb` of the route's primary candidate. The delegate clears the bar when `route.below_bar` is False
- The parent's share of `delegate_cost_usd` is the same for every delegate, so the `cost_effective` primary of this `select` is also the passing delegate with the lowest `delegate_cost_usd`
- v1.33: in that `select` every model's input size is its 5.2 input size plus B, so a model with measured token counts is priced at `model.cost(mean input + B, mean output)` and any other at `model.cost(S_in + B, S_out)`, which keeps the bullet above true; rule 5 still checks the copy's expected tokens, S_in + B + S_out. One way is a private copy of the stats with B times `token_trials` added to each input total, since rounding up `(I + B * n) / n` gives the rounded-up `I / n` plus B exactly. `router.stats` and `router.tasks` are the same after the call
- v1.38, K work calls: in that `select`, rule 5 checks each model's window against the delegate's last call, `B + K * (S_in + S_out)` with the task's expected sizes (that call reads B + K * S_in + (K - 1) * S_out and writes S_out), not against the K calls' total, and each candidate's `list_cost`, and its `routing_cost` in `cost_effective`, is the cost of its K calls, `model.cost(K * B + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out)` with its 5.2 sizes (13.2), so the `cost_effective` primary is still the passing delegate with the lowest `delegate_cost_usd`, and the 13.7 exit-3 table prints that cost. Pricing a model at its last call alone would rank by the wrong mix of input and output price, since the K calls' input grows as K squared and their output as K. With K = 1 this is the v1.34 `select`, run as v1.34 runs it. One way for K > 1 is a private `Router` subclass whose candidates are checked and priced this way; `router.stats` and `router.tasks` are the same after the call

### 13.4 Is inline allowed?
The first rule that applies blocks inline (the parent must delegate) and becomes the reason:
1. the parent's family is in `task.exclude_families`: `parent_excluded_family`
2. `task.independent_of_generator`, `generator_model` is given, and the parent is that model or in its family: `parent_not_independent` (the parent cannot check its own family's output)
3. `P.context_window < C + K * (S_in + S_out)`, with the task's expected S_in and S_out whatever the parent's measured sizes (v1.33, as 5.2 rule 5): `parent_context_full`. The last of the K work calls (v1.38) reads C + K * S_in + (K - 1) * S_out and writes S_out, folded or not; K = 1 gives the v1.37 check C + S_in + S_out

The parent clears the bar when its measured trials on the task are at least `task.min_samples` and `parent_quality_lb = wilson_lower_bound(successes, trials, task.confidence)` is at least `task.bar`: the admission test of 5.2 rules 6 and 7. `parent_quality_lb` is 0.0 when the parent is unmeasured; the parent is measured when it has `trials > 0`, as in 5.3

### 13.5 Choosing (the first row that applies wins)
1. Inline blocked: delegate, with the blocking reason and `below_bar = route.below_bar`. With no route, raise `NoModelClearsBar`
2. The delegate clears the bar and the parent does not: delegate, `parent_below_bar`
3. Both clear, `cost_effective`: delegate when `delegate_cost_usd < inline_cost_usd` (`delegate_cheaper`); inline when it is greater (`inline_cheaper`); on an exact tie, delegate when `delegate_quality_lb > parent_quality_lb` (`delegate_better`), else inline (`inline_as_good`)
4. Both clear, `cost_blind`: delegate when `delegate_quality_lb > parent_quality_lb` (`delegate_better`), else inline (`inline_as_good`). Cost is not compared
5. Only the parent clears: inline, `only_parent_clears`
6. Neither clears and `task.on_no_model` is `"refuse"`: raise `NoModelClearsBar`
7. Neither clears and `task.on_no_model` is `"best_available"`: reason `none_clear`, `below_bar` True. Delegate when `route is not None and route.basis == "measured" and (parent unmeasured or delegate_quality_lb > parent_quality_lb)`; else, in `cost_effective` (v1.31), delegate when `route is not None and route.basis == "measured"`, the parent is measured, `delegate_quality_lb == parent_quality_lb` and `delegate_cost_usd < inline_cost_usd`; else inline when the parent is measured; else delegate when there is a route (its basis is `prior`); else raise `NoModelClearsBar`
- `below_bar` is False in rows 2 to 5. A raised `NoModelClearsBar` carries the `.candidates` of the failed `select`
- Costs and bounds are compared with plain `<`, `>` and `==` on the floats as computed, never with a tolerance, so two costs that differ only by rounding are not a tie
- Equal-quality ties go inline in `cost_blind` (rows 4 and 7) and, in `cost_effective`, on an exact cost tie (an exact cost tie in row 3 is settled by the bounds first), because a hand-off has costs the model does not price: a brief can drop detail, and the parent has to trust a result it did not produce. In `cost_effective` a strictly lower `delegate_cost_usd` delegates in row 3 whatever the bounds, and in row 7 when the bounds are equal (v1.31)
- A model below the bar is chosen only under `best_available`, in row 1 or row 7, and then `below_bar` is True
- v1.31: row 7 orders the two paths as 5.3 orders the `best_available` fallback, by bound first and then, in `cost_effective` only, by the path's list-price cost (13.2), so a measured delegate whose bound equals a measured parent's and whose `delegate_cost_usd` is strictly lower is taken. The usual case is the parent's own model as the delegate (its bound is the parent's, from the same stats), where delegating saves the separate inline call's re-read of C (13.2). The costs are compared as computed (no tolerance, as above), so a delegate path that is cheaper only by rounding is taken. With a measured parent, `cost_blind`, an exact cost tie, a lower delegate bound and a `prior` route stay inline; an unmeasured parent still delegates to any route, as before. The reason is `none_clear` in every branch of row 7, so no reason code is added (13.6)
- Row 5 has no equal-model case: a parent that clears in 13.4 reaches 5.2 rule 8 (`passes`) in the delegate's `select` unless rules 1 to 5 stop it, since rules 6 and 7 read the same stats, bar, confidence and `min_samples`, and one passing candidate makes the route clear the bar. So when the route is below the bar, the parent was stopped there by rule 1 (it is in `exclude_models`) or rule 5 (its window holds C + S_in + S_out but not S_in + B + S_out); rules 2 to 4 are the 13.4 blocks that send the decision to row 1. Row 5 stays inline in both flavors, since its delegate does not clear the bar and the parent does; that includes a different delegate whose bound equals or exceeds the parent's but that has too few trials (5.2 rule 6)

### 13.6 `DelegationDecision`
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class DelegationDecision`, fields in this order: `task: str`, `flavor: str`, `parent: str`, `mode: str` (`"inline"` or `"delegate"`), `model: str` (the parent for inline, `route.primary` for delegate), `reason: str`, `below_bar: bool`, `inline_allowed: bool`, `inline_cost_usd: float`, `delegate_cost_usd: float | None`, `parent_quality_lb: float`, `delegate_quality_lb: float | None`, `route: RouteDecision | None`
- `.to_dict()` returns plain JSON-able types, keys in the field order above, with `route` as `route.to_dict()` or `None`
- Module constants with these exact values: `INLINE = "inline"`, `DELEGATE = "delegate"`, and reason codes whose values are their names in lower case: `PARENT_EXCLUDED_FAMILY`, `PARENT_NOT_INDEPENDENT`, `PARENT_CONTEXT_FULL`, `PARENT_BELOW_BAR`, `DELEGATE_CHEAPER`, `INLINE_CHEAPER`, `DELEGATE_BETTER`, `INLINE_AS_GOOD`, `ONLY_PARENT_CLEARS`, `NONE_CLEAR`
- `DelegationDecision` is re-exported from `model_routing` and listed in `__all__`; the constants stay in `model_routing.delegation`
- v1.11: `Executor.call_delegated(decision, messages, ...)` runs a `delegate` decision on its route, with fallback and logging as for any call (7)
- v1.38: the fields, `to_dict()` and the constants are unchanged; K is an input only (13.1)
- v1.41: the fields, `to_dict()` and the constants are unchanged; rho is an input only (13.1)

### 13.7 CLI
- `model-routing delegate TASK --parent MODEL --context-tokens N --brief-tokens N --result-tokens N [--carry-turns N] [--work-calls N] [--fold-inline] [--cached-read-ratio X] [--flavor cost_effective|cost_blind] [--generator MODEL] [--exclude MODEL ...] [--registry PATH] [--tasks PATH] [--stats PATH] [--priors PATH] [--json]`, with the defaults of section 10; `--fold-inline` passes `inline_separate_call=False`; `--work-calls` (v1.38, default 1) passes `work_calls`; `--cached-read-ratio` (v1.41, default 1.0) passes `cached_read_ratio` as a float
- Prints exactly eight lines, one per key in this order, the key, a colon, spaces, then the value: `mode`, `model`, `reason`, `below_bar` (`true` or `false`), `inline_cost_usd` and `delegate_cost_usd` (6 decimals), `parent_quality_lb` and `delegate_quality_lb` (3 decimals); a `None` value prints as `-`. `--json` prints `DelegationDecision.to_dict()`
- Exit codes: 0 success; 2 bad input (as for `select`, plus a `--parent` that is not in the registry, a token option that is not an integer `>= 0`, from v1.38, a `--work-calls` that is not an integer `>= 1` and, from v1.41, a `--cached-read-ratio` that is not a number in [0, 1], `nan` and `inf` included), including a missing required option, with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr and it is the last line (an argument-parsing error prints the usage first, as for `select`); 3 `NoModelClearsBar`, with the candidate table on stderr as for `select`
- v1.15: after the eight lines, the `--json` output or the exit-3 table, the price expiry warnings of section 10

### 13.8 Limits and test status
- From v1.38 the work may be K calls on either path (13.1, 13.2). Not modelled (14): prompt caching beyond the v1.41 ratio on the parent's reads of C (13.2), a parent or sub-agent that summarises between calls, whether the K calls succeed together (the bar is per call), and the wall-clock gain of running sibling sub-agents at the same time. The committed evidence (13.10, v1.73) has two multi-call points, variant points 0 and 2 (two and 15 folded calls at 64,000 context tokens), beside the one cached point, variant point 1
- The contract tests (`tests/test_delegation.py`) were committed before `delegation.py` exists, under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=ModuleNotFoundError)`, as in 12.5. The implementing commit removes that marker
- v1.9: the README shows two `delegate` transcripts on the demo's output (11), one where delegating is cheaper and one where inline is, checked like every README transcript (12.5)
- v1.12: the demo asks the first transcript's question in both flavors and runs the delegated hand-off through `Executor.call_delegated` (11), so its `calls.jsonl` holds records tagged `delegated` and `parent`
- v1.31: the row 7 equal-bound tests that need the new branch are committed ahead of the code under `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)`, next to passing tests of their fixtures and of the cases that stay inline; the implementing commit removes the markers and regenerates `evidence/delegation/results.json`, which the change moves (13.10). v1.32 is that commit
- v1.38: the multi-call tests (`tests/test_work_calls.py`, a file of their own as v1.33 had) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` that names what the v1.37 code raises: `TypeError` where a test passes `work_calls` to `decide_delegation`, whose v1.37 signature has no such keyword, and `AssertionError` where a CLI test passes `--work-calls`, on which v1.37 exits 2 as an unknown option. Next to them, plain tests pin that the v1.38 expressions with K = 1 give the v1.37 costs bit for bit on the existing fixtures and that the closed-form token totals are the sums over the calls. The implementing commit removes the markers, adds a README `delegate` transcript with `--work-calls`, and updates README Limitations (14 was updated with the spec in v1.38); v1.39 is that commit. The 13.10 `results.json` keeps K = 1; from v1.43 the 13.9 harness takes K per point (code in v1.44) (13.9), and from v1.45 a 13.10 point with K > 1 is a variant point, in `variants.json` (13.10), whose first one has K = 2. v1.48: README Limitations says the committed evidence has one multi-call point, variant point 0 of `variants.json` (two folded work calls at 64,000 context tokens), since no point of `results.json` carries `work_calls`
- v1.41: the cached-read tests (`tests/test_cached_reads.py`, a file of their own as v1.38 had) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` that names what the v1.40 code raises: `TypeError` where a test passes `cached_read_ratio` to `decide_delegation`, whose v1.40 signature has no such keyword, and `AssertionError` where a CLI test passes `--cached-read-ratio`, on which v1.40 exits 2 as an unknown option. Next to them, plain tests pin that the v1.41 expressions with rho = 1.0 and with rho = 1 give the v1.40 costs bit for bit on the existing fixtures. The implementing commit removes the markers and updates README Limitations (14 is updated with this spec); v1.42 is that commit, and it also adds a README `delegate` transcript with `--cached-read-ratio`. The 13.9 harness and the 13.10 results keep rho = 1.0; a 13.10 point with rho < 1 is a later version and, from v1.45, a variant point (13.10); v1.57 specifies it as variant point 1 (13.9, 13.10)
- v1.33: the measured-token tests (`tests/test_measured_tokens.py`) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` that names what the v1.32 code raises: `TypeError` where a test passes the new `Stats.add` keywords, `AttributeError` where it reads `Stats.tokens` or `Stats.mean_tokens`, `AssertionError` where v1.32 ignores the new keys of a stats file or a result row. Next to them, plain tests pin that a Stats with no token totals prices, routes, delegates and saves as v1.32 does. The implementing commit removes the markers and says in the README how each model's cost is estimated
- v1.86, when neither path clears: [`research/no-path-clears.md`](research/no-path-clears.md) measures 13.5 row 7 (`none_clear`) on the three committed delegation runs (13.10 `results.json`, 13.12 `results.json` and `pooled.json`) and recommends keeping the row as it is, so 13.5 does not change. Its numbers are the output of `research/no_path_clears.py`, standard library and the `model_routing` package, with no library code change: it reruns each run in process at its script's bars, base points and 50 seeds, keeping the cells its results file drops, and prints, per run and flavor, the row 7 share and, on those cells, the held-out success (correct over held-out items, pooled over the cells), the `math.fsum` cost per held-out item and the number of cells whose held-out success is at the bar or above, for the decision, always inline, always delegating and, in `cost_effective` only, the path with the lower forecast cost (delegate when `delegate_cost_usd < inline_cost_usd`, else inline). A cell keeps no forecast cost, so the script rebuilds each row 7 `cost_effective` decision from its calibration half as 13.9 states it and requires the rebuilt mode, model and reason to equal the cell's. Row 6 (`refuse`) has no path to score: it raises `NoModelClearsBar` on every row 7 cell. The note holds a `text` block of the command and its output, compared byte for byte by a cached-file test in `tests/test_research_no_path_clears.py` (skipped unless the pinned AutoBench and tau2-bench files are in the default cache), and every other number in its prose is in that output or is a section, row or seed count the test names. README Limitations gains a bullet, quoted in that test, that row 7 decides most `cost_effective` cells of both tau2-bench runs and takes a path whose held-out success is below the bar on most of them, which the cached-file test checks on the output

### 13.9 Offline delegation evidence (`delegation_eval.py`, v1.19)
Purpose: show from a public outcome matrix, with no API key, what the 13.5 decision does on items it never saw, next to always working inline and always delegating. No public dataset records inline-versus-sub-agent outcomes, so the method combines what a matrix does measure, each model's acceptance and token counts on each item, with a stated grid for what it does not, the parent's context and the hand-off sizes. The decision comes from `Router.decide_delegation`, so the evidence exercises the shipped rules of 13.2 to 13.5 and not a re-implementation of them. The task it builds excludes no family and names no generator, so of the 13.4 blocks only `parent_context_full` can apply here
- `evaluate_delegation(rows, registry, bars, points, seeds=range(10), flavors=("cost_effective", "cost_blind"), parents=None, calibration_fraction=0.5, min_samples=30, confidence=0.95) -> dict`
- `rows`: dicts with `task`, `item`, `model` and `correct` as in 12.1, and `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`, integers `>= 0` that are not bools, required even when `cost_usd` is present. `cost_usd` and other extra keys are ignored (from v1.74 all but `call_input_tokens` and `call_output_tokens`, the per-call sizes below): every cost here is a list price from `registry.get(model).cost(...)` (4.1), because the delegate path (13.2) charges one item's tokens to two models, which a billed per-call total cannot be split into
- `registry`: holds every model in the rows; its prices, families and context windows are the ones 13.2 to 13.4 use
- `points`: the grid of assumed sizes, a non-empty sequence of dicts, each with exactly the keys `context_tokens` (C), `brief_tokens` (B), `result_tokens` (R) and `carry_turns` (T), each an integer `>= 0` that is not a bool, and `inline_separate_call`, a bool (13.1), and from v1.43 optionally `work_calls` (K, 13.1), an integer `>= 1` that is not a bool. A point without `work_calls` means K = 1; any other key is still an extra key. A point's values are checked in that key order, so `work_calls` after `inline_separate_call`, and an invalid `work_calls` is a `ValueError` whose message starts `'work_calls' must be an integer >= 1`. Points are compared with a missing `work_calls` read as 1, so a point with `work_calls` 1 and the same point without the key are a repeated point. v1.57: a point may also carry `cached_read_ratio` (rho, 13.1), an `int` or a `float` that is not a bool, with 0 <= rho <= 1 (so NaN and the infinities fail), checked after `work_calls`; an invalid one is a `ValueError` whose message starts `'cached_read_ratio' must be a number from 0 to 1`. A point without it means rho = 1.0. The message for a missing or an extra key is `a point needs exactly the keys [...], and optionally 'work_calls' and 'cached_read_ratio': ` followed by the point, the list being the five keys. Points are compared with a missing `cached_read_ratio` read as 1.0 as well, so a point with `cached_read_ratio` 1.0 or 1 and the same point without the key are a repeated point
- `parents`: the model IDs tried as the parent, in the order given; `None` means every model in the rows, ascending
- Checked before any work, each a `ValueError`: no rows; a row that is not a dict, or whose `task`, `item`, `model` or `correct` is invalid as in 12.1, or whose `input_tokens` or `output_tokens` is missing or invalid; a repeated (task, item, model); a model not in `registry`; an incomplete matrix (12.3); bars, seeds and flavors as in 12.3; no points, a point that is not a dict, has a missing or an extra key or an invalid value, or a repeated point; an empty `parents`, a parent that is not a model of the rows, or a repeated parent; `min_samples`, `confidence` and `calibration_fraction` as in 12.3; an empty calibration or held-out split (12.3); from v1.74 the per-call row, task and point checks below
- One **cell** per (bar, flavor, point, parent, seed, task), nested in that order: bars, flavors, points and parents in the order given, seeds in the order given, tasks ascending. C and H are the task's calibration and held-out items from `split_items` (12.2), so a seed splits a task exactly as `evaluate` does
- The decision uses the calibration half only:
  - stats: for each model, successes = its correct rows in C and trials = |C|, and (v1.35) its token totals over those |C| calls, `Stats.add(task, model, successes, |C|, input_tokens=<the sum of its input_tokens over C>, output_tokens=<the sum of its output_tokens over C>)` with the default `token_trials` (4.3). So `decide_delegation` prices the parent, every candidate of the delegate's `select` and the delegate each at that model's own rounded-up mean calibration sizes (13.2, 13.3). Before v1.35 the stats held counts alone and every model was priced at S_in and S_out
  - S_in and S_out: `statistics.median_low` of `input_tokens`, and of `output_tokens`, over the task's calibration rows of every model, so an integer. They are the task's expected sizes, and since every model of the matrix has token totals (v1.35) they set no price: they are read only where 13.3 and 13.4 read the task's expected tokens whatever a model's measured sizes, the 13.4 rule 3 inline check `C + S_in + S_out` and the 5.2 rule 5 check of the delegate's `select`, `S_in + B + S_out`; for a point with K work calls (v1.43) they are `C + K * (S_in + S_out)` and the delegate's last call, `B + K * (S_in + S_out)` (13.3, 13.4)
  - the decision is `Router(registry, {task: t}, stats).decide_delegation(task, parent, flavor, **point)` with `t = Task(name=task, bar=bar, min_samples=min_samples, confidence=confidence, independent_of_generator=False, expected_input_tokens=S_in, expected_output_tokens=S_out, on_no_model="best_available", max_fallbacks=0)`, so a point's `work_calls` (v1.43) reaches `decide_delegation` as its `work_calls`, and a point without it leaves the default 1; likewise a point's `cached_read_ratio` (v1.57) reaches it as its `cached_read_ratio`, and a point without it leaves the default 1.0
  - a cell whose decision has no route, because no model passes 5.2 rule 5 for S_in + B + S_out (for K work calls, for the delegate's last call, B + K * (S_in + S_out), v1.43), is a `ValueError` whose message names the task, the parent and the point as `point <index>`. That is also the only case in which this `decide_delegation` raises `NoModelClearsBar`, which becomes the same `ValueError`
- Strategies, scored on H, in this order: `decision` (inline on the parent when `mode` is `inline`, else delegated to the decision's `model`), `inline` (inline on the parent for every item, even where 13.4 blocks it; the cell's `inline_allowed` says whether it could), `delegate` (delegated to `route.primary` for every item)
- Held-out cost of one item i, with in(i, m) and out(i, m) the tokens of model m's row for i, P the parent, D the model that does delegated work, and C, B, R and T from the point; each expression is evaluated left to right in floating point, as in 13.2:
  - inline with `inline_separate_call` True: `P.cost(C + in(i, P), out(i, P)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(in(i, P) + out(i, P), 0)`
  - inline with `inline_separate_call` False: `P.cost(in(i, P), out(i, P)) + T * P.cost(in(i, P) + out(i, P), 0)`
  - delegated: `P.cost(0, B) + D.cost(B + in(i, D), out(i, D)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`
  - v1.43, a point with K work calls (13.1): the cost follows the 13.2 K-call expressions with the item's recorded tokens as the sizes of each of the K work calls, S_in = in(i, m) and S_out = out(i, m) for the model m doing the work on that path, and w_in = K(K + 1) / 2 and w_out = K(K - 1) / 2:
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` True: `P.cost(K * C + w_in * in(i, P) + w_out * out(i, P), K * out(i, P)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(K * (in(i, P) + out(i, P)), 0)`
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` False: `P.cost((K - 1) * C + w_in * in(i, P) + w_out * out(i, P), K * out(i, P)) + T * P.cost(K * (in(i, P) + out(i, P)), 0)`
    - delegated: `P.cost(0, B) + D.cost(K * B + w_in * in(i, D) + w_out * out(i, D), K * out(i, D)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`
    - every token total is an integer, so with K = 1 (w_in = 1, w_out = 0) each first and second argument of `P.cost` and `D.cost` is the integer of the K = 1 expression above and every held-out cost is the v1.42 one bit for bit; a point without `work_calls` is that case
    - the item's success on each path is still its one recorded answer, so `correct` counts each held-out item once whatever K: whether the K calls succeed together is not measured, and each of the K calls is assumed to read and write what the item's one recorded call did
  - v1.57, a point with `cached_read_ratio` rho (13.1): the inline cost prices the parent's reads of C in its work calls at rho times its list input price, as 13.2 v1.41 does, and nothing else changes:
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` True: `P.cost(K * C * rho + w_in * in(i, P) + w_out * out(i, P), K * out(i, P)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(K * (in(i, P) + out(i, P)), 0)`
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` False: `P.cost((K - 1) * C * rho + w_in * in(i, P) + w_out * out(i, P), K * out(i, P)) + T * P.cost(K * (in(i, P) + out(i, P)), 0)`
    - delegated: the v1.43 expression whatever rho, and the delegate (`route.primary`) is that of the same point without the key, since rho never reaches the delegate's `select` (13.1); so the `inline` and `delegate` strategies' `correct` and the `delegate` strategy's cost are those of that point too. Only the inline cost moves, and through the decision's own rho-priced `inline_cost_usd` its `mode` and what follows from it: the cell's `model` and `reason`, the `decision` strategy's `correct`, `success` and `cost_usd`, and the summary's `delegate_rate`
    - the first argument is evaluated left to right: the integer `K * C` or `(K - 1) * C`, times rho, then the integer terms added. A point without the key keeps the v1.43 integer total, so its held-out costs are the v1.56 ones bit for bit; with `cached_read_ratio` 1.0 the argument is a float equal to that integer and `Model.cost` converts the integer to the same float before it multiplies by a price, so the costs are again the v1.56 ones bit for bit (while the totals stay below 2^53)
  - so the decision is made on calibration-half sizes, each model's own mean since v1.35 (the pooled medians before), and is scored on each held-out item's own recorded sizes for the model that did the work, as before: no held-out cost uses a mean or a median
- v1.74, per-call sizes: a multi-call item, such as one tau2-bench conversation (12.14, [`research/delegation-datasets.md`](research/delegation-datasets.md)), can be priced on its own calls only if its row keeps each call's position, so a row may carry two optional keys, both or neither:
  - `call_input_tokens` (a_1 to a_K), a non-empty list of integers `>= 0` that are not bools: a_1 is the first call's whole read, the work's own base context (on tau2-bench the system prompt with the domain policy and the tool schemas, the greeting and the user's first message), and a_j for j > 1 is call j's new input, what it reads beyond call j - 1's read and output. `call_output_tokens` (b_1 to b_K), a list of the same kind and length: each call's output. K = K(i, m) is the number of work calls of model m on item i, so two models may take different numbers of calls on one item
  - call j reads a_1 to a_j and b_1 to b_{j-1}, so the K calls read Rd = the sum over j of (K - j + 1) * a_j + (K - j) * b_j in all, write O = b_1 + ... + b_K, and leave A + O in the context, with A = a_1 + ... + a_K. The row's `input_tokens` must equal Rd and its `output_tokens` must equal O, so a per-call row's two counts are what its calls read and write, the summed counts the 12.14 `--tokens` matrix records for a model whose every new input is at least 0, and `evaluate` (12.3) on the same matrix prices each item at the list cost of its calls. Means per call never price a held-out item: with K = 2, inputs 1 and 9 and outputs 2 and 2, the two calls read 1 and 12, Rd = 13, while means of 5 and 2 would give 3 * 5 + 1 * 2 = 17 (review iter-072 must-fix 1)
  - a_1 is the work's input, which 13.2 says is fetched free and read on both paths: inline the parent reads it after C, delegated the delegate reads it after B. So B stays what the parent writes to hand the work off (13.1), never a copy of the policy; a brief that also counted a_1 would charge it twice. This replaces the research note's "the brief carries the domain policy and the tool schemas, so it is at least the first call's read": the policy reaches the delegate as the work's own input
  - checked before any work, each a `ValueError` whose message starts as quoted and ends with the row, in the same pass as the row's 12.1 and token checks, row by row: a row's per-call checks run right after its token checks and before the next row is read, so a bad per-call row is reported before a later row's 12.1 error. They are: one key without the other (`'call_input_tokens' and 'call_output_tokens' go together`); a key that is not a non-empty list of integers `>= 0` that are not bools, a float or a bool among them included (`'call_input_tokens' must be a non-empty list of integers >= 0`, and likewise for `'call_output_tokens'`); lists of unequal length (`'call_input_tokens' and 'call_output_tokens' must have the same length`); an `input_tokens` other than Rd or an `output_tokens` other than O (`'input_tokens' must be what the row's calls read`, followed by Rd, then `, and 'output_tokens' what they write, ` followed by O). After the incomplete-matrix check, for tasks ascending, a task with rows of both kinds (`task <name>: either every row or no row carries per-call sizes`, the name as a quoted repr), since the decision's sizes pool the task's rows. After the point checks, for points in order, a point whose `work_calls` (read as 1 when missing) is above 1 while any task has per-call sizes (`point <index> sets 'work_calls' above 1 and task <name> has per-call sizes`, the first such task ascending): a row's lists are already its work calls, and a point's K would repeat each of them K times. A point with `work_calls` 1 is the point without it (points above) and is accepted. A task whose rows have no per-call sizes runs as before beside one whose rows have them
  - held-out cost of item i on a task with per-call sizes, with K(i, m), Rd(i, m), A(i, m) and O(i, m) of model m's row for i, P the parent, D the model that does delegated work, and C, B, R and T from the point, each expression evaluated left to right in floating point:
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` True: `P.cost(K(i, P) * C + Rd(i, P), O(i, P)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(A(i, P) + O(i, P), 0)`
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` False: `P.cost((K(i, P) - 1) * C + Rd(i, P), O(i, P)) + T * P.cost(A(i, P) + O(i, P), 0)`
    - delegated: `P.cost(0, B) + D.cost(K(i, D) * B + Rd(i, D), O(i, D)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`
    - with `cached_read_ratio` rho, the integer `K(i, P) * C` or `(K(i, P) - 1) * C` is multiplied by rho and Rd(i, P) is added, as v1.57 does; nothing else moves
    - each path counts the calls of the model doing the work on it, so inline and delegated may count a different K for one item. A row of K equal calls, a_j = s and b_j = u for every j, gives Rd = w_in * s + w_out * u, A = K * s and O = K * u, the integers of the v1.43 expressions, so it prices as the row (s, u) without the keys at a point with `work_calls` K, bit for bit, and the item's success is still its one recorded answer. With `cached_read_ratio` (v1.75) that holds while `K * C * rho` (or `(K - 1) * C * rho`) is a whole number, as at rho 0.5 on C = 100,000; otherwise the float may differ in its last bit, since this adds the one integer Rd to it and v1.57 adds w_in * s and then w_out * u
  - the decision on a task with per-call sizes forecasts an unseen item from the calibration half, so it reads means there, as v1.35 does, an approximation that no held-out cost uses:
    - K_dec = `statistics.median_low` of K(i, m) over the task's calibration rows of every model. It reaches `decide_delegation` as `work_calls`, in place of the point's 1, so one K prices both paths in the decision even where the parent and a delegate take different numbers of calls
    - stats: `Stats.add(task, m, successes, |C|, input_tokens=|C| * ceil(A_C / K_C), output_tokens=|C| * ceil(O_C / K_C))`, with A_C, O_C and K_C the sums of A(i, m), O(i, m) and K(i, m) over C and each ceiling in integer arithmetic, so `mean_tokens` is the model's rounded-up mean new input and output per call (4.3 keeps `token_trials` at most `trials`, so the per-call mean is set through the totals)
    - S_in and S_out = `median_low` over the task's calibration rows of every model of ceil(A / K) and of ceil(O / K). The two window checks then read the forecast last call, `C + K_dec * (S_in + S_out)` (13.4 rule 3, in its v1.38 K-call form) and `B + K_dec * (S_in + S_out)` (5.2 rule 5): one call's read and write, never a summed count. At a row's own K and sizes, K * (ceil(A / K) + ceil(O / K)) is at least A + O, its last call's read and write, so the check never under-states that row's last call; at the medians it is a forecast. (At C = 64,000 the summed counts of the 12.14 `--tokens` matrix put telecom's check at 210,536 to 230,765 tokens though no conversation's last call read more than 33,765)
    - a cell of such a task carries `expected_work_calls`, K_dec; cells of other tasks keep the v1.73 keys
  - reasoning that a later call does not re-read: each b_j is charged as written and as re-read by the later work calls and the 1 + T later parent calls, and the harness cannot tell reasoning apart. A converter writes per-call rows only for a model whose every new input is at least 0, that is whose every later read is at least the previous read plus its output, which the row check enforces since no list entry may be negative; any other model is left out of the matrix with all its rows (12.3 needs every model on every item). On tau2-bench that leaves out `openai/o4-mini-2025-04-16`, whose new input is negative on 2,962 of its 3,849 steps
  - cached re-reads of the work's own history are at list price on both paths: a stated over-statement for an agent whose provider cached them (on tau2-bench list price is 1.79 to 2.35 times the recorded costs of the three OpenAI agents); rho still prices only the parent's reads of C
  - a row without the two keys prices as v1.73 bit for bit, and a matrix with no such row gives the v1.73 result bit for bit
- For each strategy, `correct` = the number of items in H on which its model's row is correct, `success = correct / |H|`, and `cost_usd` = the `math.fsum` of its item costs over H
  - v1.132, computed once per path: a strategy's `correct` and `cost_usd` depend only on the cell's task, seed, point and parent, on whether its path works inline or delegated, and on the model doing the work (the parent inline, `route.primary` delegated), never on the bar or the flavor. So a run computes the pair for each (task, seed, point, parent, path, model) once, the first time a cell needs it, and every later cell with that key, at any bar or flavor, reads the same integer and the same float; `decision` reads the pair of the path it takes. Inline and delegated stay two keys when `route.primary` is the parent. Every cell, the summary and the result are the v1.131 ones bit for bit; only the number of held-out sums falls, from three per cell to one per distinct key
- Cell keys: `bar`, `flavor`, `point` (the index into `points`), `parent`, `seed`, `task`, `calibration_items`, `held_out_items`, `expected_input_tokens` (S_in), `expected_output_tokens` (S_out), (v1.74, only on a task with per-call sizes) `expected_work_calls`, `mode`, `model`, `reason`, `below_bar` and `inline_allowed` from the decision, `delegate` (`route.primary`), then `correct`, `success` and `cost_usd`, each a dict keyed by strategy in strategy order
- Return value keys: `config` (`bars`, `seeds`, `flavors` and `points` as lists in the order given, each point with its keys in the order above (`work_calls` and then (v1.57) `cached_read_ratio` last, each only on a point that has it, so a grid of points without them serialises as before v1.43), `parents` as the list used, `calibration_fraction`, `min_samples`, `confidence`); `tasks` (`{task: {"items": int, "models": [ids ascending]}}`); `cells` in cell order; `summary`, one row per (bar, flavor, point, parent, strategy) in that order, with keys `bar`, `flavor`, `point`, `parent`, `strategy`, `success` (the mean over seeds of that seed's total `correct` over its total held-out items across tasks), `cost_usd` (the mean over seeds of that seed's total held-out cost across tasks), `below_bar_rate` (the fraction of that group's (task, seed) cells whose held-out success is below the bar, strictly: a success equal to the bar is not below it) and `delegate_rate` (for `decision`, the fraction of those cells whose `mode` is `delegate`; `None` for the two baselines)
- Arithmetic and determinism as in 12.3: every success and rate is an integer count over an integer count, every cost total and the sum behind every mean uses `math.fsum`, the same rows in any order give an equal dict, and `json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True)` is byte-identical
- What is measured and what is assumed:
  - measured, from the matrix: whether each model's answer to each item was accepted, and the input and output tokens of that call
  - from the registry: list prices, families and context windows. On public data the prices are recorded list prices, so every cost is a list-price estimate, never a billed cost
  - assumed, the grid: C, B, R, T, `inline_separate_call`, (v1.43) K and (v1.57) rho. No public matrix records a parent's context or a hand-off, so results are stated per point, never pooled across points
  - assumed, quality: the delegate is credited with its recorded answer to the whole item, as if a brief of B tokens carried everything the item needs; the parent is credited with its recorded answer as if C more tokens of context did not change it. Neither a lossy brief nor long-context degradation is measured, and each would lower one path's success. So the results show what the decision rule does under these assumptions, not the quality of a real hand-off
  - the 13.8 limits apply: one call per path unless a point sets `work_calls` (v1.43), with the bar and the success per call, never for the K calls together; and every read at list price (rho = 1.0, no prompt caching) unless a point sets `cached_read_ratio` (v1.57), which prices only the parent's reads of C in its inline work calls at rho times its list input price, an assumed ratio since no public matrix records cached prices
  - (v1.59) one dataset: the committed results (13.10) read only the AutoBench matrix (12.9). [`research/delegation-datasets.md`](research/delegation-datasets.md) finds that the tau2-bench files of 12.14, which hold a success and per-call token counts for each of the 4 agents on each item, cannot feed this harness as it stands: each item is a conversation of 2 to 100 model calls, so in(i, m) and out(i, m) taken as the conversation's summed counts mis-price both paths by amounts that grow with its number of calls; a point's `work_calls` sets one K for every item; and list price leaves out the prompt-cache discount that the recorded costs of the three OpenAI agents imply on about three quarters of their prompt tokens. Pricing each item on its own work calls would have to come first; v1.74 specifies it (the per-call sizes above), implemented by v1.75, and v1.76 specifies the converter mode that writes such rows from the pinned files (12.14 `--calls`), implemented by v1.77; v1.78 specifies delegation results on those rows (13.12) and v1.79 commits them
- On the AutoBench matrix (12.9) at the pinned revision, checked when this section was written: the `matrix.csv` column `output_tokens` already counts the reasoning tokens. On 4,931 of the 4,933 scored pool cells (the other 2 have a billed cost of 0), `(input_tokens * input_cost_per_mtok + output_tokens * output_cost_per_mtok) / 1e6`, with the two prices from the `pool.json` `members` entry whose `display_id` is the row's `model`, gives a median ratio of 1.001 to the billed `cost`, within 1% on 47% of the cells, and adding `reasoning_tokens` to `output_tokens` gives a median ratio of 1.718. v1.21: the converter writes those counts with `--tokens` and the registry with `--registry` (12.9)
- Test status: the contract tests (`tests/test_delegation_eval.py`) were committed before `delegation_eval.py` existed, under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=ModuleNotFoundError)`, as in 12.5. v1.20: the implementing commit removed that marker, re-exports `evaluate_delegation` from `model_routing` in `__all__`, and names the module in section 3 and in the README Layout block; from then on any failure in them fails the suite. The bar, seed, flavor, `min_samples`, `confidence` and `calibration_fraction` checks are the ones `evaluate` runs (12.3), from one shared helper, so the two cannot drift apart. There is no CLI subcommand: the AutoBench run is a script under `evidence/`. v1.35: the per-model size tests are committed under `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)` ahead of the harness change, on a matrix where one model writes 100 times what another does, so the pooled medians and the per-model means pick different delegates; the `cost_blind` decisions and the 13.4 rule 3 check are pinned by plain tests that pass before and after; the 13.10 results are regenerated in the implementing version. v1.36: the implementing commit removed those markers, and the v1.19 test that rebuilds each decision from the calibration half builds its stats with the token totals. v1.43: the multi-call harness tests (`tests/test_delegation_eval_work_calls.py`, a file of their own as v1.38 had) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` that names what the v1.42 harness raises: `ValueError` where a test runs a point with `work_calls`, which `_points` rejects as a key outside the five, and `AssertionError` where a test expects a v1.43 error message (an invalid `work_calls`, a repeated point, a K-call point with no delegate) that the v1.42 key check raises first with other words. Next to them, plain tests pin that points without `work_calls` give the v1.42 result bit for bit (a SHA-256 of `json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True)` on two fixture runs, taken on the v1.42 harness), that the K-call held-out expressions with K = 1 equal the v1.42 ones exactly, and that the committed 13.10 config has no `work_calls`. The implementing commit removes the markers; v1.44 is that commit. From v1.45 a 13.10 point with K > 1 is a variant point (13.10). v1.57: the cached-point tests (`tests/test_delegation_cached_point.py`, a file of their own as v1.43 and v1.45 had) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` that names what the v1.56 code raises: `ValueError` where a test runs a point with `cached_read_ratio`, which `_points` rejects as a key outside the six, `AssertionError` where a test expects a v1.57 error message (an invalid `cached_read_ratio`, a repeated point with the key read as 1.0, the new key message) that the v1.56 key check raises first with other words, or where it expects the two variant points or the `cached` column of 13.10, which v1.56 does not have. Next to them, plain tests pin that points without `cached_read_ratio` give the v1.56 result bit for bit (a SHA-256 of `json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True)` on fixture runs taken on the v1.56 harness, one with K-call points among them), that the committed `results.json` points and variant point 0 of the committed `variants.json` carry no `cached_read_ratio`, and that the `table` of `results.json` has no `cached` column. The implementing commit removes the markers; v1.58 is that commit (13.10). v1.74: the per-call tests (`tests/test_delegation_eval_call_sizes.py`, a file of their own as v1.43 and v1.57 had) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)`: the v1.73 harness ignores the two keys as extra keys, so a test sees the one-call cost of `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`, no `expected_work_calls` and no error, and an expected error is asserted through a helper that raises `AssertionError` when none comes. Next to them, plain tests pin that rows without the keys give the v1.73 result bit for bit (a SHA-256 of `json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True)` on a two-task fixture run with K-call and cached points, taken on the v1.73 harness), that the closed form of Rd is the sum of the calls' reads, and that a point with `work_calls` 1 is the point without it on per-call rows. The implementing commit removes the markers and regenerates both 13.10 files byte-identical; v1.75 is that commit

### 13.10 Delegation results on AutoBench (`evidence/delegation/results.py`, v1.22)
Purpose: the 13.9 evidence on public data. The 13.5 decision, always inline and always delegating, scored on held-out AutoBench 2026 questions for every pool model as the parent, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the tables in its RESULTS.md and the README checked against the committed file by the test suite. Standard library and the `model_routing` package only
- Inputs: the 12.9 converter's `--task-by all --tokens` matrix at the default width (one task, `all`, of 203 items over 20 models) and its `--registry` pool registry, so every cost is a list-price estimate from `pool.json` prices (13.9)
- Fixed in the script, and so in the committed config: bars 0.7 and 0.8 (in 12.10 every seed clears at 0.7 and about two thirds at 0.8), 50 seeds, both flavors, `parents=None` (the 20 pool models, ascending), the default `calibration_fraction`, `min_samples` and `confidence`, and a grid whose points 0 to 2 differ only in C: 8,000, 64,000 and 200,000 context tokens, each with B = 500, R = 500, T = 1 and `inline_separate_call` True. 200,000 is above the context windows of the two pool models with 131,072 and 131,000 tokens, so `parent_context_full` blocks inline for them at that point. v1.40 adds point 3: C = 8,000 with the same B, R and T and `inline_separate_call` False, the work folded into the parent's next call. On points 0 to 2 the `cost_effective` decision delegates on every seed for every parent at both bars, so without a folded point the evidence never exercises its inline branch. At K = 1 no folded cost reads C (13.2) and C enters only the 13.4 rule 3 check, so two folded points that differ only in C, both with room in every parent's window, give the same summary rows apart from `point`, and one folded point stands for them. Points 0 to 2 keep their indices
- `points() -> list`: the four point dicts in that order (three before v1.40), keys in the 13.9 order. `run(rows, registry) -> dict`: `evaluate_delegation(rows, registry, bars=[0.7, 0.8], points=points(), seeds=range(50))` keeping only `config`, `tasks` and `summary`, in that order (no `cells`, so the file stays under about 300 KB)
- v1.45, variant points: a point that changes a 13.2 knob the grid above holds fixed goes in a second file, `evidence/delegation/variants.json`, so `results.json` keeps its bytes and each file stays under about 300 KB (the four points above take 269,163 bytes, about 67,000 a point, so a fifth in the same file would pass it). `variant_points() -> list`: the variant point dicts in index order, keys in the 13.9 order (`work_calls` last); a later variant point is appended after them, so the indices of the earlier ones hold. Variant point 0: C = 64,000, B = 500, R = 500, T = 1, `inline_separate_call` False and `work_calls` 2. Folded work of K calls reads C in K - 1 of them on the inline path ((K - 1) * C, 13.2) and only the brief on the delegate path, so this point shows what one re-read of a 64,000-token context does to the decision; base point 3 (folded, K = 1) stands for every C with room in every parent's window, 64,000 among them (above), so the two compare at equal C and differ in K only
- v1.57, variant point 1: base point 2 with `cached_read_ratio` 0.1, that is C = 200,000, B = 500, R = 500, T = 1, `inline_separate_call` True and `cached_read_ratio` 0.1, keys in the 13.9 order (no `work_calls`, so K = 1). It is appended after variant point 0, whose index and summary rows hold, since each point's cells read only that point. Base point 2 has the largest C of the base grid, so its inline path pays most for re-reading C, and with a separate call at K = 1 that path reads C once; the two points have equal sizes and differ in rho only. 0.1 is an assumption, stated as one in RESULTS.md and the README: the AutoBench pool (13.9) records no cached price, and 0.1 is the ratio of the README cached-read `delegate` example (a cached read at a tenth of the list input price). rho moves no 13.4 check, so the two pool models whose context windows are under 200,000 tokens stay blocked from inline (`parent_context_full`) at variant point 1, as at base point 2
- v1.72, variant point 2: variant point 0 with `work_calls` set to `VARIANT_OBSERVED_WORK_CALLS = 15`, that is C = 64,000, B = 500, R = 500, T = 1, `inline_separate_call` False and `work_calls` 15, keys in the 13.9 order (`work_calls` last, no `cached_read_ratio`). It is appended after variant point 1, whose index and summary rows hold, as do variant point 0's, since each point's cells read only that point. 15 is the median of the runs' `model_calls` in the committed `evidence/trail/summary.json` (13.11: `stats`, `model_calls`, `median`, 15.0), rounded up with `math.ceil` when it is not a whole number: the median of a number of whole numbers is whole or halfway between two, so the constant is never below the observed median. The constant is a literal in the script, which never reads summary.json; a test builds it from summary.json so the two cannot drift, and a summary.json whose median moves fails that test until the constant and `variants.json` follow. Variant point 0 is the folded multi-call point, so the two compare at equal C and differ in K only: at K = 15 the inline path re-reads C in 14 calls instead of one ((K - 1) * C, 13.2) while each delegated call reads the brief in its place. Window checks at K = 15 on 64,000 context tokens (checked with a private prototype of this contract on the pinned AutoBench files when this section was written; the cached-file test pins them): over the 50 seeds S_in + S_out, the pooled calibration medians of 13.9, is 1,331 to 1,678, so the 13.4 rule 3 check `C + K * (S_in + S_out)` needs at most 89,170 tokens and the 5.2 rule 5 check of the delegate's last call, `B + K * (S_in + S_out)`, at most 25,670, both below the smallest pool window, 131,000 (`openai/gpt-oss-120b`). So neither check drops a parent or a delegate candidate on any seed: every cell of variant point 2 has `inline_allowed` True, no cell's reason is `parent_context_full`, and the run raises no 13.9 no-route `ValueError`. A delegate candidate dropped by 5.2 rule 5 while others remain would show in no reason, so on that side the bound of 25,670 tokens is what the cached-file test pins. `variants.json` keeps no cells, so the file shows this only by what it lacks, no delegation forced by a window; the cached-file test reads it from an in-process run that keeps the cells. At that C every parent keeps room up to K = 39, and TRAIL's largest run, 31 calls, would need 116,018 tokens. The point is a cost sensitivity at an observed size, never a measured hand-off: TRAIL's runs are another agent (`o3-mini` on GAIA, 13.11) on other work, the AutoBench label is still one answer's (13.9: each of the 15 calls is assumed to read and write what the item's one recorded call did, and whether the 15 calls succeed together is not measured), and the bar is held per call (13.8). RESULTS.md and the README say so
- v1.45, `run(rows, registry, grid="base") -> dict`: `grid` `"base"` is the run above on `points()`; `"variants"` is the same run, with the same bars, seeds and keys kept, on `variant_points()`; any other value, `None` among them, is a `ValueError` whose message starts `grid must be 'base' or 'variants'`, raised before `evaluate_delegation` is called
- `table(results) -> str`: one row per (bar, flavor, point) in config order, columns `bar` (as Python `str` prints it), `flavor`, `context` (that point's C), `separate` (v1.40: that point's `inline_separate_call` as Python `str` prints it, `True` or `False`, since points 0 and 3 share C), then `dec_success`, `dec_cost`, `inl_success`, `inl_cost`, `del_success` and `del_cost` (for `decision`, `inline` and `delegate`, the mean over the config parents of the summary rows' `success` and `cost_usd`, each mean a `math.fsum` over the parents divided by their number) and `delegated` (the same mean of the `decision` rows' `delegate_rate`). Layout of 12.4 (header, a dash row, two spaces between columns, trailing spaces stripped, no newline after the last row); success and `delegated` to 3 decimals, cost to 4
- v1.45, `table` on variant points: when any config point carries `work_calls`, a column `calls` follows `separate`, that point's `work_calls` as Python `str` prints it, and `1` for a point without it; a config whose points carry no `work_calls` prints the v1.40 columns, so the table of `results.json` is unchanged. `parent_table` and `breakeven_table` are unchanged and read a variants file as they read the base one
- v1.57, `table` with cached reads: when any config point carries `cached_read_ratio`, a column `cached` follows `calls` when the table prints that column and `separate` otherwise, that point's `cached_read_ratio` as Python `str` prints it, and `1.0` for a point without it; a config whose points carry no `cached_read_ratio` prints the columns it printed before v1.57, so the tables of `results.json` and of the v1.47 `variants.json` are unchanged. `parent_table` and `breakeven_table` are unchanged
- `parent_table(results, bar, point) -> str`: for one bar and point index, one row per (flavor, parent) in config order, columns `flavor`, `parent`, `delegated`, then the six strategy columns, each from that parent's own summary rows, same layout and formats
- `breakeven_table(results, bar, point) -> str` (v1.37): how much held-out success a lossy hand-off may lose, which the 13.9 quality assumption (the brief carries the whole item) leaves unmeasured, as a number from the committed file with no new run. For one bar and point index, one row per (flavor, parent) in config order, columns `flavor`, `parent`, `delegated` (the `decision` row's `delegate_rate`), `dec_success`, `inl_success` and `del_success` (the `success` of `decision`, `inline` and `delegate`), then two columns computed in floating point from those unrounded values, left to right:
  - `del_margin` = `del_success - inl_success`: the held-out success every hand-off could lose before always delegating does no better than always inline
  - `dec_breakeven` = `(dec_success - inl_success) / delegated`: the uniform drop in held-out success on the seeds the decision delegates at which the decision falls to always inline's success. Exact, up to floating-point rounding, for the success the summary stores when the results have one task, as the AutoBench file does: each summary `success` is then the mean over seeds of one cell's held-out rate, every seed weighted equally whatever its held-out size, and `delegate_rate` the share of seeds delegated, so lowering the rate of every delegated seed by L (for example, losing L of each delegated seed's held-out questions) lowers `dec_success` by exactly L times `delegated`. It is not the drop in a success pooled over every held-out question of every seed, which would weight seeds by their held-out sizes; with several tasks it is an approximation for a second reason, since a seed's success then pools its tasks. `-` where the stored `delegated` is 0, since the decision then never hands off; any other value, however small, is divided by
  - same layout as `parent_table`; every number to 3 decimals; a negative value (the decision or always delegating already scores below always inline with a lossless brief) prints with its sign, and a value that rounds to zero prints `0.000`, never `-0.000`
- All three tables raise `ValueError` for a missing `config` or `summary`, a repeated (bar, flavor, point, parent, strategy) summary key, or a missing row for a (bar, flavor, point, parent, strategy) of the config; `parent_table` and `breakeven_table` also for a bar not in the config bars or a point that is not an index into the config points
