### 13.11 Observed sub-agent sizes: the TRAIL summary (`evidence/trail/summary.py`, v1.67)
Purpose: set real sub-agent usage from public traces beside the sizes the 13.9 grid assumes, the one use `research/hand-off-data.md` finds for TRAIL. The committed delegation points of the base grid and variant points 0 and 1 do one or two work calls (no `work_calls` key, so K = 1, or 2 on variant point 0), while TRAIL's sub-agent runs make 3 to 31. These numbers are observed usage, never 13.1 inputs, and they feed no decision: TRAIL has no success label and no run of a task without its sub-agent, and the sub-agent is its parent's model. From v1.73 one of them sets a grid size: the median of `model_calls` is the `work_calls` of 13.10 variant point 2, held as a literal in `evidence/delegation/results.py` that a test checks against summary.json, so `variants.json` uses it as a cost sensitivity at an observed size, never as a measured hand-off. Standard library only (argparse, hashlib, json, statistics, sys, urllib, pathlib); the package never imports it
- Source, pinned: the public GitHub repo `patronus-ai/trail-benchmark` at commit `REVISION = "0ffbed9db859b4a66250dc783fa4dccf86869595"` (commit date 2025-05-14), folder `FOLDER = "benchmarking/data/GAIA"`, `LICENCE = "MIT"` (the repo's LICENSE file, `Copyright (c) 2025 patronus-ai`), `REPO_URL = "https://github.com/patronus-ai/trail-benchmark"`, `BASE_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/patronus-ai/trail-benchmark/<REVISION>/benchmarking/data/GAIA/"`. The gated Hugging Face copy is never read: its card asks users not to reshare it outside a gated or private repository. `FILES` maps each of the folder's 117 trace files, named `<trace id>.json` with a 32-character lowercase hexadecimal trace id, to its byte size and sha256, in ascending name order: 111,205,744 bytes, 96,308 to 9,036,173 each. The folder has no checksum file, so the pins in the script are the only trust root; when this was written each of the 117 files matched the size and git blob SHA-1 of the repo tree at `REVISION` (the GitHub API). The repo's SWE-bench traces are not read
- `default_cache_dir()` is `~/.cache/ai-model-routing-evidence/trail/` (from `Path.home()`), never inside the repo; `fetch(cache_dir=None) -> Path` and `verify(path, size, sha256)` as in 12.14: `fetch` creates the folder if needed and goes through `FILES` in order; a present file is verified and kept, and one that fails `verify` is a `ValueError` naming it and is left in place; a missing one is downloaded from `BASE_URL` plus its name into `<cache>/<name>.part` with a 60-second timeout, verified and renamed onto `<cache>/<name>`, the `.part` file removed when the download or the check fails, and any error but a `ValueError` while downloading is raised as an `OSError` naming the file; `read_trace(path)` reads one UTF-8 JSON file, and invalid UTF-8, invalid JSON (a number with more digits than the interpreter converts to an int included, 4,300 by default, for which `json` raises a plain `ValueError`, not a `JSONDecodeError`) or nesting past the recursion limit is a `ValueError` naming it, as `read_run` does in 12.14
- `runs(trace, name) -> list[dict]` reduces one parsed trace file, named `name`, to its sub-agent runs, so the script holds one trace in memory at a time. The spans form a tree: `trace["spans"]` is the list of root spans and each span's `child_spans`, when present, the list of its children; the walk is depth first in file order (a span, then its children in list order) and reads no other key for structure (`logs`, `events` and `links` are never walked); it keeps its own stack, so a deep tree of spans cannot reach the recursion limit
  - a sub-agent run is a span whose `span_name` is `ToolCallingAgent.run`; its model calls are the spans of its subtree whose `span_name` ends in `.__call__`. The top agent is the span named `CodeAgent.run`, present once in each of the 117 pinned traces, and in every pinned trace each run lies inside it
  - `ValueError`, its message naming `name` and, for a span whose `span_id` is a non-empty string, that id: `trace` not a dict; a `trace_id` that is not a string equal to `name` without its `.json` suffix; `spans` not a list; a span that is not a dict, whose `span_id` is not a non-empty string, whose `span_name` is not a string, or whose `child_spans` is present and not a list; a run with no `CodeAgent.run` ancestor (no top agent handed it work), with a run ancestor (whose calls it would share) or with no model call; a model call inside a run whose `span_attributes` is not a dict, or whose `llm.token_count.prompt` or `llm.token_count.completion` is neither a non-empty string of at most 19 ASCII decimal digits (as all 1,507 counted calls in the pinned files store them) nor an int that is not a bool, its value in either case from 0 to `2**63 - 1`, the largest signed 64-bit integer. The cap is checked before any conversion, so no count string reaches the interpreter's limit on converting digits to an int (4,300 by default, whose own `ValueError` would not name the file), and every median converts to a float. When a trace holds several of these faults, which one is reported is not promised (each test holds one)
  - returns one dict per run in walk order, with the keys `trace_id`, `span_id`, `model_calls` (the number of model calls in the run), `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens` (the sums of the two counts over those calls, as ints) and `first_call_prompt_tokens` (the prompt count of the run's first model call in walk order), in that order. A trace with no run gives `[]`. Model calls outside a run are never read, so the one `LiteLLMModel.__call__` span of the pinned files that has no token counts (in a top agent's step) changes nothing
- `summarize(per_trace) -> dict` over a list of `runs` results, one per trace read: `ValueError` for `per_trace` that is not a non-empty list or tuple, an entry that is not a list, no run in any entry, or a (`trace_id`, `span_id`) that repeats. Returns the keys `source` (`{"repo": REPO_URL, "folder": FOLDER, "revision": REVISION, "licence": LICENCE}`), `traces` (the number of entries), `traces_with_runs` (the entries with at least one run), `run_count`, `stats` and `runs`, in that order. `stats` maps each of `model_calls`, `prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens` and `first_call_prompt_tokens`, in that order, to `{"min": int, "median": float, "max": int}` over all runs, the median being `float(statistics.median(values))`; `runs` lists every run, entries in input order and each entry's runs in its order. The input is not modified
- CLI: `python evidence/trail/summary.py --out PATH [--cache DIR]`, entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`. Options are checked first, so a bad one downloads nothing (`--out` must end in a file name: an empty path, `.`, `..`, `/` or a path ending in `/` is a bad option). Then it fetches, reads and reduces the files one at a time in `FILES` order, summarizes, writes `json.dumps(summary, indent=2)` and a newline to `<out>.part`, prints `json.dumps` of the summary without its `runs` key, `indent=2`, flushing stdout, and only then renames `<out>.part` onto `--out`, so a write to stdout that fails (a closed pipe) is an I/O error that leaves `--out` as it was. It returns 0 on success and 2 with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr (a carriage return or line feed in the message written as `\r` or `\n`) and nothing on stdout for a missing or bad option, a size or hash mismatch, invalid data or an I/O error, but for a rename that fails after the print, the one failure with the summary already on stdout; on any failure `--out` is not written (an existing one is left as it was) and `<out>.part` is removed
- Choices (v1.67):
  - sizes from the model-call spans, not the run span: each run span also carries `llm.token_count.prompt` and `llm.token_count.completion`, but on each of the 60 pinned runs both are below the sums over the run's model calls (the run spans' own prompt counts span 3,230 to 245,946, the sums 5,508 to 357,993), and the files do not say what they count, so they are never read
  - first call in walk order: on all 60 pinned runs the walk order of a run's model calls is also the order of their `timestamp` values, so the first call is the earliest, with no earlier call of the run in its prompt; the files do not mark which part of that prompt is the brief
  - observed usage, not 13.1 inputs: each call's prompt count is everything that call read (the instructions, the brief and the run's history so far), while 13.1 and 13.2 take the new input each call adds and grow the context from it, so `prompt_tokens` counts each re-read again and must never be read as K times the work's input per call. `model_calls` is the one number comparable with 13.1's K, though it counts every model call of the run, whatever the call's role (a planning call included)
  - GAIA only, one model: every counted call in the pinned GAIA traces names `o3-mini`, so the summary keeps no model column and compares no price; the SWE-bench traces were not scanned for runs (`research/hand-off-data.md`)
- Default output on the pinned files (checked with a private prototype of this contract when this section was written; the cached-file test pins it): 117 traces, 47 with runs, 60 runs (38 traces with one, 5 with two, 4 with three); `model_calls` 3, 15.0 and 31; `prompt_tokens` 5,508, 94,868.5 and 357,993; `completion_tokens` 1,990, 8,343.5 and 21,188; `first_call_prompt_tokens` 549, 594.5 and 765 (min, median and max), the numbers `research/hand-off-data.md` states
- Committed files (v1.70 specifies them and v1.71 commits them; before v1.70 this bullet left them to a later version):
  - `evidence/trail/summary.json`, the output of `python evidence/trail/summary.py --out evidence/trail/summary.json` on the pinned files, under 300,000 bytes. Its text is `json.dumps` of its content with `indent=2` and a newline; its `source` holds the script's constants and `traces` the length of `FILES`; `run_count` is the length of `runs`, `traces_with_runs` the number of distinct `trace_id` values among them, each a pinned file's name without `.json`; each run has the keys `runs` returns, in that order; and `summarize` of its runs, grouped by trace in file order, with one empty entry for each trace without a run, gives the file's content again. The cached-file test regenerates it from the 117 cached files and compares the bytes
  - `evidence/trail/RESULTS.md`, starting `# TRAIL sub-agent sizes`: the sentence "In {traces_with_runs} of the {traces} GAIA traces the top agent (`CodeAgent.run`) hands work to a sub-agent (`ToolCallingAgent.run`), {run_count} sub-agent runs in all." followed by "{n1} traces hold one run, {n2} two and {n3} three.", the number of traces with one, two and three runs (no pinned trace has more); a table with the header `| count | min | median | max |`, a `|---|---|---|---|` row and one row per `stats` key in its order, `` | `<key>` | <min> | <median> | <max> | ``, each number with thousands commas and a median that is a whole number written without `.0`; a link to summary.json with its size, `[summary.json](summary.json) (<size> bytes)`; the repo URL, the revision linked to its tree and followed by `(commit date <date>)`, the date `research/hand-off-data.md` gives for it, the folder, `Licence: MIT` and the line `Copyright (c) 2025 patronus-ai`; the regenerate command alone in a `bash` block, with the number and total size of the pinned files (`<n> pinned files (<bytes> bytes)`); the observed-usage caveat of the Choices bullet ("observed usage, not the inputs of DESIGN 13.1", and that `prompt_tokens` "must never be read as K times the work's input per call"); what TRAIL lacks, one bullet each, starting `- No success label`, `- No run of a task without its sub-agent` and `- The sub-agent is its parent's model`, so that its numbers "feed no decision", with (v1.73) the one number a results file uses named: "One of them sets a grid size: the median of `model_calls`, {median}, is the `work_calls` of variant point 2 of [variants.json](../delegation/variants.json)", a literal in the delegation script checked against summary.json, "never a measured hand-off", and the phrase "no results file" nowhere; the delegation points sentence (v1.73) "The committed delegation points do one work call (`evidence/delegation/results.json`), two (variant point 0 of `evidence/delegation/variants.json`) or {median} (variant point 2 of the same file, the median below); every run here makes more than one or two."; and the workflow choice below ("The `evidence` workflow does not rerun it"). No folder README: as in `evidence/delegation/`, the script writes the committed file itself, so RESULTS.md holds the source and the command a converter README would
  - README, `### Delegation on AutoBench`: one sentence after the first link to `research/hand-off-data.md` and before the first `text` block, built from summary.json and the committed points: "Real sub-agent runs make more model calls than the grid's one, or two at variant point 0 below, and variant point 2 below takes their median (`work_calls`): in the public TRAIL traces (licence MIT), the top agent hands work to a sub-agent {run_count} times in {traces_with_runs} of {traces} GAIA traces, and each of those runs makes {min} to {max} model calls (median {median}), observed usage only, since TRAIL has no success label and no run of a task without its sub-agent ([evidence/trail/RESULTS.md](evidence/trail/RESULTS.md), `DESIGN.md` 13.11).", numbers from `stats.model_calls` written as in RESULTS.md. Its claims are checked against the committed points: every point of `evidence/delegation/results.json` has K = 1, variant point 0 of `variants.json` has `work_calls` 2, the smallest run's `model_calls` is above the K of every committed point but variant point 2, and (v1.73, which opens the sentence with variant point 2) variant point 2's `work_calls` is the rounded-up median of 13.10. The Layout block gains the line `(TRAIL sub-agent sizes: evidence/trail/RESULTS.md)` right after the delegation line, at the same indent
  - `research/hand-off-data.md` states the summary's numbers: a test builds each TRAIL figure it gives (the file count and total bytes from `FILES`; traces with runs and runs; the min, median and max of `model_calls`, `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens`; the min and max of `first_call_prompt_tokens`) from summary.json, in the file's own wording, and finds it there
  - Workflow (v1.70 choice): the 12.13 workflow does not rerun the script. Its numbers feed no decision, the one number `variants.json` uses (v1.73) is a literal in the delegation script that a test checks against the committed summary.json, the cached-file test compares the committed bytes wherever the 117 files are cached, and a rerun would add 111,205,744 bytes to the job's downloads; rerunning it there is left to a later version
- Test status: `tests/test_evidence_trail.py` is committed before the script, under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=ModuleNotFoundError)`, which v1.69, the implementing version, removed. Every test uses small synthetic traces shaped like a trace file (top keys `trace_id` and `spans`; span keys `timestamp`, `span_id`, `parent_span_id`, `span_name`, `span_attributes` and `child_spans`), except one that reads the pinned files only when all 117 are in the default cache and skips otherwise (CI never downloads them). `tests/test_evidence_trail_results.py`, the contract of the committed files, is committed before them (v1.70) under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=FileNotFoundError)`, since every test reads a committed TRAIL file first, which v1.71 removes

### 13.12 Delegation results on tau2-bench (`evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.py`, v1.78)
Purpose: the 13.9 evidence on a second public dataset, in the shape of 13.10, where each item is a multi-call agent conversation priced on its own calls (13.9 v1.74 per-call sizes) instead of one answer: the 13.5 decision, always inline and always delegating, scored on held-out tau2-bench conversations for each kept agent as the parent, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the tables in its RESULTS.md checked against the committed file by the test suite. Standard library and the `model_routing` package only. v1.78 specifies it with contract tests ahead of the code and files; v1.79 commits the script, `registry.json`, `results.json` and RESULTS.md; v1.80 specifies the README subsection and the workflow lines (12.8, 12.13) and v1.81 adds them; v1.82 specifies a pooled run beside the per-domain one and v1.83 commits it
- Matrix: the 12.14 converter's `--calls` output at its other defaults (trial 0, one task per domain: `airline` 50 items, `retail` 114 and `telecom` 114), which leaves out `openai/o4-mini-2025-04-16` (its new input is negative on 2,962 of its calls) and keeps 3 agents and 834 rows. The matrix, 341,633 bytes (its size and SHA-256 pinned by the 12.14 cached-file test), is never committed
- Per-domain tasks, as 12.15: each domain is one kind of job. At the default `min_samples` 30 airline's calibration half is under 30 on 40 of the 50 seeds (12.14), so no model clears a bar there and every airline cell of those seeds is 13.5 row 7 (`none_clear`) unless 13.4 blocks inline (row 1); RESULTS.md says so. v1.82 runs the pooled task (`--task-by all`) beside it as a sensitivity (the Pooled run bullet below)
- Registry, committed as `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/registry.json` and written by hand: the models.json format (section 9) with the top keys `as_of` (`"2026-11-06"`, the day the pages were opened), `note` (a sentence that names `research/delegation-datasets.md`) and `models`, in that order; each model with the keys `id`, `family`, `price_in`, `price_out`, `context_window`, `source` and `notes`, in that order, models in ID order; no `price_valid_until`, since no page states an end date and these prices only reprice recorded 2025 runs. Its text is `json.dumps` of its content with `indent=2` and a newline. The three models, with prices in USD per 1M tokens:
  - `anthropic/claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219`, family `anthropic`, 3 and 15, window 200,000, source `https://github.com/sierra-research/tau2-bench/tree/<REVISION>/data/tau2/results/final` with the 12.14 `REVISION`. No current vendor page states its price or its window ([`research/delegation-datasets.md`](research/delegation-datasets.md)): the prices are the ones its recorded costs give exactly on all 4,371 trial-0 calls, and the window is an assumption, which `notes` says (with the word `assumed`) along with its retirement on the Claude API on February 19, 2026
  - `openai/gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`, family `openai`, 2.00 and 8.00, window 1,047,576, source `https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-4.1`; `notes` says the page lists it as Deprecated
  - `openai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14`, family `openai`, 0.40 and 1.60, window 1,047,576, source `https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-4.1-mini`; `notes` says the page lists it as Deprecated
- Script constants: `BARS = (0.3, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.7, 0.8)`, the 12.15 bars, so the routing and the delegation evidence on this dataset read the same bars; `SEEDS = 50`; `KEYS = ("config", "tasks", "summary")`. `points()` returns the 13.10 `points()` of `evidence/delegation/results.py`, the four base points, loaded by file path with `importlib`, so the two grids cannot drift. The 13.10 variant points are not run: variant points 0 and 2 set `work_calls` above 1, which 13.9 v1.74 refuses on per-call rows (a row's lists are already its work calls), and variant point 1, cached reads of C at 0.1, runs in process for RESULTS.md only (the Cached point bullet below, v1.88)
- `run(rows, registry) -> dict`: `evaluate_delegation(rows, registry, bars=list(BARS), points=points(), seeds=range(SEEDS))`, keeping only `KEYS` in that order (no `cells`, so the file stays small)
- CLI: `python evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.py --matrix PATH --registry PATH --out PATH`, entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`, all three options required (a missing one is an argparse usage error, exit 2). It reads the rows from the JSONL matrix and the registry with `Registry.load`, writes `json.dumps(run(rows, registry), indent=2)` and a newline to `<out>.part` and renames it onto `--out`, and returns 0. An unreadable or invalid file, or a `ValueError` from the harness, prints one line starting `error: ` to stderr and returns 1, with `--out` untouched and no `.part` left. The tables come from the 13.10 CLI, which reads any results file of this shape: `python evidence/delegation/results.py --table <file>`, and with `--bar X --point I` its `parent_table`
- Checked with a private prototype of this contract on the 12 pinned files when this section was written (the cached-file tests pin each number): the run takes about 2 seconds over 21,600 cells; the results file is 132,475 bytes with 432 summary rows (6 bars, 2 flavors, 4 points, 3 parents and 3 strategies); over the seeds `expected_work_calls` (K_dec) is 10 to 12 on airline, 11 to 12 on retail and 17 to 19 on telecom, and the per-call `S_in + S_out` is 604 to 718, 643 to 675 and 512 to 574. So the forecast work `K_dec * (S_in + S_out)` is at most 10,242 tokens, the 5.2 rule 5 check of the delegate's last call needs at most 10,742 and no cell lacks a route, and the 13.4 rule 3 check needs at most 74,242 at C = 64,000. At C = 200,000 (point 2) it is above the assumed 200,000-token window of `anthropic/claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219` on every cell, so inline is blocked (`parent_context_full`) on all 1,800 of that parent's point 2 cells and on no other cell. That block follows from the assumed window, and RESULTS.md says so
- Prototype results, for the reading below: in `cost_effective` the decision delegates on every cell (`delegate_rate` 1.0 on all 72 decision rows), but mostly by 13.5 row 7, not by cost: of its 10,800 cells 6,941 are `none_clear` (no agent clears the bar, so the calibration bounds decide), 2,301 `delegate_cheaper` (no cell is `inline_cheaper`), 900 `parent_context_full` and 658 `parent_below_bar`; the 10,800 `cost_blind` cells hold the same 6,941, 900 and 658 with 1,375 `delegate_better` and 926 `inline_as_good`. At bar 0.3 and point 0 the means over the three parents are 19.8422 USD on a held-out half for the decision against 67.1856 for always inline, with held-out success 0.550 against 0.574; per parent the decision costs more than always inline only for `openai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14`, 18.9993 against 17.5937
- Assumed, each stated in RESULTS.md `## Assumed` (a test finds each phrase named here): the lossless brief of 13.9 (the delegate is credited with its own whole conversation, as if the brief carried what it needs); the first call's read, the system prompt with the domain policy and the tool schemas, is the work's own input on both paths and B the hand-off alone (13.9 v1.74); list price over-states the recorded costs of `openai/gpt-4.1-2025-04-14` 2.25 times and of `openai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14` 2.35 times (prompt caching inferred from the costs, research note) and those of `anthropic/claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219` 1.0 times, so every cost is a list-price estimate and the gap between the two vendors' agents is about a factor of two wider than recorded; `openai/o4-mini-2025-04-16` left out with its 2,962 negative new inputs; the assumed 200,000-token window; trial 0 only (at trials 1 and 2 the converter also leaves out `openai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14`, so a trial sensitivity would compare 2 agents there; not run); the agents' status on the vendors' pages (the two GPT-4.1 snapshots Deprecated, o4-mini Shut down, Claude 3.7 Sonnet retired on February 19, 2026); airline's 40 of the 50 seeds under `min_samples`; and the 13.8 limits: each conversation's success is its one recorded reward, and the bar is held per call
- Committed by v1.79: `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.json`, the CLI output on that matrix and registry, under 300,000 bytes, exactly `json.dumps(itself, indent=2)` plus a newline, with the config `run` gives (bars, the 50 seeds, both flavors, the 13.10 base points, the 3 agents as `parents`), `tasks` with the three domains, their item counts and the 3 agents, and 432 summary rows with unique (bar, flavor, point, parent, strategy) keys; and `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/RESULTS.md`:
  - first line `# Delegation on tau2-bench: inline or sub-agent on held-out conversations`; it names the source `https://github.com/sierra-research/tau2-bench`, the 12.14 revision in full, the licence MIT, and links `registry.json`, `results.json`, `../tau2-bench/README.md` and `../../research/delegation-datasets.md`; the sections `## Method`, `## Assumed`, `## Results`, `## Reading it` and `## Regenerate`, in that order; ASCII, with no en or em dash
  - `## Results`: two fenced `text` blocks, each starting with the command that prints it, `$ python evidence/delegation/results.py --table evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.json` and the same with ` --bar 0.3 --point 0`, followed by the 13.10 `table` and `parent_table` output on the committed file
  - `## Reading it`, bullets built from the committed file by a test, numbers in the table formats (success to 3 decimals, cost to 4; the context tokens and the byte size with thousands commas, as `8,000`): "In `cost_effective` the decision delegates on every seed for every parent at every bar and point (`delegate_rate` 1.0 on all {n} decision rows)." (the test requires every rate to be 1.0); "At bar {lowest bar} and point 0 ({C} context tokens), averaged over the {p} parents, the `cost_effective` decision costs {dec_cost} USD on a held-out half against {inl_cost} for always inline ({inl_cost / dec_cost to 2 decimals} times as much), with held-out success {dec_success} against {inl_success}." from the `table` means; "At that bar and point the `cost_effective` decision costs more than always inline for {each such parent with `({dec_cost} against {inl_cost})`, joined by `, `}." or "... for no parent."; and "`results.json` is {its size with thousands commas} bytes."
  - `## Method` holds the sentence "Of the {n} `cost_effective` cells, {count} `{reason}`, ... and {count} `{reason}`.", each reason of the run's `cost_effective` cells with its count, in descending count order with ties by name, joined by `, ` with ` and ` before the last, counts with thousands commas, followed by "`none_clear` is 13.5 row 7: no agent clears the bar, so the calibration bounds decide, not cost." A cached-file test builds it from an in-process run that keeps the cells, since `results.json` keeps none (review iter-089 should-fix 1)
  - `## Regenerate`: a `bash` block of exactly these lines: `python -m pip install -e .`, `python evidence/tau2-bench/convert.py --out tau2-calls.jsonl --calls`, `python evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.py --matrix tau2-calls.jsonl --registry evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/registry.json --out evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.json`, then the two table commands above without `$ `
- Pooled run (v1.82, committed by v1.83): the same script, registry, bars, points and seeds on the converter's `--calls --task-by all` matrix, the same 834 rows as one task `all` of 278 conversations, written to a second committed file, `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/pooled.json`. A second file, not an option of the script: `results.py` already runs whatever tasks its matrix holds, and `results.json`, with the README and workflow lines that read it, stays byte for byte as it is. Why: per domain, 13.5 row 7 decides 6,941 of the 10,800 `cost_effective` cells, so the decision there orders the paths by their calibration bounds below the bar and compares cost only on an equal bound (13.5 v1.31), instead of asking which path is cheaper at the bar; pooled, every calibration half is over `min_samples` and the 13.5 row 3 cost comparison is reached on more cells. A pooled bound mixes three kinds of job, which 12.15 keeps apart for routing, so RESULTS.md calls the pooled run a sensitivity of the reading, not its main result. v1.82 and v1.83 left `pooled.json` out of the README and the 12.13 workflow; v1.84 specifies both (12.8, 12.13) and v1.85 adds them
  - `pooled.json`: the CLI output on that matrix and the committed registry, under 300,000 bytes, exactly `json.dumps(itself, indent=2)` plus a newline, with the config of `results.json`, `tasks` equal to `{"all": {"items": 278, "models": <the 3 agents>}}` and 432 summary rows with unique (bar, flavor, point, parent, strategy) keys. The 12.2 split reads only the seed and the item ID, and the converter's item IDs (`<domain>|<task id>`) are the same in both matrices, so both runs score the same held-out conversations: every `inline` summary row has the same `success` and `cost_usd` in both files (a test checks it), while `below_bar_rate` differs, since each run checks the bar on its own tasks
  - RESULTS.md `## Method` gains one bullet that starts "A second run pools the three domains", names `--task-by all` and the 278 conversations, links `(pooled.json)`, gives the pooled calibration half's size over the 50 seeds as "{min} to {max} conversations" (from the cells' `calibration_items`) and contains "a sensitivity of this reading, not its main result"
  - `## Results` gains, after the parent table, a fenced `text` block of `$ python evidence/delegation/results.py --table evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/pooled.json` and the 13.10 `table` output on `pooled.json`, then a Markdown table of both runs' `cost_effective` reason counts per bar, counted from in-process runs that keep the cells (neither results file keeps them): columns `bar`, `run` and `cells`, then one per reason found in either run's `cost_effective` cells, in descending order of its count over both runs with ties by name, each name in backticks; the alignment row `| --- | --- | ---: |` with `---:` for each reason; for each bar ascending a `per domain` row, then a `pooled` row, then a `per domain` and a `pooled` row with bar `all` over every bar; counts with thousands commas, 0 where a row has no cell of a reason
  - `## Reading it` gains three bullets, numbers in the table formats: "Pooled, the `cost_effective` decision again delegates on every seed for every parent at every bar and point; at bar {lowest bar} and point 0, averaged over the {p} parents, it costs {pooled dec_cost} USD on a held-out half against {per-domain dec_cost} per domain, with held-out success {pooled dec_success} against {per-domain dec_success}. Always inline has the same held-out success and cost in both runs, since the split reads only the seed and the item ID (`DESIGN.md` 12.2).", from the two committed files (the test requires every pooled `cost_effective` decision `delegate_rate` to be 1.0); "The cost comparison (`DESIGN.md` 13.5 row 3: both paths clear the bar, so their costs are compared) is reached on {a} of the {n} `cost_effective` cells per domain ({a/n}) and on {b} of the {m} pooled ({b/m}); no cell of either run is `inline_cheaper`.", with counts in thousands commas and shares as percentages to one decimal (`{:.1%}`), where a cell reaches the comparison when its reason is `delegate_cheaper`, `inline_cheaper`, `delegate_better` or `inline_as_good` (in `cost_effective` the last two come only from row 3's exact cost tie), built by a cached-file test from the in-process runs, which also requires that no cell is `inline_cheaper`; and "`pooled.json` is {its size with thousands commas} bytes."
  - `## Regenerate` gains, after the first block, the line "The pooled run:" and a second `bash` block of exactly these lines: `python evidence/tau2-bench/convert.py --out tau2-calls-all.jsonl --calls --task-by all`, `python evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.py --matrix tau2-calls-all.jsonl --registry evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/registry.json --out evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/pooled.json` and `python evidence/delegation/results.py --table evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/pooled.json`
  - Checked with a private prototype on the 12 pinned files when this bullet was written (the cached-file tests pin each number): `pooled.json` is 126,695 bytes with 432 summary rows; the pooled calibration half holds 114 to 158 conversations; `expected_work_calls` is 13 to 15 and the per-call `S_in + S_out` 603 to 636, so the forecast work is at most 9,240 tokens, the 5.2 rule 5 check needs at most 9,740 and the 13.4 rule 3 check at most 73,240 at C = 64,000, each under the per-domain run's; inline is blocked (`parent_context_full`) on all 600 of the Claude parent's point 2 cells and on no other cell. Of the 3,600 pooled `cost_effective` cells, 1,826 are `none_clear`, 1,232 `delegate_cheaper`, 300 `parent_context_full` and 242 `parent_below_bar`; the 3,600 `cost_blind` cells hold the same 1,826, 300 and 242 with 787 `delegate_better` and 445 `inline_as_good`. The cost comparison is reached pooled on 550, 550, 132, 0, 0 and 0 of each bar's 600 cells (bars 0.3 to 0.8; at 0.3 and 0.4 on every cell where inline is allowed) and per domain on 892, 584, 514, 271, 40 and 0 of each bar's 1,800. At bar 0.3 and point 0 the pooled decision costs 10.0668 USD against 19.8422 per domain, with held-out success 0.548 against 0.550
- Cached point (v1.88, its RESULTS.md parts committed by v1.89): the 13.10 variant point 1, base point 2 with `cached_read_ratio` 0.1 (C = 200,000, B = 500, R = 500, T = 1, `inline_separate_call` True), applies to per-call rows as 13.9 defines it. It sets no `work_calls`, so the 13.9 v1.74 point check accepts it, and its ratio prices only the parent's reads of C in its inline work calls: in each held-out inline cost the integer `K(i, P) * C` times rho, to which Rd(i, P) is added (13.9 v1.75), and in the decision `K_dec * C * rho` (13.2 v1.41 with `work_calls` K_dec); never the work's own history, which a per-call row prices at list price on both paths (13.9 v1.74), and never the delegate path. It runs in process on both matrices with the committed registry, `BARS`, the 50 seeds and four points: base point 2, `variant_points()[1]` of `evidence/delegation/results.py` (loaded by file path, as `points()` is), and that point with the ratio at 0.25 and at 0, two sensitivities of the stated 0.1: 0.25 is the cached-input price over the input price on both OpenAI agents' model pages in `research/delegation-datasets.md` (0.50 against 2.00 and 0.10 against 0.40 USD per 1M tokens), and 0 charges no read of C at all
  - Result: at each of the three ratios the decision has the mode, model and reason of base point 2 on every cell of both runs, in both flavors, and so do the cell's `below_bar`, `delegate`, `inline_allowed` and per-strategy `correct` and its `delegate` cost; the `decision` cost is the `delegate` one where the decision delegates, as on every `cost_effective` cell, and the `inline` one where it works inline, as on some `cost_blind` cells, so only the `inline` cost and with it those `cost_blind` decision costs move. Why: only `inline_cost_usd` reads rho, and it does not fall as rho rises (each step of it multiplies or adds non-negative numbers, and a rounded multiply or add is monotone), so a cell that delegates at 0 because its delegate path is cheaper delegates at every ratio up to 1, as does a cell that delegates by a row that reads no cost; at base point 2 every `cost_effective` cell delegates, and at 0 none turns inline. In `cost_blind` no row reads cost (13.5 row 4)
  - So no results file is added: one would repeat base point 2's `cost_effective` decision rows and every `delegate` row, and move only the `inline` rows, which a Reading it bullet gives at bar 0.3, and the cost of the `cost_blind` decision rows where it works inline. `points()`, `run`, `results.json`, `pooled.json`, the README and the 12.13 workflow are unchanged by v1.88 and v1.89; v1.92 specifies two README lines on the point (12.8), its first Reading it bullet below in `### Delegation on tau2-bench` and one sentence in README Limitations
  - RESULTS.md `## Reading it` gains, after "`pooled.json` is {size} bytes.", three bullets in this order, built by a cached-file test from those in-process runs, numbers in the table formats (counts with thousands commas, cost to 4 decimals) and ratios as Python `format(x, "g")` prints them: "At the cached point of [the AutoBench delegation results](../delegation/RESULTS.md) (variant point 1: point 2 with the parent's reads of C at `cached_read_ratio` 0.1 of its list input price, an assumed ratio), the decision has the mode, model and reason it has at point 2 on all {a} cells per domain and all {b} pooled, in both flavors, and so it has with the ratio at 0.25 or 0: no cell moves from `delegate_cheaper` to `inline_cheaper`.", with a and b each run's cells at the point over both flavors; "There, of the {n} `cost_effective` cells per domain, {count} `{reason}`, ... and {count} `{reason}`; of the {m} pooled, {count} `{reason}`, ... and {count} `{reason}`; the {x} and {y} `delegate_cheaper` cells (`DESIGN.md` 13.5 row 3) stay `delegate_cheaper` with the ratio at 0, where no read of C is charged.", each run's `cost_effective` reasons at the point ordered as in the Method sentence (descending count, ties by name, ` and ` before the last); and "At bar {lowest bar} the ratio of 0.1 cuts always inline's cost on a held-out half at C = 200,000 from {list} to {cached} USD for `{parent}` and from {list} to {cached} for `{parent}`, the parents inline is allowed for there; the decision costs {d} and {d} per domain.", from the per-domain run's `inline` summary rows at base point 2 and at the point and its `decision` rows at the point, for each parent whose every cell at the point has `inline_allowed`, in `parents` order (the test requires every other parent to have it on none)
  - RESULTS.md `## Assumed` gains one bullet, just before the 13.8 limits one: "The cached point's ratio of 0.1 is an assumption, as on AutoBench, not read from these runs. Of the recorded costs, only those of the two OpenAI agents imply a cached price: their model pages price cached input at 0.25 of input (0.50 against 2.00 and 0.10 against 0.40 USD per 1M tokens), which the research note infers on 77.42% and 78.94% of their prompt tokens. Those are the agents' re-reads of their own conversations, which these rows price at list price on both paths, not a parent's reads of C; the Anthropic agent's recorded costs are list price, a ratio of 1.0. The 0.25 is a page price, not a parameter fitted to the runs." A plain test builds it from the research note: the two OpenAI rows of its price table (the input and cached-input prices as written, the ratio as an exact fraction of those decimals), its inferred-share sentence and its list-price sentence on the Anthropic agent, and requires each input price to be that agent's `price_in` in `registry.json`
  - Checked with a private prototype on the 12 pinned files when this bullet was written (the cached-file tests pin each number): at the point, per domain, the 2,700 `cost_effective` cells are 1,262 `none_clear`, 900 `parent_context_full`, 387 `delegate_cheaper` and 151 `parent_below_bar`, and pooled the 900 are 332, 300, 209 and 59; the `cost_blind` cells are per domain 1,262 `none_clear`, 900 `parent_context_full`, 325 `delegate_better`, 151 `parent_below_bar` and 62 `inline_as_good`, and pooled 332, 300, 187, 59 and 22; each the same at 0.25 and at 0. At bar 0.3, on a held-out half at C = 200,000, always inline costs 774.2023 USD at list price, 104.3215 at 0.1, 215.9683 at 0.25 and 29.8903 at 0 for `openai/gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`, and 213.0697, 29.8110, 60.3541 and 9.4489 for `openai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14`; the decision costs 19.8837 and 18.9993 per domain at every ratio. Inline is blocked (`parent_context_full`) on every cell of `anthropic/claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219` at the point, as at base point 2
- Test status: `tests/test_evidence_tau2_delegation.py` is committed ahead of the script and the files, every test that loads the script or reads one of the three committed files under `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=FileNotFoundError)`, which their absence raises; v1.79 removes the markers. Plain from the start, passing before and after: the 13.10 base points carry no `work_calls`; `evaluate_delegation` refuses the 13.10 variant points with `work_calls` above 1 on per-call rows with the 13.9 v1.74 message and accepts variant point 1; `research/delegation-datasets.md` states each price, window and status the registry bullet takes from it; and, when the 12 pinned files are cached, the `--calls` matrix keeps exactly the registry's 3 agents. The cached-file tests (skipped unless the 12 pinned files are in the default cache, so CI skips them) rebuild `results.json` in process (`extract` with `calls`, `convert`, `run`) and require the committed bytes, and rebuild every prototype number above from an in-process run that keeps the cells. v1.82 commits the 8 pooled-run tests ahead of `pooled.json` and the RESULTS.md parts, 7 of them failing until v1.83 adds those in the same push (the eighth, on the pooled matrix's rows, reads neither); its cached-file tests skip unless the 12 pinned files are cached, as the others do. v1.88 commits 4 cached-point tests ahead of the RESULTS.md parts: two plain ones (the point is variant point 1 and, on synthetic per-call rows, moves only the `inline` cost, by the hand-computed `K(i, P) * C` term; the Assumed bullet built from the research note and the registry), the second failing until v1.89, and two cached-file ones (every decision kept at the three ratios, with the prototype numbers; the three Reading it bullets), the second failing until v1.89 where the 12 pinned files are cached

## 14. Non-goals for v1 (two-way doors, deferred)
- Native vendor SDK adapters (the OpenAI-compatible adapter covers gateways), async execution, streaming
- Learned or contextual routers (per-prompt difficulty prediction); v1 routes per task
- Online drift detection beyond re-calibrating from audited results; budgets and alerts
- Price refresh automation
- In the delegation cost model (13): prompt caching beyond one cached-read ratio on the parent's reads of its context (from v1.41, 13.2), such as cache-write surcharges, cache expiry and discounts on other re-reads, and latency, including the speed-up of parallel sub-agents (multi-call work is costed from v1.38, 13.2)

- v1.37 (2026-11-05, review): the `dec_breakeven` clause says what is exact: the drop in the success the summary stores, the mean over seeds of each seed's held-out rate with every seed weighted equally, not a success pooled over every held-out question, which would weight seeds by their held-out sizes; `-` means a stored rate of 0, and any other rate is divided by. RESULTS.md says the same and that a value below zero belongs to always delegating or to the decision; the README bullet also names how many parents are below zero; the hand-made break-even table gains a parent with a partial rate whose decision success is not the delegate's and a positive margin that rounds to zero
- v1.38 (2026-11-05): multi-call work in the delegation decision (13.1 to 13.4, 13.7): `work_calls` K (an integer >= 1, not a bool, default 1) and `--work-calls N`; call j of a path reads its base context (C inline, B delegated) plus the j inputs and j - 1 outputs so far, and each path prices its K calls as one `Model.cost` of the integer totals `K * X + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out` in and `K * S_out` out, with w_in = K(K + 1) / 2 and w_out = K(K - 1) / 2; the 1 + T later parent calls re-read K inputs and outputs inline and B + R delegated; folded inline work takes only the last call into the parent's next call, so C is read K - 1 times; the inline context check is C + K * (S_in + S_out); the delegate's `select` checks each window against its last call, B + K * (S_in + S_out), and prices each candidate at its K calls. With K = 1 every cost, reason and check is the v1.37 one bit for bit. Contract tests in `tests/test_work_calls.py` under strict xfail (13.8); no code, README, evidence or results change
- v1.38 (2026-11-05, review): 13.2 labels the three original expressions and the D = P bullet after them as the K = 1 case, since from K = 2 folded inline work also re-reads C. - v1.123 (2026-11-08, review): tests only. `tests/test_packaging_skips.py` runs its child pytest with the parent's environment minus `PYTEST_ADDOPTS`, so a user's options cannot change the child's test set, and its check that the run with no block passes the two `pyproject.toml` tests names the test and its outcome, with a comment that this run needs the parser
- v1.124 (2026-11-08): spec only. 12.8: `research/where-routing-pays.md` and `research/where_routing_pays.py` read the five committed routing results files and set, per dataset, the tasks, the expected calibration halves, the best single model with the Wilson shortfall of its success at those halves, `best_on_calibration` and, per flavor and bar, the routed row and the models it picks across tasks and seeds, so a reader can see why routing costs less than the best single model on some datasets and never reaches its held-out success; the Results at a glance bullets are followed by one paragraph linking the note. - v1.125 (2026-11-08): implements v1.124 (12.8). `research/where_routing_pays.py` and `research/where-routing-pays.md` land, the note's `text` block the script's output on the five committed routing results files; README gains the paragraph after the Results at a glance bullets and `research/README.md` its row and bullet. On these files routing pays in cost at a bar below the best single model's held-out success and never in success: three files route one pooled task, `best_on_calibration` stays below the best single model on all five, on RouterBench's 65 tasks the top `cost_blind` pick takes 0.916 of the picks, and the Wilson shortfall at the expected halves runs from 0.033 at 356 items to 0.205 at 25. No change under `model_routing/`, to any results file, workflow or README transcript
- v1.126 (2026-11-08, review): 12.8. `research/where-routing-pays.md` no longer derives a seed count from the tau2-bench airline task's expected half: it points to `evidence/tau2-bench/RESULTS.md` for the per-seed halves and gives the cleared rate over the 3 tasks and 50 seeds as such; it says the best single model is chosen in hindsight on the held-out halves, and drops a sentence the Results block does not support. `research/where_routing_pays.py` reports a document that is not a JSON object, or holds a value of the wrong type, as one `error: ` line. `tests/test_research_where_routing_pays.py` checks the main claim on the files, skips code spans in the prose-number rule and covers four bad documents
- v1.127 (2026-11-08): tests only, no change to what any test checks. `tests/test_evidence_autobench_judges_results.py` reads and verifies the pinned AutoBench files, builds the default 12.11 judge matrix and runs its `evaluate` with cells once, in a module-scoped fixture, for the three tests that read the pinned files, where each of the three read the files and built that matrix and the regenerate and routed tests each ran that `evaluate`; the regenerate test drops the cells from a copy instead of popping them from the shared result. `tests/test_evidence_tau2_delegation.py` runs `evaluate_delegation` on each of the two matrices once, in a module-scoped fixture, for the work-call and window test and the tests that read `run_cells`, where the work-call test ran the per-domain run again. On the machine that measured it, with the pinned files cached and an unrelated load running, the two modules took 31.99 to 36.61 s of process CPU time before and 24.25 to 26.38 s after over three alternating runs; CI, which has no pinned files, skips these tests as before. No change under `model_routing/`, `evidence/` or `research/`, to either workflow, to a committed results file or to any README transcript
- v1.128 (2026-11-08, review): tests only. The `tests/test_evidence_autobench_judges_results.py` regenerate test also requires, after its reduce, that the shared result still holds its cells, so a test that pops them fails in any test order and not only when the routed test runs after it; v1.127 says which of the three tests ran the shared `evaluate` and gives the measured CPU times instead of a per-rerun estimate
- v1.129 (2026-11-08): spec only. 12.11: the default candidate search checks the committee-size rule once per answer, groups the answers that pass by their pool graders other than the answer model, and counts each candidate set's kept answers from those groups, after which the drop rules run once per answer for the chosen set; the chosen set, its tie rule, the rows and the report are unchanged. `tests/test_evidence_autobench_judges.py` pins that the drop rules run once per scored answer, under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)` since the v1.128 search runs them once per answer per candidate set, and adds plain tests, passing before and after, that the default choice equals trying every set on every answer on 300 seeded random matrices (ties, later winners, sets that keep nothing and a `size` above the judges among them) and that on a matrix where no set keeps an answer the first set is chosen
- v1.130 (2026-11-08): implements v1.129 (12.11). `evidence/autobench-2026/judges.py` chooses the default candidates with `_most_cells`, which turns each answer that passes the committee-size check into the bitmask of its pool graders other than the answer model, counts equal masks once (1,455 masks for the 4,933 scored answers of the pinned files at the defaults) and sums for each candidate set the counts of the masks that hold it; the v1.129 call-count test becomes a plain test. On the pinned files, on one machine at a load average under 4, one default `judge_matrix` call took 2.737 to 2.900 s before and 0.113 to 0.121 s after over three alternating runs, and its rows and report are the same before and after (SHA-256 of the sorted JSON) at the defaults and with `size` 4 or 6, `tolerance` 1.0, `answer_tokens` `output`, `min_committee` 5 or `task_by` `domain`; the committed judge results regenerate byte-identical. No change under `model_routing/`, to a committed results file, either workflow or any README transcript
- v1.131 (2026-11-08, review): 12.11 and `evidence/autobench-2026/judges.py`. The committee-size check is one helper, `_small_committee`, that the default search and the drop rules both call, so the two cannot drift apart; the v1.129 sentence says the grouping reads each answer once and only the count for each set grows with the number of distinct groups. No change to the chosen set, the rows or the report
- v1.132 (2026-11-08): spec only. 13.9: `evaluate_delegation` computes each held-out path's `correct` and `cost_usd` once per (task, seed, point, parent, path, model) and shares the pair across bars and flavors, since neither enters a held-out sum; every cell, the summary and the result are unchanged bit for bit. `tests/test_delegation_eval.py` pins that a run's cells make one `math.fsum` per distinct key, under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)` since the v1.131 code makes three per cell, and adds a plain test, passing before and after, that a run on three bars and both flavors gives, cell for cell, the cells of the six runs on one bar and one flavor each
- v1.133 (2026-11-08): implements v1.132 (13.9). `evaluate_delegation` keeps a run's held-out pairs in one dict keyed by (task, seed, point, parent, path, model), which `_cell` fills the first time a cell needs a key; the v1.132 call-count test becomes a plain test. On one machine the three runs of `research/no_path_clears.py` on the cached pinned files took 9.37 to 9.88 s of process CPU time before and 4.19 to 4.36 s after, over three alternating runs, with the same output (SHA-256 equal), and the regenerate tests of the four committed delegation results files pass byte for byte. No change to any cell, summary, results file or README transcript
- v1.134 (2026-11-08, review): tests and a comment only. The v1.132 call-count test checks that the 3-bar, 2-flavor run has 6 cells and at most 4 keys per (task, seed, point, parent) instead of a bound that always held, and runs `evaluate_delegation` a second time to show it again makes one held-out sum per key, so no pair is kept from one call to the next; a comment in `_cell` names what each part of the key fixes. No change to any cell, summary, results file or README transcript
- v1.135 (2026-11-08): spec only. 12.3: `evaluate` computes each (task, seed)'s calibration counts and mean costs, each model's held-out correct count and cost, and the `oracle`'s held-out pair once and shares them across bars and flavors, since neither enters those sums; every cell, summary row and per-task row is unchanged bit for bit. `tests/test_evaluate.py` pins that, with the summary and per-task rows stubbed out, a run makes 2M `sum` and 2M + 1 `math.fsum` calls in the module per (task, seed), on a second run too, under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)` since the v1.134 code makes 2M + 3 and 2M + 4 per cell, and adds a plain test, passing before and after, that a run on three bars and both flavors gives, cell for cell, the cells of the six runs on one bar and one flavor each, with the routed model moving with the bar and no `models`, `success` or `cost_usd` dict shared by two cells
